# Agentmaster

> A fork of **Windows Terminal** (`microsoft/terminal`, MIT) that turns it into a manager
> for multiple **Claude Code** sessions.

## Development Rules

- Do not build or deploy or install without the user's permission.
- Every build writes to `Q:`, never to `K:`: a layout goes to a `Q:\agentmaster-layouts\<slot>` through `-LayoutDir`, and the build trees (`bin`, `obj`, the package's `bin` / `obj` / `AppPackages`) are junctions to Q: (*Building FAST* #1).
- Do commit changes once done — a single `git add` + `git commit` with an extensive
  message (see *Commit Instructions* in the global guidance). Committing needs no
  permission; only build/deploy/install do.

## What we're doing & our aim

- **Aim:** one Windows Terminal–based app that manages **N Claude Code sessions across M working directories** — letting you **fully interact** with each session *and* giving the app **100% programmatic control** (inject prompts, read output, drive state).
- Each session is a real `claude.exe` on a **ConPTY** connection: a full-fidelity terminal with a shared stdin (you and the orchestrator coexist), output tapped for the UI, and semantic state taken from **Claude Code hooks** — never screen-scraping.

### Converged design:

#### Design A — Native Graft:

- the management "brain" lives *inside* the fork (C++/WinRT).

#### Agent Manager page (C1 "Linked Lenses") — behind the tab strip's Home button:

- **Not a tab.** The Agent Manager used to be a pinned, leftmost, non-closable tab 0; it is now a per-window **page** that the tab strip's **Home** button (the `TabStripHeader` glyph left of the `<` scroll arrow) opens — the **only** user-facing way in. Home is always shown and paints as the strip's active entry (accent tint) while the page is up.
- **On the page, Home TURNS INTO the jump-back arrow** (2026-10-12, NOT BUILT yet; the user's request: "I want the home icon to turn into return icon instead and have you jump back"). While a session that THIS window hosts as a live tab is selected in the Manager (usually the tab you came from — the Linked-Lenses sync selects it), the button shows ↶ (`E7A7`, the Undo glyph) and a click returns to that session's tab; with nothing to go back to it keeps the Home glyph and a click does nothing.
  - **Before:** a separate **Jump Back** button appeared BESIDE Home on the page (since the page replaced the pinned tab on 2026-09-25). Before that, Home hid on the Manager tab and Jump Back took its spot.
  - **One face at a time** (`_ApplyManagerHomeButtonFace`, change-gated on the tooltip text): the glyph, the tooltip (`AgentSetTip` — it names the session by its tab title; it keeps the open delay the old XAML tooltip had, 2 × the system hover time, `_ManagerHomeButtonTipDelay`) and the accessible name flip together. `_ManagerJumpBackSessionId` decides; the click (`_OnManagerHomeButtonClick`) reads the same live state, never the face. A hover re-runs the update, so a rename while the page is up never leaves a stale title in the tooltip. ⚠ The FontIcon must stay the button's direct Content, and the button must get no XAML tooltip (`TabRowControl.xaml`).
  - **Double-click guard:** a click within the system double-click time of a click that opened the page or jumped back is ignored (`[nav] home-button click ignored …`), so a double-click never opens the page and leaves it at once.
- Leave the page by clicking any tab, the jump-back arrow, a tab-switching key (`nextTab` → first tab, `prevTab` → last tab — the cycle never lands back on the page), or anything that opens a tab.
  - **Except a launch made ON the page** (MGR-K1's fix, 2026-10-23, NOT BUILT yet; the user's "make selected but do not go to the tab as if user clicked it"; flows/01 MGR-07): the launch row's Launch / Resume / Fork, a board / tree / External row menu's Open New Session Here / Fork session / Adopt / a Codex launch, and the side panel's "+" card while the page shows all run through `_LaunchFromManagerPage`. The new tab opens in the BACKGROUND (`_openClaudeTabInBackground`), and one Low-priority tick later `_FinishManagerPageLaunch` starts its claude in place (`_ActivateDormantSession`) and selects the session on the page (`SelectSession`: card, tree row, right pane, the tab's pill, the side panel's card, Home's `Jump back to <title>`); logged `[nav] manager-page launch <sid8> (<origin>): started in place, selected on the page`. A Resume of a session that is already OPEN still jumps to its tab; a fork decides when it RUNS (after its question). Before, a page launch switched to the new tab, and the page never selected it, because `_InitializeTab` selected the tab before `_claudeTabs` mapped it (the funnel's `_SyncManagerSelectionToTab` found no session). A launch that does select its new tab (from a tab's menu, the "+" card over a tab, a Sessions-page resume) now runs that sync once the tab is mapped (`_NoteLaunchedSessionTab`, called at the end of `_LaunchClaudeSession` / `_LaunchCodexSession`).
- The page is split into three selection-synced regions:
- **Triage Board** (top) — sessions as cards in five state columns (Running · Waiting-for-you · Needs-approval · Error · Idle/Done), plus an observe-only **External** census column.
- **Explorer Tree** (bottom-left) — the M working directories → their N sessions.
- **Prompt Queue** (bottom-right, a tab beside the read-only **Summary** tab) — a per-session prompt queue + the **Prompt Queue Runner**.
- **Mechanics** (`TerminalPage.AgentEngine.cpp`): `_CreateAgentManagerPage` builds the ONE `AgentManagerContent` per window at first layout (owned by `_managerPage` for the window's life, never in `_tabs` — so no tab loop / drag / close / split / rename can reach it; its lens starts HIDDEN). `_ShowManagerPage(reason)` swaps its root into the SAME `_tabContent` host the tab used (layout + side panel unchanged), **deselects the tab strip** (no tab reads as focused, so every tab-targeting action no-ops), re-opens a Sessions page left open over it, and runs `_ApplyAgentTabSelection(nullptr)` (lens on screen + catch-up rebuild, pill, bring-into-view). `_managerPageVisible` is the ONE authority — `_GetFocusedTabIndex` answers `nullopt` while it is set even if MUX re-selected a tab under the page (MUX auto-selects a neighbor when an item leaves a selection-less strip; `_RemoveTab` re-clears it). A tab selection leaves the page through the funnel (`_OnTabSelectionChanged` → `_HideManagerPage` → `_UpdatedSelectedTab`); the one case the funnel can't see — selecting the tab MUX already holds selected — goes through `_LeaveManagerPageOntoSelectedTab` (`_SelectTab`, `_FocusClaudeSessionTab`, a header press, `OpenSettingsUI`).
- **Automatic shows** (the strip has nothing else to show): the **last tab closed** (the window stays open on the page; the Manager-only check then self-closes it unless it is the last window), a close whose **every remaining tab is dormant** (FAVORITES.md §4a — showing a dormant tab would start its claude.exe), a **Deactivate** of the focused tab, a zero-tab window at startup, and a window **reopened while it was on the Manager** (its record names no focused tab; with async shell replays the batch shows the page after its last tab via the `_showManagerPageAfterStartupBatch` latch). The page's window title is the content's "Agent Manager" / "Agent Manager Dev". An `agentManager` pane request (`_MakePane`) is refused — there is never a second Manager.

#### Prompt Queue / Prompt Queue Runner ([`DELIVERY.md`](doc/agentmaster/DELIVERY.md) §17)

- queue prompts; on **turn-complete** (`Stop` hook) the next prompt is auto-sent.
- Approvals and clarifying-questions are handled separately.
- **Two modes per session:** **Paused** (nothing is sent by itself) and **Auto** (the next queued prompt goes out when the session is ready). The stored words, the logs and the CLI say `Off` / `Full` (`AutorunnerModeLabel`); the UI says Paused / Auto (`AutorunnerModeDisplayName`). The SemiAuto mode and its Send / Skip confirm were removed on 2026-10-09 (NOT BUILT yet): a stored `SemiAuto` loads as `Off`.
- **The names (2026-10-09, NOT BUILT yet):** "Prompt Queue Runner" was "Tests Autorunner", and the "Prompt Queue" pane was "Auto Testing". Only the visible text changed. Every identifier (`AutorunnerMode`, `_autoTestBody`, `autoTestingShowsSummary`), every persisted key (`autorunner`, `defaultAutorunnerMode`), `autorunner.log`, every log tag and the CLI output keep the old words, and so do most code comments: read "autorunner" as the runner and "Auto Testing" as the Prompt Queue. (Before that: "Flight Plan" / "Autopilot"; the persisted `autopilot`/`flightPlanShowsSummary`/`defaultAutopilotMode` keys still read back for back-compat.)

#### The Prompt Queue Runner runs in EVERY build — Debug Mode gates nothing today

- **Since 2026-10-09 (NOT BUILT yet)** the runner and all of its UI are in the release package, the dev package and an unpackaged build alike. `Engine.cpp` starts the scheduler with both of its jobs (the auto-advance and the pickup watch) and logs `[engine] prompt queue runner started (auto-advance + pickup watch, every build); default mode for new sessions: <Off|Full>`.
  - **Before:** the feature was gated on `Profiles::IsDevOrDebugPackage()`. An ordinary release ran no auto-advance and showed none of the UI: no `[Summary | Auto Testing]` toggle, no toolbar Pause button, no Settings tab, no ⚙ queue badge, and no mode button, `⏳N` count, `settling` cue or mail button on the per-tab overlay.
  - **What changes for an install that ran without Debug Mode:** its launched sessions already carry the cog's default mode (Auto unless changed), so the runner is on for them at once. A queue there is normally empty. A prompt that stayed `Pending` (a /handover briefing that was never delivered, a queue left by a Debug Mode run) is sent after the session is open, started and settled.
  - **The Settings tab that was hidden holds four switches for every send path** (draft swap, Ctrl+S stash, verified placement, restore re-fill). An ordinary release could not change them before.
- **Debug Mode is KEPT, and no feature reads it** (the user's decision: later developer-only features will). The gate is `Profiles::IsDevOrDebugPackage()`: the `AgentmasterDev` package, or a release with the `--debug` launch flag / the `AGENTMASTER_DEBUG` env var / the Settings cog's **About → "Enable Debug Mode"** toggle (`AppSettings::debugMode`, applied at the NEXT start). Gate a debug-only feature on that predicate.
- ⚠ The persisted toggle's startup apply (`ApplyPersistedDebugMode`, run in the EXE prelude) must propagate **process-wide**: the `DebugForced` latch is MODULE-local (one private copy per linked binary — an inline header function's static never crosses the DLL boundary), so the apply also exports **`AGENTMASTER_DEBUG=1`** into the process env block (the `AGENTMASTER_PROFILE` idiom) for `TerminalApp.dll`'s gates, and `Engine.cpp` re-applies as a belt. Without that, the toggle enabled debug in the EXE only ("did not get enabled across the entire app").

#### Per-tab link badge (overlay) — built ([`TAB_OVERLAY.md`](doc/agentmaster/TAB_OVERLAY.md)):

- **every terminal tab the Fleet Observer classifies** carries a small **top-right terminal HUD** that makes the tab ⇄ Agentmaster link legible *while you work inside the session*.
- A **linked** Claude session shows the full badge — status (color-matched to the Triage Board) + the Fleet Observer's `model · effort · kind`, Prompt Queue Runner mode (**Paused / Auto**; it reads `<mode> (Off globally)` while the Manager toolbar's global Pause is on), queued count, and link state **⛓ linked**, plus a dim **second row** `<workdir folder>/<branch>` and a dim **third row** `⏳ <next queued prompt>` (the first `Pending` prompt's first line, ≤300 chars + `...`; shown only when something is queued, mode-agnostic).
- Any **other** tab shows a dim **observe badge** `○ <kind> · unlinked` (kind = `pwsh` / `cmd` / `claude` *started-but-not-yet-prompted* (§11d) / `codex`) that **flips in place** as the tab's activity changes — a `pwsh` tab → `claude` the moment you run it → the full linked badge on its first prompt; cleared when claude exits or the tab closes — so a started-but-unprompted claude (no transcript id yet) is never invisible.
- Dim until hover; hover/click **expands** controls (the Prompt Queue Runner toggle, Paused ↔ Auto · Send-now · queue peek · Jump-to-Manager). (The contextual SemiAuto confirm went away with that mode on 2026-10-09.)
- Off-switchable (`AppSettings.showTabOverlay`).
- Hover also reveals a **row of actions** — a folder button (Open Path) + a **mail** button (**queue the UNSENT input-box draft** into this session's prompt queue — the Manager compose row's "Add to queue" envelope, on the badge: same `\xE715` glyph, same registry append, the draft resolved by the same `PickCurrentPromptText` live-else-remembered rule; a **plain click MOVES it** — the draft is then **cleared out of the input box** via the verified **DISCARD ladder** (`_ClearLiveDraftForSession`: lock → `DecideDraftDiscard` — per-round `End`+`Ctrl+U`, then `End`+backspaces, probe-measured true discards + mid-turn safe — → unlock-FIRST-then-bookkeep, no send/restore, shares the `_draftSwapsInFlight` box-mutex + the gate's clear tag, `SetPendingInput("")` on a verified clear; ⚠ deliberately NOT the swap's `DecideDraftClear`, whose Ctrl+S rung is a STASH claude auto-restores at the next submit — a stash-and-walk-away re-planted the moved draft right after its own queued copy delivered) — while **Shift+Click KEEPS it** in the box (the historical copy, Rule #13, via `IsShiftDown()`); the button is **enabled only while a draft exists** (`_RefreshQueueButtonEnabled`, tracking the "3 dots"/`pendingInput` flip, so no silent dead-click on an empty box); shown only for **Claude**, in every build since 2026-10-09 (it was dev-or-debug only before); explicit-click so no one-shot latch — TAB_OVERLAY.md §13j / PENDING_INPUT.md §8d) + a copy menu (Session Id · working dir · branch · the **Current Prompt** [the UNSENT input-box draft — PENDING_INPUT.md §8] · the REAL Claude/Codex launch CLI · the full session **Summary** · a **Transcript ▸ Default / Max / Full / Compact / Minimal** submenu [the conversation as Markdown, turn by turn — `AgentMaster/TranscriptTurns.{h,cpp}`, a C++ port of the am-agentmaster-cli skill's `transcript_turns.py` (v9) pinned byte for byte to goldens the reference script writes (`tests/turns/`, `TestTranscriptTurns`), so the app and the skill copy the same text: Minimal = your messages + each final reply · Compact = + compactions, your answers, slash commands, each subagent's brief + report · Default = + every tool call on one line (how it ended) and the remarks between calls · Full = + each Bash command, the first output line, per-call durations · Max = + every Write's content and a stats line per turn; the sessions it was forked / plan-restarted from and the subagents it spawned are rendered too; codes 9-13 from the ONE shared `kTranscriptCopyMenu` table (`AgentCopyActions.h`); a Codex rollout falls back to the plain-text readers (Minimal/Compact = the per-turn `ReadConversationFollowupText` brief, Default/Full/Max = `ReadConversationText`); it replaced the flat **Transcript** and **Transcript Followup** items; its FIRST item, **Show** (then a separator), opens the transcript in the window-level reading view instead of copying it — [`TRANSCRIPT_OVERLAY.md`](doc/agentmaster/TRANSCRIPT_OVERLAY.md)]) + a **pencil** that toggles a **SUMMARY PANEL**: a second overlay below the badge (≤20% pane width) rendering the `session-end.js` box (messages/files/tasks/plan) analyzed from the transcript, its show/hide a GLOBAL setting (`AppSettings.showSummaryPanel`); a **wrap-line toggle** (↵) at the right of its times bar flips a message's newlines between a literal `\n` and real multi-line (`AppSettings.summaryPanelWrapNewlines`, also GLOBAL); its Messages list is the session's **WHOLE conversation** — every earlier session it continues (a fork's parent, a plan-restart parent, a `/clear` predecessor, a `/handover` origin; upstream only, never a session that continued it) numbered 1..N straight through as ONE conversation, a thin **session separator** naming the id's first 8 characters after each earlier session's part — led by the word **fork** when the session below is a fork of it (`─ fork 358db618 ─────`, 2026-10-12, NOT BUILT yet; a plan-restart / `/clear` / `/handover` link keeps the bare id; one rule, `SummarySeparatorLabel`, for the panel and the copied Summary's `──── fork <full id> ────`) — (click = copy the full id + the tooltip "Copied session id of up to here"), the `⏮` toggle (`AppSettings.summaryPanelShowHistory`, GLOBAL, default ON) — TAB_OVERLAY.md §13e.
- The per-tab *here-and-now* lens, complementing the Manager's *fleet* view.

- The session database (DuckDB; IMPLEMENTED 2026-10-15, NOT BUILT into the app, Off by default): every Claude Code session on the machine in one per-profile DuckDB file, served by `agentmaster-sessiondb.exe` over a named pipe to the Sessions page, the AI search, the CLI (`search` / `sql` / `sessdb`) and a stdio MCP server `agentmaster`; refreshed by the app's watcher (D16); the design, the decisions D1–D16, §16 = what was built, how it differs, the full-corpus measurements, and §17 = the review against the user's requirements of 2026-10-16 (DuckDB the engine of BOTH the agent and the Sessions page's search, the agent calls that code as a tool with no separate program or MCP server, a build from scratch in < 30 s that then only adds the difference — the build of §16 meets none but the last half): [`doc/agentmaster/SESSIONS_DB.md`](doc/agentmaster/SESSIONS_DB.md).
- The session database's DESIGN (2026-10-12; implemented 2026-10-15 — the bullet above, and SESSIONS_DB.md §16 says where the build differs and what each decision became); D14 and D16 decided by the user 2026-10-14: one DuckDB database per profile holding every Claude Code session on the machine (every transcript line verbatim, so a transcript rebuilds byte for byte — §5.5; the block text; our own inverted index in DuckDB tables; a hot tier for new text), owned by a per-profile service process that serves the Sessions page, the AI search, the CLI and an MCP server over a named pipe; a virtualized Sessions table. Refreshed by the app's observer (§7.6, D16 decided): the app's `TranscriptWatcher` counts each session's changes, and every refresh tick (default 5 s, a setting) the sessions that changed are upserted (`upsertSessions`; `resync` after a connect or a re-index); a tick without a change does nothing; never rebuilt for a search — measured ~190 MB written a day, a tick ~17–27 ms, the busiest hour 0.18–0.34% of one core. The session row (§5.6): ~120 columns from a census of what Claude Code writes — normalized place keys by `NormDirKey` (cwd first / last, inferred dir, git root, color key), Claude Code's own cost record (`cost-state`), usage, outcomes, lineage. The database can form the session summary (§8.5, D14 decided) — a precomputed row in ~3 ms, equal to `AnalyzeSessionTranscript` on 921 of 921 sessions — for the AI search's tools, the MCP `session` tool and the CLI only; the app's summary surfaces keep `AnalyzeSessionTranscript`; what DuckDB buys and what it does not (§3.5); the other parts that would read it (§8.6). Indexes (§6.8): large tables kept sorted by their read key (zonemaps), ART only for batched random id lookups. The eleven tools' full contracts and the checklist for adding one (§8.4). Measured on the real corpus with a prototype (DuckDB 2.0 alpha): content search 2–33 ms against today's 2–42 s; why not DuckDB's own `fts` extension (§6.5): [`doc/agentmaster/SESSIONS_DB.md`](doc/agentmaster/SESSIONS_DB.md); the benchmark scripts: `tools/sessiondb-bench/`.
- The agent harness (2026-10-10; in the v0.7.9 pre-release of 2026-10-11, not installed on this machine; the permission policy of 2026-10-12 NOT BUILT): Claude conversations that Agentmaster hosts itself — one windowless `claude.exe` per conversation, driven through the pinned `refs/claude-agent-sdk-cpp` submodule (stream-json both ways, in-process MCP tool servers), `--no-session-persistence`, Agentmaster's own record + digest for persistence, auto-compaction at 500K, park / reset; how the SDK is compiled (one unity file + our own Windows process backend), the launch policy (§5.1: bypass permissions always, auto mode when Claude Code does not allow bypass, `dontAsk` when it allows neither — the mode is read from the process itself), the process's own inventory (tools, servers, commands, skills, agents, plugins), the `AM_HARNESS` mark that keeps the child out of the fleet views, the live test, and what to check after a move of the pin: [`doc/agentmaster/SDK_HARNESS.md`](doc/agentmaster/SDK_HARNESS.md).
- The session agent — a redesign of the AI search's tools (2026-10-13, DESIGN ONLY, nothing built): `search` with up to 6 alternative phrasings (merged by reciprocal rank), scopes that split shell commands, file edits, other tool calls and tool output, and folder / session exclusion; `grep`; `peek` (up to 8 sessions: the summary facts + one line per turn); `read` (one session at the Copy ▸ Transcript levels, outline → minimal → compact → default → full → max → raw, paged by turns, addressed by refs `<id>:<line>`); `files`, `related`, `timeline`, `usage`, `sql`; `show` with a reason and an evidence ref per row; `reveal`. Around them: a context line on every request (what is on the screen), an instant search of the user's words, an `elapsed` clock, secret redaction of every result, and an evaluation set. Phase A runs on today's code, phase B on the session database. Measured: the transcript size per level (26 sessions), the first real failure replayed (52.6 s, a session from the excluded project), the prompt cost (1,389 → 6,786 tokens); the tool list as the model reads it is `SESSIONS_AGENT.tools.json`: [`doc/agentmaster/SESSIONS_AGENT.md`](doc/agentmaster/SESSIONS_AGENT.md).
- Observer-owned session state (the PULL state engine — design, pre-implementation): [`doc/agentmaster/STATE.md`](doc/agentmaster/STATE.md).
- Restart session — the redesign PLAN, not implemented (the intent "Ctrl+C, then relaunch the same session id": find the session's own pane, stop the old agent and WAIT for it to exit, reset the record, choose resume / re-fork / fresh after the stop, then start; today's 13 defects with their evidence — incl. the stale-heartbeat re-promotion to Running proven in the release scanner.log, and the restart erasing the unsent draft; D3 DECIDED 2026-10-06 — the unsent draft comes back in the new box, each `[Pasted text #N +M lines]` restored from the paste journal through the fork carry's piecewise fill, §3.13, incl. why the inline renderer's dead input box must be erased first; D1, D2, D4–D7 open): [`doc/agentmaster/RESTART.md`](doc/agentmaster/RESTART.md).

e667b103b + 00945aa56 + the empty-initial-list change 97e7e1d54 + the NanoGPT / RunPod providers of 2026-10-09 NOT BUILT) (the file keeps its proposal name, HARNESSES.md: named presets, NONE by default (the user's rule, 2026-10-06: the list starts EMPTY; while it is empty every launch menu shows "Add presets…" in the presets' place — an OK dialog naming the way to Settings ▸ Presets ▸ TEMPLATES, where one Add per tested template, or "Add all tested templates", adds a preset LINKED to its template — the five OpenRouter ones are the user's launcher profiles with their values unchanged + effort max; the Xiaomi pair (R3), the NanoGPT templates — the NORMAL models, not the abliterated copies the user's evaluation ran, and DeepSeek V4.1 Flash on its `:thinking` id at effort max with DeepSeek's benchmark sampling (R8) — and the RunPod one (R9) say "not tested yet" and are added only by their own Add; NanoGPT runs on its direct API host (`https://api.nano-gpt.com/api`, `NANOGPT_API_KEY`, no routing block); RunPod has no default URL (the user's own endpoint: a load-balancing Serverless one, `https://<ENDPOINT_ID>.api.runpod.ai` with `RUNPOD_API_KEY`, or a Pod's proxy URL with vLLM's own key) and refuses a preset that names no model, since a vLLM endpoint serves exactly one; a launch-presets.json written by 0.6.8.18, which added the templates by itself, loses the presets it added that nobody changed) — listed after a separator in every "Open New Session Here" / "Fork session" menu and the Launch button's hover menu as an `@<id>` launch target; a preset's effort rides the settings file as `CLAUDE_CODE_EFFORT_LEVEL` too, because Claude Code obeys that variable over `--effort` and the user's settings.json sets it; the /handover hint picks a preset only by a BRACKETED prefix of its name, never by a bare word; every field resolves box > Agentmaster env > Windows env > template / provider default, an empty box showing the inherited value as a gray placeholder; the session's FLATTENED `launchArgs` snapshot in the session store, reused by resume / restart / fork / handover / /clear; a content-addressed, atomic `--settings` file carrying the hooks AND the preset env, because claude's own settings files override the process env; the key in Windows Credential Manager, only as `ANTHROPIC_AUTH_TOKEN`, scoped to the claude call, since `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` opens an unanswerable "use this API key?" modal; a preset that cannot launch is refused, never downgraded to the Claude login; checks R1–R9 still open — R1 = continuing a MiMo conversation on Anthropic models, R3 = Xiaomi's auth header, R8 = a NanoGPT run, R9 = a RunPod endpoint): [`doc/agentmaster/HARNESSES.md`](doc/agentmaster/HARNESSES.md).
- Commandline introspection (the `agentmaster <verb>` CLI): [`doc/agentmaster/CLI.md`](doc/agentmaster/CLI.md).
- Summary-panel JUMP (transcript→buffer resolve + center the view on a prompt): [`doc/agentmaster/SUMMARY_JUMP.md`](doc/agentmaster/SUMMARY_JUMP.md).
- The transcript overlay ("Show Transcript" below every session menu's Copy ▸ + "Copy ▸ Transcript ▸ Show": the conversation rendered as Markdown in a 90% × 80% reading view over the window, a tab per level, opened at the level last picked in any window; each turn's tool calls folded into one summary row; a ↵ line-wrap toggle in the header — ON by default, OFF cuts long lines off at the right edge — remembered for every window; every "session N" in a chained transcript's Session chain list is a link that scrolls that session's heading to the top; a fork with no messages yet shows the transcript it was forked from, under a one-line note, and its summaries and copies read that transcript too (§3e, `ResolveClaudeDisplayTranscript`); closed by Esc / a click outside / any tab switch, move or drag): [`doc/agentmaster/TRANSCRIPT_OVERLAY.md`](doc/agentmaster/TRANSCRIPT_OVERLAY.md).
- Favorite + Close refactor (Archive removed; Sessions is the sole history view): [`doc/agentmaster/FAVORITES.md`](doc/agentmaster/FAVORITES.md).
- Pending-input monitor (detect an UNSENT draft in a Claude tab's input box): [`doc/agentmaster/PENDING_INPUT.md`](doc/agentmaster/PENDING_INPUT.md).
- System notifications (Windows toasts when a session leaves Running; click = foreground + jump to tab) + the taskbar unread count (§8 — a red number on the taskbar button) + the per-window preview frame (§9 — a frame on the taskbar / Alt+Tab preview of each window holding an unread tab): [`doc/agentmaster/NOTIFICATIONS.md`](doc/agentmaster/NOTIFICATIONS.md).
- Messaging — the fleet in Telegram, Microsoft Teams and any other messenger (2026-10-15, revised twice 2026-10-16; **Telegram v1 IMPLEMENTED 2026-10-16** — §21 lists the code, the deviations and the live checks; **LIVE on this machine's DEV install since 2026-10-16 18:50 with the owner's own bot** (§21.4: 47 topics at the first connect, no API error; four first-run fixes — the bot's own service messages are no longer logged as a foreign sender; the state file is keyed by the bot id with an identity gate before the first `getUpdates`, so replacing the token with another bot's can never leave it deaf; the card shows CLOCK times, never relative spans, every call keeps the ~1 s per-chat pace and the work-log gap grows with the open turns, because 47 cards re-editing `3m`→`4m` every minute earned a 429 with a 21-minute `retry_after`, read from now on in the 10-minute `[msg] traffic` line; and a START-UP HOLD on "closed" topic deletes that lasts until every open window has re-homed its tabs (`Engine::WindowsStillRestoring`, 120 s at least, 30 min at most), because at an app start every session reads `live=false` until its window re-homes it — without it the first tick after a swap deleted all 47 topics and re-created 38, and a fixed 120 s hold still churned the 16 tabs a 140 s first layout re-homed last; **§21.5, the user's first look from the phone:** the card is STICKY — deleted and re-sent as the topic's last message once newer posts settle, no pin — with a RECAP block (the last prompt + reply, seeded from the transcript tail, shown only while nothing newer is posted); an assistant remark before a tool call is its own message and the work log rolls under it; `▶ Reactivate` on the card / `/reactivate` resumes a parked tab or restarts a session in Error, a remote prompt is delivered into Error, and an Error is announced once per episode; SECRETARY MODE — the Telegram Business connection (`business_connection` update, `_sendAsUser`) is PROBED once with a send as the user into the Search topic, and prompts typed at the PC appear as the user's own messages only when Telegram allowed it; **§21.6, the user's manual QA (dev 0.0.1.16, 2026-10-16 night):** topics are PER TAB — a new tab or fork is listed at once, before its first message (`QualifiesForTopic` reads the creation-stamped last activity; a fork's copied `forkedFrom` lines are skipped), and a topic FOLLOWS its tab to the next conversation (`Rekey`: a /clear or /resume in the tab by `tabToken`, a /handover-here successor by `continuedFromId`); the recap is Claude Code's own `away_summary`, shown only while newer than every message (§21.5's block only repeated the reply); a Bash line leads with its `description`; exit / Error / NeedsApproval re-send the card WITH SOUND instead of a bot line (one sound per "needs you"); `cardTextHash` stops re-edits of an unchanged card; Reactivate also restarts an exited claude; a phone message settles 5 s, not 40 s (`kRemotePromptSettleMs`); a secretary echo guard (no loop); two copy-write-back races of the tick fixed; **§21.8 (dev 0.0.1.17):** the topic list follows the side panel's MOST RECENT order — Telegram lists topics by their last message, so topics are CREATED (and a tick's posts made) lowest-ranked first, by the ONE key `MostRecentSortKeyMs` that `MakeSortKey` now calls too; the topics of older builds were RECREATED once (`kTopicLayoutVersion`, the persisted `orphanThreads` deleted first, then the list built oldest first; live: 48 + 48 in ~2 min, in order, no 429), and `/recreate` (asks first) does it again; the direct topic calls take the outbox's ~1 call/s slot; mid-turn remarks / work logs and the card's pings still move a topic up — Telegram needs a new message for those; **§21.9 (2026-10-16 night; LIVE on dev 0.0.1.20, in production since 0.6.8.21, in no GitHub release):** every topic carries its session's WHOLE HISTORY (the user's "a thread is a reflection of the entire session"; D26 decided: every turn as messages) — the transcript up to the live mirror's start replayed oldest first, a fork's copied lines included: per turn a day line, the user's prompt (sent AS THE USER through secretary mode where it works, else the bot's `⌨ HH:MM` line), the narrative packed (remarks + folded tool lines, ≤ 3,500 chars a message) and the final reply as its own message (`RenderHistory`; 1,175 messages for 49 sessions instead of 6,581); its own lowest send priority, one message per 2 s after every live one, one batch at a time committed when answered; the most recent sessions first, the card re-posted once below the history, then the list RE-SORTED in MOST RECENT order (`PlanTopicResort`; also when it drifts, if quiet); `kTopicLayoutVersion` 2 recreates the topics once with history; secretary mode is not connected on this machine (Telegram Business → Chatbots, Premium); the user's ANSWERS to questions (`toolUseResult.answers`) are user posts too, history and live; Telegram's flood budget for the chat is near the GROUP limit of 20 messages a minute — ~27 a minute earned a 429 after ~11 min — so a history message goes only while the last 60 s hold < 18 calls, pauses 5 min after a 429, each TURN commits when answered, and the re-sort re-posts one card per tick; live: 48 topics, 1,600 messages, 1 h 40 min, the list then exactly in MOST RECENT order; **§21.11 (2026-10-17, dev 0.0.1.21, in production since 0.6.8.21, in no GitHub release):** Claude's Markdown goes as Telegram RICH messages (Bot API 10.1 `sendRichMessage`: tables, headings and code render — `TgPrepareRichMarkdown` escapes `<` outside code, which Telegram would drop with its text, and adds the hard breaks a terminal keeps; a refused part goes as HTML IN ITS PLACE, by the outbox's new `(priority, seq, subSeq)` order; the cog's rich switch, default on); every topic is VERBOSE or THE CONVERSATION ONLY (user → Claude → user → Claude), `AppSettings::messagingVerbose` the default for all (OFF) and `TopicRecord::verbose` a topic's own (`/verbose`; `/recreate` remakes one topic or all in a mode, all = the new default); every topic NAME starts with its state glyph like the tab's dot (🔵 🟡 🟠 🔴 🟢 ⚪; one paced rename per tick within the flood budget, Telegram's "topic renamed" notice deleted) — since §21.22 (2026-10-18, NOT BUILT) the state is the topic's ICON instead (⚡️ 💬 ❓ ‼️ 🏁 ☕️ — Telegram's allowed topic icons hold no circles) and the name is the color square and the title, because Telegram draws an icon-less topic with the name's first character inside and the glyph showed twice; the glyph is the fallback for a run in which Telegram refuses an icon; Telegram documents a connected business bot sending only to the user's chats with OTHER bots, so an optional second SECRETARY bot (its token in Credential Manager, Secretary Mode + Bot-to-Bot Communication in @BotFather, connected under Telegram → Settings → Chat Automation) posts the user's turns as the user, live and in a recreated history; ⚠ a change to `SessionModels.h` needs the build-install skill's §5.8 purge — the incremental build of this work crashed dev in `_SidePanelSelectSession` on a stale `TerminalPage` layout; **§21.12 (2026-10-17, dev 0.0.1.22):** every line the bot posts in the user's place reads `<b>👤 You</b> · 14:02 · typed at the PC` (or `📱 from the phone`, `⚙ runner`, `mid-turn`, `↩ answer`, `command`) over the words (`BuildPromptLineHtml` & co.); the turn's final message REPLIES to its prompt (`reply_parameters` from a shared `PromptRef` the prompt send's answer fills and the reply's `OutItem::beforeSend` reads — no extra call; prompt lines now go at the Final priority so a fast turn's reply never overtakes them); only the LAST prompt carries the turn's STATUS (⏳ running · 🟠 needs you · ✅ answered · 🔴 error · ⏹ ended · ■ interrupted: an edit of our line, or a REACTION 👨‍💻 🤔 👌 💔 😴 🙈 on the user's own message), removed when the next prompt arrives (`TopicRecord::prompt*`, persisted); **one bot per install since 2026-10-17:** DEV polls the new `@ClaudeSessionsDebugGateBot` (8986177119, Credential Manager `…:.agentmaster-dev`); `@ClaudeSessionsGateBot` moved to the RELEASE profile (token in `Agentmaster:messaging:telegram:.agentmaster`, dev's topic state copied to `~\.agentmaster\messaging\telegram\state.json`) — ⚠ production 0.6.8.20 was built 2026-10-14, BEFORE the Telegram feature; **§21.12.5 (2026-10-17 midday):** a purged Release build of 46e3aad42 checked in `Q:\agentmaster-layouts\Release` (692 s), and a one-shot scheduled task (session 0b1f8635, running inside production) swaps production onto it once that session's turn ends, writing "Run the Telegram bot" + the owner id into the release `settings.json` while production is STOPPED (0.6.8.20 saves settings from a struct without the messaging keys, so any earlier write could be dropped); its first start lists ~411 topics (406 of 666 tabs are saved "Waiting for you") and replays ~2,000 turns (~4,000 silent messages, ~5 h) — the user confirmed; DONE: production 0.6.8.21 runs the bot since 12:01 — the user had quit production at 12:00:34, so the swap stopped nothing; connected 12:01:04, 413 topics by ~12:25, no 429; **§21.13 (2026-10-17, 3a73980d9; LIVE on dev 0.0.1.23 since 13:16 — its 48 topics re-squared quietly in ~10 min, no 429; not in production):** every title the bot prints wears its TAB'S COLOR as a square emoji (🟥 🟧 🟨 🟩 🟦 🟪 🟫 ⬛ ⬜ by hue, `TgTitleColorSquare`; measured: Telegram refuses colored HTML, a rich message's mark is the client's own color, custom emoji need Premium) — after the state glyph in the topic NAME (`🟡 🟦 title`, the user's "State, then color"), on the card, in the Search answers and in every reply, resolved like the tab (`ResolveSessionColorHex`, one dir-colors snapshot per sync); a new title or a tab's new color posts a silent line `“🟦 old” changed the title to “🟩 new”` / `… changed the color to 🟩` (the user's wording; Telegram's own notice is still deleted); a color change waits 8 s and the name tick's budget (`PickNameRename`), and a mode switch, a topic named before the square or one under 60 s old changes quietly (`TopicRecord::nameColor` / `nameColorMode`); **§21.14 (2026-10-17, 4f3c643d9; LIVE on dev 0.0.1.24 since 17:24, not in production; the user's "rename topic when the topic has a running session (blue circle) instead of the yellow one or as red when error"):** a topic's NAME follows its session during the history replay — renames needed the call window under 12 calls a minute and the replay held it at 18 for hours (production: 1 rename in five hours, every topic stuck at 🟡); now a rename goes when the window leaves room for its two calls under 18 (`NameRenameFits`) and the replay HOLDS for a due rename up to 90 s (`HistoryYieldsToRename`, `_renameWaitingSinceMs`), and a rename refused with a 429 stays due; and a LONG 429 (retry_after over 60 s) PARKS only the refused method (`FloodParking` / `ParkForFlood`: its items stay queued, every other method goes on; a second method refused parks them all) — the send loop used to sleep the whole retry_after, and an editMessageText answered with 6542 s silenced production's bot from 17:00:59 to ~18:50; the tick's topic calls park the same way (`_handleTickFlood`; createForumTopic's sleep did not even end for Stop); the traffic line counts edits by kind (`edits(card=… prompt=… worklog=… other=…)`); ⚠ that 6542 s hold covers the WHOLE chat — the topic renames tried at 17:06 got the same deadline, 18:50:02 (§21.14.5) — so parking cannot shorten it, and why Telegram answered hours at the cap with a 1 h 49 min hold is not known (123 of 412 topics still to replay); **§21.15 (2026-10-17, 7017fd863; LIVE on dev 0.0.1.25 since 18:25, not in production; the user's "Go for 10 with copy_text button is great, even better if we can specify hex color code or equivalent of hex color code"):** every title the bot prints in a MESSAGE is a COLOR CHIP — an in-text copy_text button of a rich message in the Telegram look nearest the tab's color (🟦 primary, 🟩 success, 🟥 danger, the plain button for the other squares: Telegram gives a button no hex color, only three looks the app paints, and exact colors would need custom emoji, which need the bot owner's Premium), the §21.13 square in its label, a tap copying the title; the builders write title TOKENS that render classic (the §21.13 text byte for byte, so the card's hash and the rich-switch-off path are unchanged) or rich (`TgRichHtmlFromClassic`: rich HTML reads a raw line break as a space, so `<p>` / `<br>` carry the structure; a chip is lifted out of bold or italic); a refused rich message goes as HTML in its place; the cards that exist are converted one at a time (`_takeCardFormatSlot`, one per 10 s within the budget; `TopicRecord::cardRich`, from Telegram's answer); topic NAMES keep the square; Telegram Desktop shows the chips from 7.1; **§21.16 (2026-10-17, 00b4bc3aa; LIVE on dev 0.0.1.26 since 20:40 — message 916 posted rich and pinned —, not in production; the user's "a pinned message per bot … a nice list of dedicated topics … all tags and the tabs in the tag … titles with coloring and when clicked jumps to the topic … two rows of stats"):** ONE pinned DIRECTORY message in each bot chat's main view ("All", the General topic) — two stats rows FIRST (the pinned bar shows the start: `🔵 5 running · 🟡 15 waiting · ⚪ 300 idle`, then `🟠 need approval · 🔴 error · 🟢 done · ⏳ queued` and `⏸ runner paused` while paused, over the `/board` population), the DEDICATED topics (🔎 Search; `_directoryTopics()` is the list), and every tag that an open tab carries with those tabs (a tag on no open tab is left out and never edits the message — the user's "Tags with 0 tabs should not be shown", the same evening, a7196c11b, LIVE on dev 0.0.1.27 since 21:17: its first start edited message 916 down to the one tag an open tab carries), each title a URL chip `https://t.me/<bot>/<topic>?topic=<topic>` in its tab color's look (a tab with no topic: a disabled chip); a footer whose relative `tg-time` the apps keep current; pure in `AgentMaster/MessagingDirectory.h`, run by `_directoryTick` (a check every 15 s, an edit at most every 2 min and only when the content hash or the form changed; posted once and pinned, the pin's note deleted, a deleted directory posted again after 30 min); ⚠ no link opens a topic of a bot chat on Telegram ANDROID (by its source, and the user saw it: the link lands in "All" at the topic's first message; Desktop opens the topic when its topic list is loaded, iOS should by its reply-thread path), so each listed tab's state DOT is a callback button (`d:front:<thread>`) that puts its topic FIRST in the thread list on every app — a silent re-post of its card, which the quiet re-sort then leaves on top until the session's next turn (§21.16.7, the user's "Link + dot button", e4c953201, LIVE on dev 0.0.1.28 since 2026-10-18 00:09 — its first start edited message 916 with its dot button; a tap on the phone is not checked yet); the tag palette moved into the engine (`SessionStore.h` `kTagPaletteRgb` / `TagPaletteIndexFor` / `ResolveTagDisplayHex`, unchanged values) so the bot and the app paint one tag one color (Rule #17); since 2026-10-19 (§21.16.8, in production since 0.6.8.23 and dev since 0.0.1.31; the user's request) the directory ENDS with a 🕘 Recent activity section — the ten newest open Claude tabs that are not stationary (only Done — claude ended — is left out; Idle tabs are listed too, the user's "Do include idle ones please" the same day), in the topic list's MOST RECENT order, each row a tagged tab's dot + chip (`PickDirectoryRecentTabs`, `DirectoryStateIsStationary`), built before the tag sections so a full message never drops it; **§21.17 (2026-10-18, ac4167e63, LIVE on dev 0.0.1.29 since 02:27 — all 48 cards relinked, no 429; the user's "the title at 2nd row … clickable jumping to the thread … Other titles … should jump to the thread … should be a reply to previous user message … When creating a new thread … ask user to pick workdir"):** a title token can name its topic (`kTgTitleTokenThread`), and every title the bot prints is then a LINK to it — a URL chip (rich) or an `<a>` (classic) through ONE builder, `TgTopicUrl`, the directory's too (Android still lands in "All", §21.16.3); every hash is taken WITHOUT the username, so the card's link is a FORM (`TopicRecord::cardLinked`) the slow upgrade adds one card at a time; every "👤 You" line has the session's title as its SECOND ROW (`UserLineTitleRow`, live, status edits, history); a turn's prompt line REPLIES to the previous turn's (live: the mirror's `PromptRef` / the record; history: a chain kept in `TopicRecord::historyPromptMessageId`); a thread the USER opens starts a NEW SESSION — the bot holds what the user writes there (👀) and posts a folder PICKER (`MessagingNewThread.h`: 🕘 Recent and 🏆 Most used over every registry session via the lean `SessionRegistry::PlaceSamples`, temp roots and gone folders left out, ten a page, each list with a PAGER row — `[1 / 4] [Recent ▶]`, `[◀ Recent] [2 / 4] [Recent ▶]`, `[◀ Recent] [4 / 4]`, the label a no-op that answers which page it is (2026-10-18, `BuildNewThreadPagingRow`, NOT BUILT yet; dev's registry knows only 20 folders, so its lists have two pages) —, `nt:` buttons, a typed path also picks), a pick opens a background tab under a session id the bot minted (`RemoteLaunchRequest::newSessionId` → `_LaunchClaudeSession`'s `presetSessionId` → `BuildClaudeSpawn`'s `targetSessionId`, now honored by a fresh launch) and the tick BINDS the user's thread as its topic before the sync's plan (`_adoptBoundThreads`), handing over the held messages; the thread's explicit name is the title, and a rename of the thread renames the session (`forum_topic_edited`, `StripTopicNameDecoration`); **§21.25 (2026-10-18, NOT BUILT yet; the user's request): the picker is the first step of a WIZARD** (`MessagingNewThreadWizard.h`, one picker message edited step by step) — a TYPED folder that does not exist is checked case-insensitively (`ResolveNewThreadFolder`: a Windows lookup's disk spelling, a listing for a case-sensitive folder; never a second folder that differs only in case) and offered for creation ("create it?" → Create / Back), then an optional MODEL (the right-click menu's models and presets — `SetLaunchMenuSource`, set by the app layer — or a typed id like `claude-opus-5-5[1m]`, checked by `ParseNewThreadModelId` before it can reach the pwsh line; skip = Default), then a WINDOW by number with each window's tab count, newest tab and ten newest titles (the census, on a worker thread); the default is the window of the NEWEST tab working in that folder (launch or inferred; the new, persisted `SessionInfo::createdUnixMs`), and the new tab opens right after it (`RemoteLaunchRequest::windowId` routes to that window only, `afterSessionId` places the tab); ✖ Cancel at any step and a day of nothing chosen DELETE the thread (the user's rule for an aborted creation; what it held is copied into the main view), a refused launch goes back to the step that can fix it and is never retried without a tap, and the wizards and launches persist in `state.json` (`MessagingState::newThreads`) across restarts; ⚠ the build must purge (`SessionModels.h`); **§21.24 (2026-10-18/19, the user's requests; 4d8826851 + bcc546a30 in production 0.6.8.22 and dev 0.0.1.30 since 2026-10-19 — the builds of ec966a2ca; d0fbc4e31, the Restart / Resume rule, in production since 0.6.8.23 and dev since 0.0.1.31):** the sticky card's buttons on TWO PAGES, two a row — MAIN [⚙️ Session], with [🔄 Restart Session] beside it only for a session in Error or one whose claude exited (the user's rule of 2026-10-19), or [▶ Reactivate] for a parked tab; ⚙️ Session flips the card's keyboard to SESSION: [⑂ Fork] [🆕 New Session] first (the user's placement), [🔄 Restart Session] with [🔧 Resume Session] — ▶ Reactivate's action under the user's name — beside it in Error, the old five ([🖥 Screen Text] being the old Screen), [🖼 Screen Image] and [◀ Back]; Restart on a PARKED tab starts it in place, as Reactivate does (the window's restart refuses a never-started connection — the typed `/restartsession` did nothing there either, fixed too) (`BuildCardButtonRows`, `CardState::page` in memory, a tap edits at an answer's priority, a card that starts to ring goes back to Main); ⑂ Fork / 🆕 New Session / 🔄 Restart Session run the topic's `/forksession` / `/newsession` / `/restartsession`; 🖼 Screen Image and `/screenimage` send the terminal's screen as a PHOTO — the hosting window reads the live screen as a STYLED snapshot (`ControlCore::ReadScreenSnapshot` / `TermControl::ReadScreenSnapshot`, new in both `.idl` files: the active buffer's mutable viewport, colors as the renderer resolves them; `ScreenSnapshot.h`), a worker thread draws it with Direct2D + DirectWrite (`ScreenImage.{h,cpp}`: whole-pixel cells, box and block glyphs stretched to join, color emoji; measured 88–183 ms for 200×60–300×80), `sendPhoto` with a document fallback; not redacted (the §9.6 redactor is not built); ⚠ the `.idl` change needs the build's purge; **§21.26 (2026-10-19, in production since 0.6.8.23 and dev since 0.0.1.31; the user's request):** the sticky card LEADS with its STATE line (`🟡 Waiting for you since 08:21`, a pinned session's 📌 at its end) and puts the title (`📌 🟦 title`) second — every other line keeps its order; the card's hash covers the line order, so the build that brings it edits every card once, like d0fbc4e31's keyboard change (one edit per card when both ship together); **§21.27 (2026-10-19, in production since 0.6.8.23 and dev since 0.0.1.31; the user's request):** the card's place line reads `/source/Agentmaster · agentmaster · opus-5-5 · max · 427K`: a cut folder has no `…` (`detail::ShortPath(dir, 2, false)`), the model has no leading `claude-` (`CardModelLabel`, not `ShortModelName`, whose `opus-5.5` the board shows), and the context size has no `ctx`; it costs no extra edit when it ships with §21.26; **§21.28 (2026-10-19, in production since 0.6.8.23 and dev since 0.0.1.31; the user's "buttons we click on should look 'active' as long as they are waiting for serverside to react"):** a tapped button keeps Telegram's loading indicator until its work is in — the tap's answer (`answerCallbackQuery`) is HELD (`MessagingTaps.h`: per-thread `TapScope`, RAII `TapHold`, `OutItem::tapHolds`; continuations carry it: the window's screen replies, the picture's worker, a launch's revive / new thread's `_bindTapHolds`, the wizard's census, the card's NEXT change `CardState::tapHolds` for ⚙️ Session / a dot / 🔄 Restart / ▶ Reactivate / the confirms) and goes when the last hold goes, or at `kTapAnswerHoldMaxMs` (9 s; Telegram drops an answer ~15 s after the tap, measured by others) with the handler's toast or "⏳ Still working on it …"; `[msg] tap <button>: answer queued N ms after the tap` in hooks.log; **§21.29 (2026-10-19, in production since 0.6.8.23 and dev since 0.0.1.31; the user's rule "We dont need the first as long as second always sends on a change"):** a `/rename` posts NO reply of its own — the topic's change line `“🟧 old” changed the title to “🟧 new”` is its one answer (a rename to the current title gets "The title is already …") — and that line now goes out for EVERY change: HELD while the topic's history is replayed (`TopicRecord::changeLineHeld` + its from-title and from-square, persisted; `HoldTopicChangeLine`, posted by `_pumpBackfill` through `TakeHeldTopicChangeLine` once the replay is done), a rename whose call got no answer made again at the next tick instead of recorded as done, and Telegram's `TOPIC_NOT_MODIFIED` counted as a rename that took (`TgResult::TopicNotModified`; `NotModified` knows only a message edit's answer); **§21.31 (2026-10-19, in production since 0.6.8.23 and dev since 0.0.1.31; the user's request):** an IDLE session's topic is deleted (confirmed: Idle, not pinned, untouched for `messagingIdleGraceMinutes`, default 10 min — D25), and the chat's MAIN VIEW ("All") now gets ONE silent line for it, `💤 🟧 title is idle — its topic left the list. /idle in 🔎 Search wakes it.`: `_deleteTopic(…, "idle")` adds the title and square to `_idleNoticePending`, and `_flushIdleNotices` (each tick, after the sync) posts once the deletes have settled — 10 s quiet or 60 s at most (`IdleNoticeDue`) — so an app start's burst is ONE line of at most 20 titles (`BuildIdleNoticeHtml`); a closed tab's topic stays quiet; **§21.32 (2026-10-19, RESEARCH ONLY):** a tap on a file link (Claude's `file:///` links, shown as dead hyperlinks today) can download the file WITHOUT a file message only through a web server the phone reaches — a plain `http(s)://` link to an endpoint on the PC, or the bot's Main Mini App opened by `https://t.me/<bot>?startapp=<token>` calling `Telegram.WebApp.downloadFile` (Bot API 8.0, an HTTPS file URL; the phone fetches it, Telegram does not); this PC serves nothing on 80/443 and the LAN services are LAN/VPN-only; with no server only a file MESSAGE works (`t.me/<bot>?start=<token>` → `sendDocument`); **§21.32.1 (2026-10-19, DESIGN ONLY, the owner's decisions open; the user's "Lets do cc.sysd.cc … does agentmaster host or rely on user to provide a template … What if we provide both"):** the name `cc.sysd.cc` follows `catalog.sysd.cc`'s LAN-only pattern (a Technitium zone → Caddy 192.168.50.5 with the Let's Encrypt `*.sysd.cc` wildcard → a port on this PC that a firewall rule opens to Caddy only); four modes over ONE link rewrite (`file:///…` → the link template, e.g. `https://cc.sysd.cc/f/%KEY%/%FILENAME%`): **Built-in** (a listener in Agentmaster, pull — the proposal), **Relay** (the owner's server, e.g. a fastrun app, pulling from it: Mini App page, `initData` check), **Upload** (a copy pushed through a configurable request — method, URL / header / body templates, `%SECRET:<name>%` from Credential Manager, a signed webhook; fastrun keeps no file, so it needs a store), **Link only** (a `%SIG%` + `%EXP%`-signed template for a server that reads the files itself); keys are bearer secrets serving every fetch until they expire (one tap fetches 2–3 times); ⚠ Telegram Android never answers a client-certificate request and downloads through Android's DownloadManager, so Caddy's sysd gate refuses Telegram's own downloads through the VPN (they work on the home Wi-Fi; Chrome / Brave work through the VPN with the access certificate) unless the `cc.sysd.cc` block leaves the gate out; **§21.32.2 (2026-10-19, DESIGN ONLY, the owner's second round; it replaces §21.32.1's proposal and four modes):** the file links are TELEGRAM-ONLY (the Messaging tab, the Telegram builders, keys per topic session, a listener that runs only with the bot) in TWO modes — **self-hosted**, a listener in Agentmaster on the LAN whose listen URLs (`http://<LAN address>:<port>/`, one per up LAN interface, plus the PC's name) the tab offers in a dropdown as the links' base (Telegram allows cleartext and so does the DownloadManager it hands downloads to, so a tap works inside Telegram at home and through the sysd VPN; a firewall rule by PORT, since the layout folder changes at every swap), and **hosted**, `cc.sysd.cc` on the Cloudflare zone with mTLS only (the access certificate at the edge, plus the zone's post-quantum guard and Israel rule; the hosts rule must admit the name, the 5 Free custom rules are all in use), keyed by `%SESSIONKEY%` = HMAC-SHA256 of the session id with a shared secret (a plain hash is no key: session ids are printed everywhere); ⚠ Telegram can never pass the mTLS check, so the phone needs Telegram → Settings → Chat Settings → Web Browser → "Don’t open in-app" → `sysd.cc`, which opens the link in Chrome / Brave (the Mini App way is out); open: what "custom hash" means, H1 (a tunnel home, a fastrun relay pulling from the listener, the PC on) or H2 (a Cloudflare Worker + R2, Agentmaster uploading each linked file, the PC may be off), one link or two; **§21.32.3 (2026-10-19, DESIGN ONLY, the owner's third round; it replaces §21.32.2's mTLS-only hosting and HMAC):** H1 chosen; ONE link shape `<base>/station-<station>/<hash>/<exact path>` (`https://cc.sysd.cc` or a LAN URL from the dropdown; station `homepc` by default; the path with the drive as its first segment); `<hash>` = a CUSTOM SHA-256 (`Sha256.h` with its eight initial words taken from a 32-byte secret in Credential Manager) of `<station>:<session id>`, 64 lowercase hex — ⚠ the repository is PUBLIC, so the secret words, not the code change, are what keep it custom; the listener serves only a path the bot LINKED in that session (else one link opens any file by editing its URL); Cloudflare's Free edge can check the pattern INCLUDING the hash's length and characters without a regex (`matches` needs Business; `substring` + `len` + `remove_bytes` do it), folded into the existing rules (the hosts rule opens `cc.sysd.cc` only for the pattern; the certificate rule and the challenge skip it) — a well-formed wrong hash still reaches home (404) unless a Worker + KV list stops it; the tunnel's connector in its own container, its ingress regex repeating the check; ⚠ the zone's post-quantum guard may block Telegram's downloads: WebView (Chromium) offers X25519MLKEM768, but an attachment goes to Android's DownloadManager on the platform TLS, which a hands-on April 2026 test found without it — to test on the phone; `/download <path>` answers with the same link plus a 📤 Send as file button (a multipart upload from the PC, ≤ 50 MB) — Telegram fetching the hosted URL itself works only for .PDF / .ZIP ≤ 20 MB and from outside Israel, so not proposed; **§21.30 (2026-10-19, in production since 0.6.8.23 and dev since 0.0.1.31; the user's "Kindly close the gap, enabling telegram picking a choice, including the user manual text input … a managed message that will cover this interaction and including custom answer"):** a Claude Code `AskUserQuestion` dialog is ANSWERED FROM ITS TOPIC through a QUESTION CARD — ONE managed message per dialog, edited in place: the options as buttons (a tap answers a one-question card at once, the PC's Enter; several questions are TABS with the PC's review — the 2026-10-19 evening change, NOT BUILT: one tab per question, a pick moving on to the next unanswered one and then to "Review your answers" with 📨 Submit answers; before it, ◀ / ▶ and 📨 Send answers; multi-select toggles ☐ / ☑ and keeps the TICK order, as the PC does), ✍️ the user's own answer and 📝 a preview question's note through a ForceReply prompt (or any reply to the card), the previews inline as monospace blocks and 🖼 as one picture on demand, 💬 Chat about this, ✖️ Cancel (the evening's change: confirmed first, then the PC's Esc — deny + `interrupt`, the turn ends); a PLAIN message while the card is open is held and asked about (my answer, 💬 a reply instead of answering — Claude Code's own `response` field, the evening's change —, or the next prompt?); Claude Code's idle timeout is shown as such (evening); the answer is composed EXACTLY as the PC composes it (`AskQuestion.h`; proven live against Claude Code 2.1.291); the way in is a `PermissionRequest` hook (`<profile>\agentmaster-ask.ps1`, exec form, in the hooks settings only while the bot is on) whose request goes over `\\.\pipe\agentmaster.<pid>.ask` (`HooksBridge.h` `AskBridge` / `AskRegistry`; the service is its acceptor) and which prints the decision; the card's own watch reads the transcript for the result — ⚠ Claude Code does NOT end the hook when the PC answers first, so the engine RELEASES it (and when the session ends, and every one at the bot's Stop); every answer line now carries the NOTES; `Messaging.Questions.cpp`; INTERACTIVE_TOOLS.md §10; **§21.33 (2026-10-19, in production since 0.6.8.23 and dev since 0.0.1.32; the user's "Add support for /steer which is like send-now"):** `/steer <message>` in a session's topic types the words into the box NOW — the Manager's Send now through `SubmitPrompt`, with a new prompt — so a running turn takes them in at its next step (Claude Code's own mid-turn handling) and a session at rest starts a turn; never typed into a tab whose claude is not started (it is started, the words wait first in the queue), one whose claude ended (a shell may be there) or a menu on the terminal (`DecideSteer`, `AgentMaster/MessagingSteer.h`); the status is a reaction on the /steer message (⚡ sending → 👨‍💻 taken, ✍ queued), a send the window declines comes back as the first queued prompt and is told once (`_sweepSteers`), and D8 still holds for every plain message; **§21.34 (2026-10-19, in production since 0.6.8.23 and dev since 0.0.1.32; the user's "support /add-dir from here and also picker for … the menu that comes after … The default is the 1st option and … auto-answer after 60s in a clean way"):** `/add-dir <path>` from a topic is queued and WATCHED (`Messaging.Menus.cpp`, the pure half `AgentMaster/TuiMenus.h`): Claude Code runs it itself, with no hook and nothing in the transcript until its "Add directory to workspace" dialog is answered, so the hosting window READS the dialog off the screen (`menu-screen`, `ParseAddDirMenuScreen` — pinned on the probe's own capture, `tools/add-dir-probe/`) and the topic gets ONE ringing MENU CARD (options 1 Yes, for this session · 2 Yes, and remember this directory · 3 No as buttons, a `<tg-time>` countdown); a tap, or nobody within 60 s (the DEFAULT, option 1 — `kTuiMenuAutoAnswerMs`), asks the window to press the digit (`menu-answer`), which it types ONLY into that very dialog, read again at the press (`DecideMenuAnswer`: still open, the card's path, no delivery holding the box); the transcript's result edits the card with the outcome and who answered (Telegram, by itself after 60 s, or the PC — whose dialog stays usable, first answer wins); a command with no dialog gets its result as a reply, a bare `/add-dir` (a path input) is refused with the usage line; and two delivery fixes beside it (DELIVERY.md §18) — the scanner consumes a LOCAL command's transcript echo as its pull echo (before, every `/add-dir` or `/model x` a topic sent got a 👎 and paused the runner 15 s later) and the lost-send verdict waits while a menu is open; **§21.35 (2026-10-20, in production since 0.6.8.23 and dev since 0.0.1.32; the user's "Reduce wasted cpu and disk on the Telegram bot's background loop"):** the tick marks the state file dirty only for a field that changed (`CopyBackTopicProgress`) and as PROGRESS, saved at most every 10 s (a structural change still within 2 s; a topic's posted clock and its card's hash are progress too); a save whose bytes equal the file's writes nothing; the backup `state.json.prev` is copied once a minute and never from a file this run could not read; the app's last window flushes the state (`NoteAppClosing`, at most 250 ms of lock wait under the window mutex); the card refresh reuses the pass's session copy, and a transcript is stat'ed before it is opened — production had rewritten its 465 KB `state.json` every ~3.2 s unchanged (~24 GB/day, UI_THREAD.md §13d); the cost: up to 10 s of mirrored lines posted twice after an UNEXPECTED end; ⚠ `MessagingService` gained data members: both configurations' builds of af3d40b5e (2026-10-20) were purged; **§21.32.4 (2026-10-20, the owner's "Implement and host using fastrun and the tunnel on this machine"; the Agentmaster half in dev since 0.0.1.33, not in production, the route home LIVE):** FILE LINKS — with the cog's Messaging ▸ FILE LINKS on (Off / On the home network / Through cc.sysd.cc; `AppSettings::messagingFileLinks*`, default Off), every `[x](file:///K:/…)` in Claude's text (the mirror and the history replay, `_filesRewriteItems`) to an existing file that is no folder, looks like no secret and lies in no excluded folder becomes `<base>/station-<station>/<session key>/<encoded path>`, and `/download <path>` answers with a link and a **📤 Send as file** button (an upload from the PC, at most 50 MB); the session key is SHA-256 with eight secret initial words (`Sha256WithInitialState`; the 32-byte secret in Credential Manager, `Agentmaster:messaging:telegram-filelinks:<profile leaf>`, made on first use); the server (`MessagingFileServer.{h,cpp}`, one per install: release `homepc` on 32594, dev `homepc-dev` on 32595, private peers only) serves only a file its session linked (the LINKED SET, `<profile>\messaging\telegram\filelinks.json`: 30 days after the last mention, 4,000 files a session) and re-checks the FINAL path; the pure rules are `MessagingFileLinks.h`, the service's half `Messaging.Files.cpp`; the hosted route: Cloudflare's edge admits only that URL shape (K:\source\proxmox `sysd/cloudflare/10-zone.sh`, `SYSD_CC*`, the post-quantum guard on), the tunnel `sysd-cc` (its connector: the service `SysdCcTunnel` on this PC), the fastrun relay `cc-relay` (nginx, port 2104, a station → address map; `cc-sysd/`); checked end to end through Cloudflare with a stand-in station (a post-quantum handshake → 200, a classical one → 403, a malformed URL → 403 at the edge, a wrong key → 404 from the station); open: Android's downloader against the post-quantum guard (the fallback is `SYSD_CC_PQ_GUARD=off`); ⚠ `SessionModels.h` changed: the next build must purge; **§21.36 (2026-10-22, RESEARCH ONLY; the user's "Can telegram bot know if user is looking actively at a topic?"):** NO — the Bot API (10.3 still the newest) has no read receipt, presence, typing or "chat open" signal in any update or method, and TDLib drops read receipts for a bot (`read_history_outbox` returns at once for `is_bot()`); only the user's own actions name a topic. But a live DRAFT (`sendMessageDraft`) has its own limit, the typing actions' 20 per 5 s and 40 per 30 s per peer (Telegram's "AI features for bots"; `sendChatAction` shares it), apart from the ~18 calls a minute that messages and edits share, so live state fits every Running topic as a draft refreshed every ~25 s, with ~2 s refreshes for the topic of the user's last action; a draft lives 30 s, sits UNDER the sticky card (never above it) and any post in the topic removes it; edited messages fit one topic at a time; **§21.37 (2026-10-22, NOT BUILT yet; HOOKS.md *Interrupted turns*):** a restart or a fork the phone asks for never pops a dialog on the PC — `/restartsession` at rest and the card's 🔄 Restart ask "Continue the interrupted turn?" in the topic (▶ Continue the turn / ⏸ Keep it interrupted / Cancel) when the session's tail is a user interrupt a rewind would continue, and pass the answer through `ServiceHooks::restartSession(id, RestartTurnChoice)` and the engine's restart sink; `/forksession`, the card's ⑂ Fork and the Search topic's ⑂ pick ask "Fork interrupted / uninterrupted" when the fork would continue the source's turn (`RemoteLaunchRequest::forkTurn`, resolved by `_requestLaunch` before the request leaves the bot); **§21.38 (2026-10-23, NOT BUILT yet; the user's "I expected Activate button but instead I had resume, fork and others but we already exist"):** a 🔎 Search pick's menu fits where the session stands — `ClassifySearchPickState` now reads whether the tab's claude runs (`started`, `external`) and knows a deactivated tab with or without its topic (🌙) and an exited claude (⏹): ▶ Activate starts it in place through `_reactivate` (the window's `start` = the tab menu's Activate Tab, no focus change; an exited one is restarted) and then holds an Idle one in Waiting for you so its topic appears; an idle tab whose claude runs gets ⏰ Wake, one with a topic 💬 Go to topic (`_bringTopicToFront`), and only a CLOSED session is offered ▶ Resume; no button pins (the user's "Remove the pin overloads": the 📌 variants and ▶ Resume 📌 are gone, a session is pinned in its own topic); every tap re-reads the state, so Resume / Activate / Wake all "make it usable from here" (`SearchPickMenuRows`, `kSearchPick*`, MessagingModels.h); **§22 (2026-10-17, DESIGN ONLY): folder groups** — a bot cannot create groups, communities or folders (Bot API ≤ 10.3), a topic belongs to one chat, so the proposal MIRRORS each session into a forum group per folder (the user creates each group, the bot is added by a `startgroup` + `admin` link), the private chat staying the all-topics view, optionally folded with the groups into a Telegram Community; the key measured: 23 groups by launch folder with worktrees folded vs 49 by inferred folder, 21% of topics elsewhere and ~38% changing as they work; decisions G1–G5 open), the code: `AgentMaster/Messaging.{h,cpp}` + `Messaging.Inbound.cpp` (the service inside the engine: poll / send / tick threads, the topic sync, the transcript mirror, remote prompts, `/commands`, buttons, the Search topic), `TelegramApi.{h,cpp}`, the pure `TelegramFormat.h` + `MessagingModels.h`, `MessagingSearchBridge.cpp`, `TerminalPage.AgentMessaging.cpp`, the cog's **Messaging** tab (the token goes to Credential Manager, never a file), `QueuedPrompt::remoteSource` (D30: a topic's message is sent whatever the runner's mode) and `SessionInfo::pinned` (§5.3, the pin hold); built into dev on 2026-10-16, into production on 2026-10-17 (0.6.8.21); Teams / webhooks / external connectors still design only; the user's decisions in §18: the bot runs INSIDE the running Agentmaster process; a topic shows the WHOLE session, every message type Off / Default / Verbose; a message in a topic is a QUEUED prompt, never a steer; Claude Code only; Claude Code commands that leave no record are refused; the tab menu's non-destructive actions + `/restartsession` are `/commands`; every topic message is silent except the turn's final one): FleetLink + the messaging hub + connector threads in the engine, the hardening that replaces a process boundary (H1–H6), the per-chat ~1 message/s budget and its pacing, the 24-hour backlog rule, what a topic cannot do (§10.4); - GPU vs software rendering (every shown terminal owns its own D3D11 device; past ~62 in one process the NVIDIA driver hang-recovers at every new tab or focus and a new tab can stay BLACK; the cog's **Software rendering** switch, its measured CPU/RAM cost, the read-only diagnosis; §4b: what 100 tabs cost with one on screen — the frame pipeline, hidden working tabs still painting, the driver's per-device polling, the tab-header animations that keep XAML ticking): [`doc/agentmaster/RENDERING.md`](doc/agentmaster/RENDERING.md).
- Where the release's CPU goes at fleet scale (550 tabs, measured 2026-10-03 without suspending a thread): the UI thread's 60 Hz XAML frame loop that Forever pulses keep running (composition walk + storyboard ticks, worst with the side panel's non-virtualized card list), the per-tab `LayoutUpdated` fan-out (every never-initialized `TermControl` + every tab header), the tag-badge transforms, the engine's per-pass registry copies; ranked fix directions; and `tools/stacksnap.cpp`, the stack sampler that is safe on production (it never suspends a thread — unlike `tools/livesample.exe`); §11: the bookmark-tag surfaces — the Tags panel read all 2,181 session-store files on the UI thread at every open and toggle (2026-10-10, the scan cache; NOT BUILT yet); §12: the freeze of 2026-10-14 — a re-bind loop, overlay grips and board ScrollViewers that never freed, ~4 s rebuilds back to back; the persistent board columns and the adaptive rebuild gap (NOT BUILT yet); §13: production 0.6.8.22 measured again on 2026-10-20 at ~700 tabs (42% of one core on average over 19 h, 52% at a quiet night): no animation drives XAML any more, but every frame (33–38 a second, from property changes) walks 3,634 composition nodes, 81% of them side-panel cards (two windows show the panel GLOBAL: ~706 cards each, all loaded), ~250 ms/s of the UI thread; the same cards hold ~224 MB of DirectWrite state; the Telegram bot rewrites its whole 465 KB `state.json` every 3.2 s unchanged (an unconditional `_stateDirty` per topic per tick: ~24 GB/day of forced writes; fixed in code 2026-10-20, in production since 0.6.8.23 and dev since 0.0.1.32 — MESSAGING.md §21.35) and walks all 441 topics every 1.5 s; Agent Manager rebuilds still take 1–2.7 s; ranked fixes, the zero-build mitigation (GLOBAL → LOCAL), and `tools/cpuhunt/` + `tools/leakhunt/xamltree.py`; §14: the side panel's cards fixed in code on 2026-10-20 (in production since 0.6.8.23 and dev since 0.0.1.32) — childless rounded backgrounds (no composition node per card) and a virtualized list (SIDE_PANEL.md §2f), with the WinUI evidence and the expected effect (~700 nodes instead of 3,634): [`doc/agentmaster/UI_THREAD.md`](doc/agentmaster/UI_THREAD.md).
- Memory — leaks and what holds production's 2 GB (2026-10-19 → 20, four read-only dumps of the RUNNING release with `tools/leakhunt`: a PSS-snapshot dumper, a vftable census, a segment-heap walker for live blocks, owner attribution, diffs across dumps; ⚠ corrected 2026-10-20: the first walk read the 26200 LFH busy bitmap in the 2016 paper's layout and halved every small-block count, §7): the one leak is upstream Windows Terminal's — `UTextAdapter.cpp`'s `utextClone` lost `UTEXT_HEAP_ALLOCATED`, so every URL scan / Find leaked a 144-byte ICU `UText` (381,056 = 52 MB after 17.5 h, 47,000–61,000 an hour while terminals print; FIXED in source, in production since 0.6.8.23 and dev since 0.0.1.31, proven by `tools/leakhunt/utext_clone_leak_repro.cpp`); the rest is bounded but multiplied by ~700 tabs — ~0.5 GB of DirectWrite state of measured TextBlocks, ~205 MB of eagerly built tab context menus with their `ToolTip`s, 790,000 XAML event sources, `SaveSessions`' 25 MB cached JSON, the summary's upstream cache holding every line uuid of its chains (~8 MB): [`doc/agentmaster/MEMORY_LEAKS.md`](doc/agentmaster/MEMORY_LEAKS.md).
- Release symbols (2026-10-23; in `release.yml` + `ci.yml`, not run on GitHub yet): every release ships the PDB of every binary it builds - PUBLIC PDBs (`/PDBSTRIPPED`) beside the binaries in the portable zips, the FULL ones in `Agentmaster_<ver>_<arch>_symbols.zip`, Source-Linked + srcsrv-indexed to the commit, each matched to its binary by GUID + age; why (959 MB of full PDBs per arch, ~70% of it C++/WinRT and STL code compiled into our own functions, so no "our code only" cut exists; a native PDB cannot be embedded), how to use them, the local builds (a PDB beside every binary; Debug's are FASTLINK) and what was checked: [`doc/agentmaster/SYMBOLS.md`](doc/agentmaster/SYMBOLS.md).
- End-to-end UI testing (2026-10-23): the scripted model, guest runner, UI driver and host orchestrator now exist under `e2e/`; 67 tests (the Agent Manager page, the side panel, the tab's context menu, the tab life cycle, workspace quit/restore, accessibility, robustness, the CLI) against Claude Code 2.1.291 in a claude-fleet desktop: the suite of f710660e0 (49 tests) was green on portable 0.8.0; all 67 are green on a TEST build 0.8.1.1 holding every fix of 2026-10-23 (runs `fixall-0811` + `fixall-0811b`, NOT BUILT into a release), and each `needs_fix` test fails on 0.8.0 on its defect (flows/README *Checked both ways*). The spike's native Claude/mock findings remain in `tools/e2e-spike/`; the proposed app testability changes (E2E timings, read-only bridge) remain unimplemented. The UI Automation half (E2E_TESTING.md §6.3) is in the code since 2026-10-23, NOT BUILT (flows TAB-K3 + A11Y-K1): every ContentDialog the page shows is built in code (a declared one was invisible to UI Automation), and `AgentAutomation.h` gives the Agent Manager page, the tabs, the side panel and the per-tab badge `am.*` AutomationIds, names where a control had none, and item status (state, selection, the tab's selection pill, unread, draft); `e2e/suites/test_accessibility.py` checks it (`needs_fix`). Design and current status: [`doc/agentmaster/E2E_TESTING.md`](doc/agentmaster/E2E_TESTING.md); runner API and usage: [`e2e/README.md`](e2e/README.md).

The README maps the implemented subset to tests; the remaining flows are specifications, not claims of coverage: [`doc/agentmaster/flows/README.md`](doc/agentmaster/flows/README.md).
- E2E reliability (2026-10-23, PLAN ONLY): [`doc/agentmaster/E2E_RELIABILITY.md`](doc/agentmaster/E2E_RELIABILITY.md) extends the existing suite with a mandatory setup-through-teardown guard, guest supervisor and host watchdog; scoped expected exits, process generations, crash/corruption evidence, bounded collectors and verified export; separate normal / Verifier-PageHeap / ASan / leak lanes; calibrated live-allocation/resource measurements; and 38 proposed guard/lifecycle/leak/recovery scenarios with phased acceptance. It audits the current fixture's startup, shutdown, child-exit and dump-export gaps. No guard implementation, build, installation or test run was performed for this plan.
- The E2E crash guard (2026-10-23, PLAN ONLY; a second plan for the same request, written independently of `E2E_RELIABILITY.md`): ONE Guard wraps every test — a pytest plugin that starts every process of the test in a per-test JOB OBJECT (exact membership and exit codes even after the app died; measured: the job's "abnormal exit" misses the fail-fast, stowed-exception and heap-corruption codes, so the Guard classifies every exit code itself), watches WER, the app's logs, the UI from outside (measured: UI Automation waits 20 s per call into a hung app unless `CUIAutomation8` sets its timeouts), resources and the profile's files, and stops the test at the crash so the crash, never the timeout after it, is the failure; a Sentinel in the guest (pytest crashed or hung: evidence, kill, resume) and a Watchdog on the host (QEMU's monitor, reset, resume); a special suite — GSF canaries (including a real upstream crash, microsoft/terminal#20570), SDN sudden kill / crash / Windows restart / power loss with a durability check, CST damaged files, MCR page heap / Application Verifier / ASan / lifecycle stress / hostile input, LKS leak cycles with a slope test, `v0.7.10-prerelease` as the UText leak's positive control and `tools/leakhunt` attribution, HMC a hostile machine; decisions CG-D1–CG-D17: [`doc/agentmaster/E2E_CRASH_GUARD.md`](doc/agentmaster/E2E_CRASH_GUARD.md).

## Status

### End-to-end pipeline

- The full pipeline is: Launch → real `claude.exe` on a ConPTY → `--settings` hooks → PowerShell forwarder → named pipe → registry → state machine → UI, plus `claude --resume` restore on reopen (traces in `~/.agentmaster/hooks.log`).

### Workspace persistence

- The registry/bridge/scheduler are now ONE process-wide `SharedEngine` (`AgentMaster/Engine.{h,cpp}`), so the WindowEmperor's many windows share one fleet over one pipe instead of two registries racing on `\\.\pipe\agentmaster.<pid>` and clobbering `sessions.json`.
- The per-window `WindowRecord` schema + `windows/<id>.json` (de)serialize, shaped as **Option 1** — per-window *UI state only* (geometry + Manager lens + ordered tab *refs*); `sessions.json` + `SessionInfo.live` stay the single session source of truth, never duplicated.
- Each window **claims** its `windows/<id>.json` at engine init (`Engine::ClaimWindowRecord`, or mints a fresh GUID), `_CaptureWindowRecord` reads live geometry (the `PersistState` recipe) + ordered tab refs + the Manager lens, a debounced autosave (`_saveWindowRecordThrottled`, fed by tab/resize/recolor/lens-push triggers + a one-shot save at end of startup) persists it, and a claimed record **seeds the lens** (`AgentManagerContent::Get/SetManagerState` + a lens-changed push).
- A relaunched window reopens at its saved position/size/launch-mode via the `TerminalWindow` startup seam (`GetInitialPosition`/`GetLaunchDimensions`/`GetLaunchMode` — WT's own persisted layout is OFF in our DefaultProfile mode; note WT only calls `TerminalPage::PersistState()` when `firstWindowPreference != DefaultProfile`, which our mode is *not*, so geometry capture rides the autosave, not a close-flush).
- On startup the `WindowEmperor` reopens the **open-at-exit** set: it scans `windows/*.json` (sorted by filename, matching `LoadWindowRecords` so `-s <idx>` is canonical), reads the **`open-windows.json` manifest** (a byte-scan for braced `{guid}` tokens — it links `TerminalApp.dll`, not the `TerminalAppLib` static lib, so it can't call the JSON helpers), intersects, and dispatches `wt -w new -s <idx>` only for the records that were open at last exit; each window resolves `records[idx]` for geometry (TerminalWindow) and claims it by id (TerminalPage), so geometry + lens agree.
