- A **decide-prompt** (Yes/No "Reopen your N previous Agentmaster windows?") gates the reopen when >1; a lone record / first run is silent.
- The **manifest** is owned by the process-wide `SharedEngine` (NOT the Emperor enumerating the projected surface — the engine already has every window's id): each `TerminalPage` `RegisterLiveWindow`s its `_windowId` at engine init and `UnregisterLiveWindow`s in `~TerminalPage`, and every change rewrites `open-windows.json` = the live id set **except** a change that empties it (skip-empty preserves the final snapshot; a hard shutdown that kills the threads before they unregister leaves the full set).
- So a window closed mid-session is **pruned** from the manifest (won't be re-offered) while its record stays on disk.
- The Manager's **"Reopen Windows (N)"** recover button (in the toolbar before the cog — `_reopenBtn`, shown when N>0 == records-minus-live, `Engine::RecoverableWindows`) is the "if I answered No" path: it reopens each not-currently-open record via `<our alias> -w -1 -s <idx>` (`TerminalPage::_ReopenSavedWindows` ShellExecutes **`_AgentmasterReopenTarget()`** — the per-IDENTITY execution alias **by name**, `agentmaster.exe` release / `agentmasterdev.exe` dev, so side-by-side installs never reopen into each other; not the upstream `wt.exe`, which doesn't exist for our packages; unpackaged falls back to the neighbor `Agentmaster.exe` — and the single-instance handoff routes it back to the Emperor) — the runtime analog of the Emperor loop.
- A window claimed-then-closed THIS session is **re-claimed** (its real id + lens) from a second **reclaimable pool** (`Engine::reclaimableWindowRecords`, fed by `UnregisterLiveWindow`, drawn by id ONLY), not minted as a lens-less duplicate; the no-arg front-pop claim never draws from it, so a plain "+ new window" never adopts a closed window's layout.
- A reopened window re-homes its **whole workspace**, not just geometry + lens.
- `TerminalPage::_RestoreWindowTabs` (run from `_OnFirstLayout` after `_RestoreClaudeSessions`, gated on a *claimed* record) walks the record's ordered tab refs and rebuilds them in place: each **Claude** ref resumes its session INTO this window (`_LaunchClaudeSession`, lazy-start safe — no eager `connection.Start()`); each **Other** ref replays its captured WT startup actions (`WindowLayout::FromJson` → one `ProcessStartupActions`) to recreate the shell tab with its title + color + cwd.
- Capture fills the Other ref's `actionsJson` from `BuildStartupActions(Persist)` → `WindowLayout::ToJson` in `_CaptureWindowRecord` (so `windows/<id>.json` now carries the pwsh/cmd tabs, not just Claude refs).
- A clobber guard keeps a not-yet-laid-out window (no tabs AND no geometry, or pre-Initialized) from overwriting a good record on disk (`_FlushWindowRecord`), and the close-flush captures the final state before teardown clears `_claudeTabs` (`CloseWindow`).
- **The close-flush is the window's LAST write** (flows TAB-K1, fixed 2026-10-23, NOT BUILT yet). The close is only POSTED to the emperor (`WM_CLOSE_TERMINAL_WINDOW`), so the page and its 750 ms autosave timer outlive the archive that empties `_claudeTabs`; an autosave a tab switch, resize or lens change had armed within 0.75 s of the close then saved every Claude tab as a shell (the e2e suite saw `[teardown-archive]` → 67 ms later `[window-save] … [sh,sh,]`; reproduced on 0.8.0 for a save due 0–~80 ms after the archive, 3 of 9 swept closes, and verified on a test build: `e2e/suites/test_workspace.py`, flows/07). Once `_windowRecordTeardownFlushed` is set, `_FlushWindowRecord` refuses and logs `[window-save] <id> late save refused`, and `_ScheduleWindowRecordSave` arms nothing. ⚠ A new teardown seam must flush BEFORE it sets the latch and archive only AFTER both.
- A reopened (claimed-record) window also **suppresses the default startup tab** — `_OnFirstLayout` gates `ProcessStartupActions` on `!_windowRecordClaimed`, because in DefaultProfile mode a reopen's `wt -w new -s <idx>` (where `ShouldUsePersistedLayout` is off) falls back to a default `newTab` that would otherwise append a spurious pwsh tab to every reopened window and **accumulate one shell per reopen cycle** in the record; a reopened window's content comes from the re-home, not the default action.
- The reopened window also **re-selects the tab that was focused at close** — persisted by STABLE IDENTITY (`WindowRecord::selectedSessionId` = the Claude conversation id; a shell tab, which has no cross-restart id, falls back to `selectedTabIndex`; Manager / none ⇒ both unset).
- `_RestoreWindowTabs` resolves the focused tab's live position and applies it deterministically — a trailing `SwitchToTab` appended to the shell `ProcessStartupActions` batch (so it runs AFTER the async shells exist), or a synchronous select when there are no shells.
- Known limitation: exact left-to-right interleave of Claude vs Other tabs on reopen is not preserved (Claude tabs land first, then shells — see PERSISTENCE.md §13.5).
- **The RESTORE WAKE** (2026-10-19, in production since 0.6.8.23 and dev since 0.0.1.31; [`PERSISTENCE.md`](doc/agentmaster/PERSISTENCE.md) §14; the user's report: with 100 tabs of which 5 were busy, a restart activated only the tab they had been looking at, and the other busy ones stayed stuck). Every re-homed tab stays dormant except the one its window opens to, so a reopened window now also starts, by itself, each restored tab whose session was in the middle of something at the restart — its persisted state was anything but Idle / Waiting-for-you / Done (`SessionInfo::midTurnAtRestore`, re-checked at wake time by `RestoreWakeWantsSession`: a pinned record's Waiting counts, Running elsewhere / Done / a proved turn end do not).
  - **When:** only after the tab EVERY reopened window opens to has finished launching (its claude fired a hook after the restore, a non-Claude terminal started, or nothing to wait for; 15 s hookless settle, 90 s cap) and this window's UI thread is idle (1.5 s, else 30 s). The cross-window join is the process-wide `RestoreWakeBarrier`: `SetAgentmasterWindowId` announces each window at CREATION (the Emperor creates every reopened window before the first lays out), `_RestoreWindowTabs` joins at its end (a window restoring nothing leaves), a 5-minute cap releases stragglers.
  - **How:** one tab at a time in place (`_ActivateDormantSession`, the "Activate Tab" path), each on a slot of the process-wide `RestoreWakePacer` — the "Activate All Tabs" drip's 500 ms / at most 4 per 10 s, but across EVERY window, because the barrier releases all windows at once (per-window drips would start 16 claudes in 1.5 s with 4 windows); the per-window drip itself is untouched. The pure half is `AgentMaster/RestoreWake.h` (`TestRestoreWake`), the glue `TerminalPage.AgentWindowRecord.cpp`; logs `[restore-wake]`. Whether the turn then CONTINUES is Claude Code's (`CLAUDE_CODE_RESUME_INTERRUPTED_TURN=1`, the API-error rewind). ⚠ `TerminalPage` gained data members: purged in dev 0.0.1.31's build and in both builds of af3d40b5e (2026-10-20); `TerminalPage.h` is not on the purge-trigger list, so a change of its members must force the purge.

### The Fleet Observer (O1–O7)

- It is the **PULL** half of the state engine: a process- + transcript-driven survey that detects + correlates + enriches **every** Claude session and tab activity **out-of-band** (reading each `claude.exe`'s PEB — cwd / cmdline / env — and its transcript), so a hand-typed `claude` that fires **zero hooks** (a shell `claude` function/alias shadows the PATH shim) is still detected, bound, and driven — with **no hooks, no shim, no settings, and nothing the user can feel** (all reads are out-of-band; it **never** writes to a shell).
- It is the always-correct floor beneath the lossy hook **push** and subsumes the old per-tab Toolhelp walk + the global `projects/` discovery scan.

### Codex

- **Codex (the OpenAI Codex CLI, `codex.exe`) is a first-class MANAGED agent — observe + state + the full LAUNCH / RESTORE / WINDOW-RESTORE / ADOPT lifecycle (*lifecycle + state only*)** (OBSERVER.md §11f / §19-Q3).
- All Codex integration is zero-write to `~/.codex` (every read is out-of-band, Rule #13).

#### C1 — observe-only enrichment

- The Fleet Observer reads every `codex.exe` out-of-band: `ReadCodexFacts` (PEB cwd/cmdline/env → model `--model`/`-m` · sandbox `--sandbox`/`-s` · approval `--ask-for-approval`/`-a` · `WT_SESSION`/`AM_SESSION`/`CODEX_HOME` · an explicit `resume <guid>`), a **date-sharded rollout resolver** (Codex shards by LOCAL date — `<CODEX_HOME|~/.codex>/sessions/YYYY/MM/DD/rollout-<ISO-ts>-<uuid>.jsonl`, the uuid IS the conversation id, so the Claude cwd-encoded glob does NOT apply: scan the start-day ±1, confirm each candidate's cwd by a head-scan of its `session_meta` line, pick by **ctime≈start identity** with a **newest-mtime-in-cwd fallback for a `resume`d** session whose rollout predates the process), and a rollout reader (`RolloutLine`/`payload` JSONL — model/effort/sandbox/approval from the first `turn_context`; title + human prompts from `event_msg`/`user_message`, skipping the AGENTS.md `response_item` blobs).

#### C2 — state via a rollout-tail PULL reconciler

- `ClassifyCodexLine` (pure: an `event_msg` payload → a turn-boundary verdict) + `ReadCodexStateDelta` (a byte-cursor forward delta over the rollout — first-sight tail-seek of the last 1 MiB, a 4 MiB catch-up cap, a partial trailing line left unconsumed, sticky on a quiet read) derive **Idle / Running / Waiting** from the transcript tail: `task_started` → Running; `task_complete` / `turn_aborted(interrupted)` / `thread_rolled_back` → Waiting; **last-boundary-wins** (timestamps are 100 % monotonic, file-order = truth).
- **NeedsApproval / Error are NOT PULL-derivable** — no approval/permission/error event exists in a rollout — so Codex runs on a **3-state floor** (Running / Waiting / Idle); the new `Activity.h` `CodexState` maps onto the board + tree state dot.

#### Managed lifecycle

- **Managed lifecycle — launch / restore / window-restore / adopt** (mirrors Claude — "do what we do there").
- A managed Codex is a real registry citizen via a **two-id model** (the §19-Q3 follow-on, resolved to the **first-class `AgentKind` path**, not a parallel one): `SessionInfo.id` = OUR minted durable handle (the registry / persistence / `_claudeTabs` key — Codex can't pin a session id at launch, so we never re-key), `SessionInfo.codexSessionId` = the real rollout uuid (the `codex resume` target + the "does a transcript exist" gate), filled by the observer's `_ReconcileManagedCodex` on the first prompt.
- `SessionInfo` gained **`kind`** (Claude default) + `codexSessionId`; `TabKind` gained **Codex** (the window-record tab refs + persistence (de)serialize it).
- **Launch** — the primary launch bar carries a **Claude⇄Codex toggle** (the scope/sort/autorunner toggle idiom; Codex is *directory-only* — no typed-id resume/fork, button reads "Launch Codex"), and the EXTERNAL menu's **Open New Codex Session Here** spawns one in a running codex's cwd → `_SpawnCodexSession` → `_LaunchCodexSession` (`BuildCodexCommandline` = `<codex>` / `<codex> resume <uuid>` / `<codex> fork <uuid>`, where `<codex>` is the launcher resolved ONCE at engine init to a **full path** (`ResolveCodexLauncher` → `Engine::codexExePath`) and run by path — a `.exe` quoted, a `.cmd`/`.bat` via `cmd /c` — because ConPTY's `CreateProcessW` appends only `.exe` and IGNORES `PATHEXT`, so a bare `codex` token would miss an npm `codex.cmd` and die `0x80070002` (the same trap Claude's native-exe policy fixed; Codex is NOT exe-only — the observer finds `codex.exe` as a DESCENDANT, so a `.cmd` re-exec is fine, and an empty launcher falls back to the bare `codex` token + the surfaced error); immediate managed card, `AM_SESSION` stamp, no `CCMGR_*`).
- **Restore** — the resume seam branches on `kind` (`_RestoreArchivedSession`, now reached from the Sessions page's "Resume here", → `_LaunchCodexSession`, transcript-gated on the rollout existing, else fresh — Rule #6).
- **Window-restore** — `_RestoreWindowTabs` re-homes a Codex tab ref via `_LaunchCodexSession`.
- **Adopt** — the EXTERNAL menu's Adopt brings an external codex's rollout under management via a **Fork-a-copy vs Resume-anyway** choice (`_AdoptExternalCodex(pid,cwd,fork)` behind `_ConfirmChoice`): fork = `codex fork <uuid>` into a NEW rollout — safe while the original is still running (no two-writers hazard) — else `codex resume <uuid>` (take-over); original left running.
- A managed Codex reads distinct everywhere via a teal **`codex` pill** (board card + tree row, the same teal as the External pill).
- **Lifecycle + state ONLY** — NO stdin injector / Prompt Queue Runner / Send-now (driving the Codex TUI is C4); a managed codex's `autorunner.mode` is Off.
- The observer still **never** feeds a codex to `ObserveClaude` (the External census stays observe-only); a MANAGED codex is registered by the launch path and reconciled (state + `codexSessionId` + `tabToken`) by the UI-lane `_ReconcileManagedCodex`, and is **deduped out of the External census** (`managedCodexTokens`) so it shows once.
- **Deferred:** **C3** = low-latency PUSH via Codex `notify` / `~/.codex/hooks.json` (a GLOBAL config mutation — a product decision, no per-session `--settings` like Claude); **C4** = bind a stdin injector + Prompt Queue Runner (drive the Codex TUI; launch is PULL-correlated, resume=`codex resume <id>`).

### Archive removed — Favorite + Close

- **Archive is REMOVED — replaced by Favorite + Close; the Sessions browser is the SOLE history view** ([`FAVORITES.md`](doc/agentmaster/FAVORITES.md)).
- The full-window Archive *page* (`TerminalPage.AgentArchivePage.cpp` — deleted), the **Archived (N)** toolbar button, the retired in-content archive overlay, and the Archive page's per-window "Reopen window" + synthetic "Saved window" rows are ALL gone.
- A managed tab's lifecycle verbs are just **Close** and **Favorite**: **Close** *always archives* (keeps the `live=false` record so the session stays resumable from Sessions — it NEVER deletes; the confirm is **Close · ★ Favorite & Close · Cancel**, the batch close **Close All · ★ Favorite & Close All · Cancel All** — **★ Favorite & Close** (dialog `Secondary`) stars the session(s) via `SetSessionFavorite` before archiving, so a keep-this close is one gesture; closing NEVER auto-favorites).
- On the **single-tab** close confirm the `Secondary` button **flips sense on the session's current star** (`IsSessionFavorite`): an already-favorite session offers **☆ Unfavorite & Close** instead (the hollow ☆ anti-star — the Sessions ★ column's own unfavorited glyph — `SetSessionFavorite(id,false)`), since re-favoriting a starred session would be a no-op.
- **Favorite** (a hollow ☆ / filled-yellow ★) is the new "keep/find this" marker, persisted via the **SessionStore `favorite` key** (the durable per-session *title* store, reused — survives Close, works for never-managed on-disk sessions, one sparse scan; `IsSessionFavorite` / `SetSessionFavorite` / `LoadAllFavoriteSessions`).
- It surfaces as a **leftmost ★ column** + a **`[ ] Favorite` filter** + a row "Favorite/Unfavorite" menu on the Sessions page, and a **Favorite/Unfavorite** item on a managed Claude tab's right-click menu (beside Close).
- The removal lost NO capability: the Sessions page's "Resume here" rehydrates a closed session's Prompt Queue via `_RestoreArchivedSession` (it subsumes the old Archive "Restore here").
- **Reopen window is unchanged** — the toolbar **"Reopen Windows (N)"** button + the `wt -w -1 -s <idx>` mechanism stay.
- A transcript that's gone (rare — Claude seldom sweeps) simply isn't listed (no broken Resume — "not found => don't show").
- `_RemoveSessionRecord` survives only as the internal resume-fresh stale-drop (no longer auto-hides).
- Detail: *C1 UI* + the lifecycle bullet under *Persistence*.

### The Sessions browser

- **The Sessions browser ([`SESSIONS.md`](doc/agentmaster/SESSIONS.md)) is the SOLE history view** (the Archive page is gone — FAVORITES.md).
- A **"Sessions"** toolbar button (the toolbar's rightmost, after Pause Prompt Queue Runner) opens a full-window page (the deferred-pointer-handler discipline the Archive page pioneered) listing **EVERY on-disk Claude Code session** (`~/.claude/projects/*/<uuid>.jsonl` — not just managed ones) in a selectable window: the `[1 month]` button click-cycles 1d/3d/7d/14d/1mo/3mo, hover opens a **From/To range popup** (plain text boxes — islands-safe).
- It carries a **leftmost ★ favorite column** (hollow ☆ / filled-yellow ★, click toggles the durable SessionStore star) + a **`[ ] Favorite` filter** + a row "Favorite/Unfavorite" menu (FAVORITES.md).
- The search bar `[ search ] (👤)(🤖)(📁)(📄)(🏷)(F) [☐ Open] [☐ Hidden] [☐ Favorite] [1 month] [↻]` runs **two-phase**: FAST = in-memory over per-session **sidecar indexes** (`~/.agentmaster/sessions-index/<sid>.json` — `(size,mtime)`-invalidated, **incrementally** re-accumulated from the stored byte offset; built by `TranscriptStore`) + the **`history.jsonl` accelerator**; SLOW = **ripgrep**-prefiltered transcript content (`rg -il`, PATH-resolved, batched under the cmdline cap, full in-process fallback), every match **scope-attributed in-process** (👤 typed prompts vs 🤖 assistant text/thinking + tool inputs/results — rg can't tell them apart, `ClassifyTranscriptLine` can), generation-cancelled on re-type.
- Both message scopes OFF ⇒ title + directory + the sidecar's cached first prompt (message *bodies* still need a 👤/🤖 scope); 📁/📄 match the **directories/files a session's tool calls touched** (`TranscriptStats::pathsAccessed`) and **default ON** (fast-phase-only — in-memory over the sidecar, no rg/transcript IO; 👤 also **default ON** — it flips on the SLOW content scan for your own typed prompts by default; 🤖/(F) default OFF — 🤖 also flips on the SLOW content scan); (F) fuzzy has identical rg/in-process semantics (`BuildSearchRegex`/`MatchesQueryText`, tested as a pair).
- The query parses into **whitespace-split AND terms** (`ParseSessionQuery` — every term must hit, each may hit a different field): `"quoted phrase"` = ONE **exact** contiguous term ((F) never applies inside quotes), and a bare **whole session-id GUID** token ({braces} tolerated, never fuzzied) also matches the session's **identity** — its id + fork-parent id — so a pasted id finds the session and its forks; in the content phases a guid term **scopes** hits to that session, and a guid-only query is answered by the fast phase alone.
- **When a search is active the rows are RANKED IN RELEVANCE TIERS** (`_RenderSessionsTable`, reusing the fast phase's `ParseSessionQuery`/`MatchesQueryText`/ guid-identity primitives over the name fields): **tier 0** a NAME/identity match (the displayed title, branch, or a pasted id) > **tier 1** a dir/path-only match (cwd or a tool-touched path) > **tier 2** content-only (the slow phase), with the chosen **column sort applied WITHIN each tier**.
- So the session you searched BY NAME lands on top instead of being buried under a recently-active *incidental* content match — the default sort is Active-desc.
- Rows carry fork-aware **created** (a fork duplicates its parent's lines verbatim with `forkedFrom` stamps — file birth is the truth), **line-derived last-activity** (file mtime lies: measured median ~1 h, max ~43 days — SESSIONS.md §5), the title precedence `customTitle > aiTitle > legacy summary > first REAL prompt`, the msgs·tools weight, and the session's **per-dir tab color** as a chip (solid = OPEN here, dim = on-disk) with a **presence ring** when claude's own heartbeat reports busy/idle/ waiting.
- Detail = metadata + scope-tagged match snippets + the numbered prompt list (off-thread, (id,mtime)-cached); actions: **Jump** (OPEN here), **Resume here** (`_ResumeSessionFromDisk`: an unknown sid gets a minimal archived-shaped record, then the SAME transcript-gated `--resume` seam — title pinning, dir color, hook correlation all reused).
- **Resume / Fork / window-restore open EXACTLY the picked/recorded session id — no "continuation tail" redirect.**
- There is NO solid on-disk signal for a `/clear`/`/compact`/plan SUCCESSOR: a `/compact` is **in-place** (one file, a `system/compact_boundary` line; it never mints a new id), a `/clear` leaves **no** link to its successor, and a plan-restart child references its **parent** (backward), never the parent its successor. (Agentmaster RECORDS the `/clear` and `/handover` links itself — `SessionInfo::continuedFromId`, below.)
- **The summary's WHOLE-CONVERSATION view follows every solid link BACKWARD only** (TAB_OVERLAY.md §13e): a fork's parent (copied uuids), a plan-restart parent (the explicit `"read the full transcript at: <parent>.jsonl"` reference), and the two links no transcript carries, recorded on the session (`SessionInfo::continuedFromId`/`continuedVia`, persisted) — a `/clear` predecessor (`SessionRegistry::OnHookEvent` pairs `SessionStart(source "clear")` with the `SessionEnd(reason "clear")` that just arrived on the same ConPTY; HOOKS.md) and a `/handover` origin (`_HandleCommandHandover`; COMMANDS.md). `TranscriptTurns.h` `CollectConversationUpstream` walks the chain (cached process-wide); the old `CollectConversationLineage` (plan parent only) stays for the CLI but the summary no longer calls it.
- **Fork here** (`_ForkSessionFromDisk` — the duplicate-tab fork's recipe: `claude --resume <parent> --fork-session --session-id <new>`, the new id minted by us so hooks/registry correlate from the first event; offered on EVERY row **including a LIVE one** — a fork writes its OWN transcript, so the adopt path's two-writers hazard doesn't apply; transcript-gated → fresh; titled via `DeriveForkTitle` (`"<title> (fork)"`, then `(fork 2)`/`(fork 3)`/… on a fork-of-a-fork, never stacked `(fork) (fork)`), logged `[sessions-page->fork]`), **Open New Session Here**, an **Edit Title** (rename in place), a right-click **Filter ▸** submenu, and a right-click **Hide from list** (`_HideSessionFromList` → `_AddSessionIdToHiddenList` → `AppSettings.hiddenSessionIds`, persisted + filtered out of the browser; cleared from the cog's **Reset hidden sessions**); double-click = resume.
- **Edit Title — rename a session IN PLACE** (right-click **Edit Title** OR a **"slow double-click"**: a re-click of the already-selected row, the Windows-Explorer rename gesture, disambiguated from a fast double-click=resume by an OS-`GetDoubleClickTime` arm timer a `DoubleTapped` disarms).
- It swaps the row's Title cell for a focused in-place `TextBox` (`_BeginSessionsRename`; a `ContentDialog` text box gets no keypresses under XAML Islands, so editing is inline — the Manager's Explorer-tree rename idiom), Enter / focus-loss commits + Escape cancels (both deferred so the re-render can't tear the box out; a blank edit keeps the old title — never empty, Rule #11).
- The title is **persisted durably** (`_PersistEditedSessionTitle`): a registry-known session (open OR archived) routes through `_RenameClaudeSession` (registry + the WT tab if open + the Engine observer mirrors it to the **SessionStore** `title` key AND `sessions.json` — the title is ONE value), a pure on-disk session writes the SessionStore directly (`SetStoredSessionTitle`, the overlay `LoadAllStoredSessionTitles` reads for closed rows); the edit reflects instantly into the in-memory rows + 🏷 search index (no re-gather) and survives across windows/runs.
- The **Filter ▸** submenu (`_SessionsRowFilterState` / `_SessionsRowFilterKind`, SESSIONS.md §1) narrows the list to sessions **like the clicked row** — **By Same Directory** (filesystem-aware `NormDirKey`), **By Same Branch** (exact; only when the row has one), **By Same Day / Week / Month** (the row's **created** time bucketed in **local** time, DST-safe `[start,end)` via `SessLocalBucket`; week is Monday-start), and **By Fork Family** (the anchor's connected fork-graph component, when it has lineage).
- Facets **stack across dimensions as AND**, the time dimension holds **one granularity at a time**, and a facet the anchor row already matches reads **✓** and toggles **OFF** on re-click.
- It is applied at the SAME render chokepoint (`_SessionsRowPassesRowFilter` in `_RenderSessionsTable`), so it composes (**AND**) with the search text + the scope/Open/Hidden toggles; a dismissible **`✕ filter: …`** chip beside the search box (`_sessFilterChip`) shows + clears the active facets (the submenu also has **Clear filters**), the count line notes `· filtered`.
- Pure browse-state — nothing persisted, the transcript untouched (it resets on restart, unlike `hiddenSessionIds`).
- **The FOLDER FILTER** (2026-10-12; in dev since 0.0.1.6 and production since 0.6.8.20; [`SESSIONS.md`](doc/agentmaster/SESSIONS.md) §10): **`📂 Folders ▾`** in the filters row (after Favorite) opens a dropdown of the folders the listed sessions worked in — each session's **launch** folder AND its **inferred** folder — split **50% / 50%**: **RECENT** (the folder whose newest session was active last, first) over **MOST USED** (the folder more sessions worked in, first), both over the page's gathered window; a find box (Enter adds the top RECENT match, or a typed absolute path as is), the picks as `name ✕` chips, **Include subfolders** (segment-boundary subtree match; default OFF = exact, like the counts), **Clear**.
  - The picks combine with **OR** (`_SessionsRowFilterState::folders` → `SessionInPickedFolders`, the one facet that ORs); the folder facet ANDs with everything else and rides the `✕ filter` chip / "Clear filters" / `· filtered`.
  - Every row's inferred folder comes from a **follow-up off-thread pass** (`_InferSessionsRowDirs`: the tab colors' rule — temp excluded, `InferWorkingDirectory`, the git arm per `inferGitRoot` through `GitRootMemo`, an ancestor-memoized `FindGitRootForDir`), cached per session by the transcript's (size, mtime) + the git setting, applied only to the gather that asked (`_sessionsGatherGen`). ⚠ Never move it into the gather: cold `.git` probes measured **6.6 s** for one month of sessions.
  - Rows and the detail pane show `Works in → <inferred>`. Pure half: `AgentMaster/SessionFolderFilter.h` (`TestSessionFolderFilter`); one `[sessions-folders]` hooks.log line per pass.
- "Hide from list" is **manual** — `_RemoveSessionRecord` does not auto-hide (Close keeps every session, never deletes — FAVORITES.md).
- A search-bar **"Hidden" checkbox** (`_sessHiddenBtn`, default OFF, beside "Open") **reveals** the hidden set — shown dimmed, with the row menu's **"Unhide"** (`_UnhideSessionFromList`) replacing "Hide from list" — so a manually-hidden session is findable + resumable without clearing the whole set from the cog.
- **Presence integration (§7-Q5's separation):** `TranscriptStore::ReadSessionPresence` owns the raw `~/.claude/sessions/<pid>.json` read; the **observer** validates rows against its process snapshot (stale/PID-reuse dropped) and publishes a `Presence()` table + the transient `SessionInfo.presenceStatus` fact through `ObserveClaude` (**never** `SessionState` — Rule #13).
- `ReadTranscriptInfo` honors `ai-title`/legacy `summary` + skips sidechain/compact-summary lines, and the shared **noise filter** (`IsNoiseUserPrompt`) keeps interrupt markers / command echoes / task notifications out of titles, prompt lists, and the scanner's Prompt Queue back-fill.
- **The full-window Sessions page shares generic chrome:** a window-level **overlay registry** — each page `_RegisterAgentPageOverlay`s its host + atomic visibility mirror + an optional dismiss hook (the Sessions page closes its range Popup there: popups render in the popup ROOT, a collapsed host does NOT hide them) and the tab-switch seam (`TabManagement.cpp`) calls `_DismissAgentPageOverlays()` — every page closes on tab switch with no page named there, so a future page binds automatically by registering.
- **Up/Down navigate the visible (sorted + filtered) rows** — nothing selected ⇒ Down picks the FIRST row, Up the LAST; then ±1 **wrapping** at the ends — via `PreviewKeyDown` on the page host (tunneling, so it beats the focused search box), deferred to a clean tick, recolor-only highlight (`_UpdateArchive/SessionsSelectionHighlight`) + `StartBringIntoView`; the page **focuses its search box on show** (keyboard events only route through the page when focus is INSIDE it — typing filters immediately, arrows work from the first keystroke).

### The agent harness + the Sessions page's AI search — written 2026-10-10; in the v0.7.9 pre-release (2026-10-11); production 0.6.8.19 and dev 0.0.1.6 hold it with the permission policy of 2026-10-12; the system prompt's first-step rule of 2026-10-13 in production since 0.6.8.20 (dev's prompt was rewritten by §9.14's three-part prompt in 0.0.1.28)

- **What it is.** The Sessions page has an **AI** toggle INSIDE the search box, at its right end, like the regex toggle of an editor's find box (flat while off, accent-filled while on; the user's request of 2026-10-10). With it on, the box is a request line ("the session where I fixed the tab drag crash", Enter). A hosted **Sonnet 5.5** (thinking off, low effort) understands the request, searches with the page's own search, and puts the sessions that fit in the table, best match first, with a one-sentence answer in a strip under the header. A follow-up refines the list. The crossed circle (⊘) right of the box throws the conversation away. Details: [`SESSIONS.md`](doc/agentmaster/SESSIONS.md) §9.
  - The toggle is not a part of the `TextBox`: it is a sibling in the box's grid cell, drawn over the box's right end. `_SessionsAiFitToggleIntoBox` grows the box's right padding by 36 DIP and shifts the box's own clear (✕) button — the template part `DeleteButton` — left of the toggle with a render transform. ⚠ Any new control placed inside a `TextBox` this way must do both, or its text and its ✕ run under the control.
- **One conversation per process** (`SharedSessionSearchAgent`, `AgentMaster/SessionSearchAgent.{h,cpp}`): every window's Sessions page is a view of it. The page side is `TerminalPage.AgentSessionsAi.cpp`.
- **The engine is the agent harness** ([`SDK_HARNESS.md`](doc/agentmaster/SDK_HARNESS.md)): `HarnessConversation` (`AgentMaster/AgentHarness.{h,cpp}`) owns one windowless `claude.exe`, driven through the pinned SDK submodule `refs/claude-agent-sdk-cpp` (0xeb/claude-agent-sdk-cpp, MIT): stream-json in both directions, in-process MCP tool servers, no built-in tools, no user settings / hooks / `CLAUDE.md`, `--no-session-persistence` (the conversation never shows up among the sessions), auto-compaction at 500,000 tokens.
  - **Two tool servers** (`AgentMaster/SessionSearchTools.{h,cpp}`): `mcp__sessions__search` (changes nothing on the screen) and `mcp__display__show` (the one place where the screen changes).
  - **Read before show** (the user's choices of 2026-10-17; in dev since 0.0.1.20 and production since 0.6.8.21; [`SESSIONS.md`](doc/agentmaster/SESSIONS.md) §9.12): two more tools on the `sessions` server — `mcp__sessions__grep` (a text or regex in the conversations' full text, each match in context with who wrote it and when; `GrepTranscripts`, ripgrep-prefiltered on a required literal, 20 s budget) and `mcp__sessions__read` (up to 8 sessions as compact transcripts, a long one cut to its first and last turns) — and a `why` per id on `show`. Rules 4–6 of the (now nine-rule) prompt: read every candidate, one line of evidence each, a verdict sentence that never names a screen. The page draws each `why` as a quote line under the row's title; the Telegram Search topic shows one evidence card per session (`MESSAGING.md` §21.10, D35).
  - **Windows and tabs, and a prompt that explains itself** (the user's request of 2026-10-17; in DEV since 0.0.1.28, 2026-10-18 00:06, and in dev 0.0.1.29 since 02:27 — both built by another session, from 2404f7838 and ac4167e63 — not in production, not used live yet; [`SESSIONS.md`](doc/agentmaster/SESSIONS.md) §9.14): a third tool server `tabs` in BOTH conversations (the page's and the Telegram Search topic's) — `mcp__tabs__list` (every window: number, short id, `last_used`, `asked_from`; every tab: pos, title, session id, state, `dormant`, `dir`, `works_in`, `branch`, `last_turn`, `active`, `selected`), `mcp__tabs__resume` / `mcp__tabs__fork` (a session as a tab of a named window, right after a chosen tab; a fork defaults to right after its source, the tab menu's Fork session; background unless `focus`) and `mcp__tabs__deactivate` (ONE tab, the tab menu's Deactivate Tab). Guards IN CODE (bypass permissions refuse nothing): with more than one window and no `window`, resume / fork REFUSE and describe every window, so the model must ask the user; at most 5 opens and 1 deactivate per request; deactivate refuses an adopted tab and a running / needs-approval one without `cut_off_turn:true`. The calls reach the windows through ANSWERED per-window agent-tool sinks (`Engine.h` `RegisterWindowAgentToolHandler` / `CollectWindowCensus` / `RunWindowTool` / `RunWindowToolOnHost`, `WindowTabTools.h`, `TerminalPage::_RunAgentWindowTool`); `_ResumeSessionFromDisk` / `_ForkSessionFromDisk` / `_RestoreArchivedSession` / `_ForkManagedSessionById` gained an insert slot and report the opened id. The system prompt was rewritten into `# Instructional Behaviors` (10) / `## Tools` / `## Workflow Guidelines` (19, numbered), composed from shared pieces for both modes: the model opens every request with an "I'll 1. ... 2. ... 3." plan (in the same message as its first calls) and ends with a descriptive reply and the likely next step, instead of one sentence; the plan is the request's NARRATION (`SessionSearchSnapshot::narration`), shown in the page's strip while it works and above the Telegram answer. Tests: `tests_tabtools.cpp` (`TestTabTools`). **`## Tools` teaches how each tool MATCHES** (the user's review of 2026-10-18; in DEV 0.0.1.29 since 2026-10-18 02:27, its ac4167e63 build holding 069d6a37a; not in production; SESSIONS.md §9.14 "What ## Tools teaches"): any case, a word inside longer words (one stem covers its forms; other words, Claude's terms and the other language are separate calls of one step), all of a query's words in ONE kind of place (so a folder goes in `dir`; on the page the branch is only a filter), "Patterns for grep" (one regex for every form — `drag(ged|ging|s)?`, `tab[ _-]?drag` — with a plain piece of 3+ letters OUTSIDE every group, or the page reads every session and the database refuses), and each tool's traps; `TestToolsTeaching` checks the prompt's own examples against `GrepRequiredLiteral` / `SearchIndexFast`.
  - **The system prompt's first rule** (the user's request of 2026-10-13; in production since 0.6.8.20; in dev until 0.0.1.28 rewrote the prompt, §9.14; [`SESSIONS.md`](doc/agentmaster/SESSIONS.md) §9.3): the model's first step mentions which kinds of tool calls it can use for the request, out of everything the two tools can do, and how it will use them in different ways, in the same message as its first calls. No extra model request: measured, 14 of 14 requests started with it, and a first request's first call came ~0.4–0.5 s later. The page does not show it (the strip shows the final sentence); the record and the live test's trace keep it. ⚠ It made the replies wordier, so the reply rule now says exactly one sentence, that the mention is not the reply, and shows an example. The m5 suite pins the rule and the count of rules; the redesign's proposed prompt has the same rule ([`SESSIONS_AGENT.md`](doc/agentmaster/SESSIONS_AGENT.md) §8.6).
  - **The thinking level** (the user's request of 2026-10-14; in dev and production; [`SESSIONS.md`](doc/agentmaster/SESSIONS.md) §9.1, [`SDK_HARNESS.md`](doc/agentmaster/SDK_HARNESS.md) §5.3): the strip's right end reads `thinking off  ctx 15k / 200k`, and a click on `thinking off` rotates **off → low → medium → high → xhigh → max** (adaptive thinking at that EFFORT — the effort is how Sonnet 5.5 defines the thinking level; off = no thinking at effort low, the old fixed setting). ⚠ On Sonnet 5.5 "off" is the API's own `{"thinking": {"type": "between_tools"}}`, which the harness puts into the request body itself (`CLAUDE_CODE_EXTRA_BODY`, changed live): Claude Code sends `disabled` only to the older models it lists and LEAVES THE FIELD OUT for every other one, and the API then thinks by default — so the old fixed "thinking off" never held (`SDK_HARNESS.md` §5.3, `HarnessModelRefusesDisabledThinking`). GLOBAL + persisted: `AppSettings::sessionsAiThinkingLevel`, written by the toggle's own freshest-disk RMW, preserved in both cog-Save blocks, read once by `SharedSessionSearchAgent()`; a click sets the agent, whose event repaints every window. A level the model lacks is skipped (the `initialize` answer's `supportedEffortLevels`; Sonnet 5.5 has all five).
    - **Applied LIVE before the next request** (`HarnessConversation::SetThinking` → `ApplyThinkingLive`): `set_max_thinking_tokens` 0 / null, `apply_flag_settings {env}` for the between_tools request body (the COMPLETE env object: the request merges shallow, `BuildHarnessEnvPatchJson`) and `apply_flag_settings {effortLevel}`, then `get_settings` for the effort in effect (`Inventory().effort`; `Inventory().thinking` reads `adaptive` / `between_tools` / `disabled`), sent through the harness's own transport tap (`WatchedTransport::Request`, since the SDK's control protocol is private). ⚠ Order: toward off, thinking 0 FIRST and the body after; toward on, the body cleared FIRST — the API refuses between_tools next to Claude Code's adaptive request (its `clear_thinking` strategy). The conversation keeps its process and its context; a refused request falls back to a restart with the digest. Measured (live test, 76/76): off at effort high 0 thinking blocks, adaptive at max 1 block (~290 tokens), off again 0, one process.
    - ⚠ **Every harness process now starts `--thinking adaptive`**, also when thinking is wanted off (switched off before the first message): Claude Code returns to an EXPLICIT start config on `null`, and after `--thinking disabled` there is no way back. ⚠ **The harness never sets `CLAUDE_CODE_EFFORT_LEVEL`**: it overrides every live effort change while Claude Code answers "success" (measured). It OWNS `CLAUDE_CODE_EXTRA_BODY` (always in its `--settings` env, removed from the inherited environment with the other thinking variables). `HarnessEvent::thinkingBlocks` + `thinkingTokens` (Claude Code's `system/thinking_tokens` estimate) are the per-turn evidence.
  - **Persistence is Agentmaster's own**: `<profile>\harness\<name>.json` (the record) and `<name>.view.json` (the shown list). A new process (after a restart or a park) gets a digest of the record.
  - **Park:** an idle `claude.exe` holds about 400 MB. When no page shows the AI search, the agent ends its process 10 minutes later; the conversation continues from the digest. (Until 2026-10-18 it also freed its session index at once; the index is now the process's shared one and is never freed — §9.15.)
- **A harness process ALWAYS runs with bypass permissions, and in auto mode when bypass is not allowed** (the user's rule, 2026-10-12; in production 0.6.8.19 and dev 0.0.1.6; [`SDK_HARNESS.md`](doc/agentmaster/SDK_HARNESS.md) §5.1). The harness adds `dontAsk` for the case that auto is not allowed either. A windowless process must never wait for a person's answer.
  - **Before:** every harness process ran in `dontAsk` (a tool that was not on the allowed list was refused).
  - **The launch** passes `--permission-mode bypassPermissions` and `--dangerously-skip-permissions`. The `--allowedTools` list stays: bypass does not read it, but in `auto` it removes the classifier request (0.3 to 0.6 s per tool call, measured) and in `dontAsk` it is what lets a tool run.
  - **Claude Code lowers a mode it does not allow WITHOUT a word.** With a policy that switches bypass off it starts in `auto`; with auto switched off too, in `default`. The one sign is what the process reports: `current_permission_mode` in its answer to `initialize` (before any model request) and `permissionMode` in each turn's `system/init`. `EnsureProcess` reads the first, `OnSystemMessage` the second; `SettlePermissionMode` then sends `set_permission_mode` (`auto`, then `dontAsk`) when a mode that can ask is in effect.
  - **A start that Claude Code refuses with an error** (`--restricted`, or a version that does not know a mode word) is done again with the next lower mode, at most twice; the lower mode is then kept for that conversation object's life (`HarnessStartFailureNamesPermissionMode`).
  - **Check a conversation with `HarnessConversation::Inventory()`**, the process's OWN report: the permission mode in effect, and after the first turn every tool, tool server, slash command, skill, agent type and plugin its model has. hooks.log has one `[harness] <name>: permission mode …` line per process start.
  - ⚠ **With bypass, nothing refuses a tool call.** What the model can do is decided by the tools it is given (`--tools ""`, the tool servers). Give a harness conversation a tool only when it may use that tool without limit.
  - **The model gets the caller's tools and nothing else.** The SDK's MCP server announces a `resources` capability that it does not have, and Claude Code then adds three MCP-resource tools to the model's list (769 tokens per request; callable under bypass). The harness removes the capability from the server's `initialize` answer.
- **Searches never miss** (the user's rule of 2026-10-18, NOT BUILT yet; [`SESSIONS.md`](doc/agentmaster/SESSIONS.md) §9.15). Made and checked with a PROMPT EVAL: five queries of five kinds plus four held out, each in a fresh conversation on Sonnet 5.5 at thinking medium (`harness_live.exe --eval`), answer keys built from the transcripts on disk, every tool call read each round. Mean recall went from 55.3% (the app as it was) to 100% on all nine, in the last three runs and in a final run on the BUILT-IN prompt, precision 100% but two labeled "weaker match" extras. A tenth query, q6 "find betterclipboard session" (the dev bot's empty first answer of 2026-10-18 12:36 — DEV 0.0.1.29 searched an index of ONE session), was added after the tuning: 100% in 7 of 7 runs, and the final main and held-out rounds (every fix, the built-in prompt) scored 100% recall and precision on all ten. The kit for the next prompt change: `tools/ai-search-eval/` (queries, scorer, and `pieces.txt` — the prompt's SOURCE, from which `gen_prompt.py` generates the `AM_PROMPT_*` macros; edit the pieces, never the macros by hand); the answer keys name real sessions and stay out of the repository.
  - **One session index per process** (`SessionCatalogKeeper`, `SharedSessionCatalogKeeper()` in `SessionSearchAgent.h`): read by the page's and the Telegram Search topic's conversations, built for EVERY session at the bot's start, never freed by a park or a page hide. A tool call waits up to 90 s (`kCatalogSearchWaitMs`) for an index that is complete, fresh and carries the session store; over an index still being read it searches NOTHING and the model is told to call again — never a part.
  - **Every session by default** (`kSessionSearchDefaultDays` 0, search and grep); a period OVERLAPS a session's activity, and a dated row says `in_period` (its own messages inside the period, `ReadPeriodActivity`); search 30 / 200 rows with `more`; grep 20 / 100 with `also` (150 one-liners with the folder) and `folders` (the matches per folder); show 300 ids; the Telegram answer sends the rows past its 10 cards as lines (`MESSAGING.md` §21.20).
  - **grep** narrows every alternative by its own literal (`GrepRequiredLiterals`; an optional spelling — `[ _-]?`, `colou?r`, `(ed|ing)?` — multiplies into its spellings, up to 32, which the line reader checks through the text they share, and a key holding another is dropped), runs ONE ripgrep over the projects folder (its own 120 s bound, `kRgTreeTimeoutMs`: with the transcripts out of the file cache it took 37–52 s and its old 30 s timeout made the grep read nothing; a run of 5 s or more, or a failed one, logs `[grep]` in hooks.log), reads with several threads, never parses a line whose word is only in its `cwd`, starts its 20 s read budget after that ripgrep whether it filtered or failed, and has `in:edits` (the files Claude CHANGED). A shown session without a `why` gets the evidence its results had; grep rows carry `dir`; a touched-path search row carries the session's `first_prompt`. The deep search (and the page's slow content phase) got the same prefilter and threads: 79 s -> 7 s over every session, the same results.
  - **The Search Procedure** (`## The Search Procedure`, seven steps between the behaviors and `## Tools`; the introduction ranks completeness first, evidence second, speed third): read the facets; cast a wide net in the FIRST step; make every list complete before judging it (to narrow, REPEAT THE SAME CALL with dir or a period added, never fewer words); sort by evidence and never leave a candidate out on a guess; check; show every one in one call (never a sample of similar sessions; the user's words count in any form); reply. The pieces are generated from the winning prompt file, so the built-in prompt is the one that scored.
  - ⚠ **The dev bot understates search quality: the DEV install is a Debug build.** Measured 2026-10-18 with a Debug build of the harness: a transcript is read ~14× slower, and the grep's reader threads and the read tool's render threads gain nothing from running in parallel (the debug heap serializes their allocations) — a grep that reads its 363 candidates in 4.5 s optimized read 1 in its 20 s, and the dev bot's read of 8 sessions (0.6 s optimized) left 2–5 unread. Judge the search on an optimized build (production, or the eval's harness).
- **A search never reads the disk** (until 2026-10-18; superseded by the bullet above). An index keeper reads the session index in the background, in steps, newest sessions first. A search took the newest published step; when that did not reach back as far as the search asked, the result had an `indexing` field and the model said that older sessions were still being indexed.
  - ⚠ **A superseded request's QUEUED search runs in full** (seen 2026-10-13, NOT FIXED; [`SESSIONS.md`](doc/agentmaster/SESSIONS.md) §9.11). The handler takes the request generation when it STARTS, so a call that the SDK queued behind a slow call of the same step reads the next request's number and is never cancelled: a 29 s full-text scan held up a follow-up. The fix is in [`SESSIONS_AGENT.md`](doc/agentmaster/SESSIONS_AGENT.md) §7.6.
  - ⚠ **A request after a pause answers "nothing found, still being indexed"** (seen 2026-10-17 in the dev bot's Search topic, FIXED 2026-10-18 by the bullet above, NOT BUILT yet; [`SESSIONS.md`](doc/agentmaster/SESSIONS.md) §9.13). The bot's park after 10 idle minutes and the page's hide free the index (`ReleaseCatalog`); a rebuild from nothing takes 11–13 s in production and up to ~100 s in dev; a search waits ~2.4 s and then searches the 1–34 sessions of the keeper's first step; rule 9 tells the model not to search again. The proposed fix: one process-wide index that is never freed, and a search that waits for it.
- **The hidden process is not a session.** Its environment carries `AM_HARNESS=<name>` (and no `WT_SESSION` / `AM_SESSION`); the Fleet Observer's census and the CLI's skip such a process (`ClaudeProcessFacts::harnessName`).
- **How it is checked:** the app was built with the harness on 2026-10-11, locally in both configurations (staged on `Q:`, not swapped) and by the GitHub workflows: the **v0.7.9 pre-release** (tag at `b842fcf5a`) holds it. ⚠ That pre-release runs the harness in `dontAsk`, with five tools in the model's list; the permission rule and the two-tool list are in `ba11d315d`, which production 0.6.8.19 and dev 0.0.1.6 hold (checked 2026-10-13 with a string probe of their `TerminalApp.dll`). Every file changed since then passes `cl /Zs` with that build's flags; the m5 suite covers the model-free half (`tests_harness.cpp`); a live test (`tests\harness_live.cpp`, run by hand with `run-harness-live.bat`) passes 72 checks against the real `claude.exe` (63 in the harness and permission scenarios, 9 in the search agent's). Measured there: process ready in 0.7 s; from a cold process the list is on the screen 3.2 to 4.0 s after the request. `--agent "<request>"` prints HOW a request was answered: the command line, the system prompt, the tools as the model reads them, each tool call with its arguments and result, and the process's inventory. **The page's XAML was not run.**
- **A build needs** the submodule checked out (`git submodule update --init refs/claude-agent-sdk-cpp`; CI does it) and the vcpkg package `nlohmann-json` (in the manifest's `terminal` feature; vcpkg installs it at the next build).
- Logs: `[harness]`, `[harness-proc]`, `[ai-search]` and `[nav] sessions ai …` in hooks.log.

### The session database (DuckDB) — written 2026-10-15 (c0253b8b0 … 41fe5e493), NOT BUILT into the app

- **⚠ 2026-10-17: the database could never START on this machine** (the fix is in DEV since 0.0.1.28, 2026-10-18 00:06: dev's layouts now have `duckdb.dll`; dev's profile is still Off; [`SESSIONS_DB.md`](doc/agentmaster/SESSIONS_DB.md) status block): every loose layout (stock `bin\x64\<Config>` and every `Q:\agentmaster-layouts` slot) had `agentmaster-sessiondb.exe` WITHOUT `duckdb.dll` (the wapproj Content item never reached a registered build folder). Fixed by `CascadiaPackage.wapproj`'s `CopyAgentmasterDuckDbToLayout` (the images target's twin; `Swap-AgentmasterLayout.ps1` warns about a layout without it), and both AI search conversations take the database's tools only when the mode is On AND `SessionDbServiceInstalled` (else the page search, logged). The user asked to enable the database for BOTH conversations: the default stays Off; this machine's two profiles are switched On at the deploy of a build with the fix (not done yet).
- **⚠ 2026-10-16: the user's three requirements, which this build does NOT meet** ([`SESSIONS_DB.md`](doc/agentmaster/SESSIONS_DB.md) §17):
  - **DuckDB is the engine of BOTH the AI agent and the Sessions page's search.** Today: Off by default, and only the page's slow content phase uses it.
  - **The agent calls that code as a tool, with no separate program and no MCP server.** Today: every tool call crosses a named pipe into `agentmaster-sessiondb.exe`, which also carries a stdio MCP server.
  - **A build from scratch in < 30 s, then only the difference.** Today: 37 min; the incremental half holds.
  - **Measured** (§17.2, scripts `tools/sessiondb-bench/review_cold_build.py` + `bench_extract.cpp`): the existing C++ extractor parses the whole corpus (10.74 GB) in 16.4 s on 16 threads; DuckDB writes the search text in 13.5 s; a search by scan with no index takes 1.4 s over everything. DuckDB's own JSON functions are NOT usable for the cold build (out of memory at 8–16 threads, a torn line aborts the statement, `try()` crashed DuckDB 1.5.0).
  - **The recommended redesign** (§17.3): DuckDB inside the app, a parallel cold build of search rows only (no lossless line store, no inverted index, summaries formed on demand), search by scan. Decisions (a)–(e) of §17.4 are open.
- **What it is** ([`SESSIONS_DB.md`](doc/agentmaster/SESSIONS_DB.md), §16 for the build): one DuckDB file per profile (`<profile>\sessiondb\sessions.duckdb`) holding every Claude Code session on the machine — every transcript line verbatim (a transcript rebuilds byte for byte), ~110 facts per session, the text of every block, the touched paths, token usage, Agentmaster's own titles / tags / favorites / links — and our own full-text index (tiers name > path > prompt > text, BM25, snippets).
- **One process owns it**: `agentmaster-sessiondb.exe` (DuckDB 1.5.6, pinned by SHA-256 in `tools\Get-DuckDb.ps1` + `sessiondb\duckdb.props`), one per profile (a mutex), a named pipe (4-byte length + UTF-8 JSON), a query pool of 4 and one writer thread, deadlines (`sql` 2.5 s, the rest 10 s), exits 10 min after its last client. The GUI never loads `duckdb.dll`; it copies the service and the DLL out of the package into `<profile>\sessiondb\bin` and starts them there.
- **Off by default.** The Settings cog's SESSION DATABASE section: **Off / Shadow / On**, the refresh period (1–60 s, default 5), DuckDB's threads (4) and memory limit (1024 MB). Shadow builds it and runs the Sessions page's content search on it BESIDE today's, logging `[sessdb-diff]` in hooks.log; On makes the page use it and (from the next app start) gives the AI search its ten read tools as `mcp__agentmaster__*`.
- **Refresh (D16, decided):** the transcript watcher records which sessions changed; the engine's `SessionDbRefresher` thread sends them as one `upsertSessions` every tick, a `resync` sweep after a connect, a re-index, or every 30 s while the watch is unhealthy; Agentmaster's own facts when the store's facts epoch moves.
- **Consumers:** the Sessions page's slow content phase (`pageSearch`); the AI search on the database (`SessionSearchToolset` with `Callbacks::database`, `show` resolving short ids through `resolveIds`); the CLI verbs `agentmaster search <words>`, `agentmaster sql "<SELECT>"`, `agentmaster sessdb status|stop|resync|rebuild`; the stdio MCP server (`agentmaster-sessiondb.exe mcp`, register by hand: `claude mcp add --scope user agentmaster -- "<profile>\sessiondb\bin\agentmaster-sessiondb.exe" mcp`; the Settings button, D6, is not built).
- **Measured on the whole corpus** (3,595 sessions, 11,898 files, 11.3 GB): a cold build of 37 min; the file 6.9 GiB (3.5 GiB used: the hot tier's churn leaves free blocks); a warm search 0.3–0.7 s (5–100× today's slow phase); the summary equal to `AnalyzeSessionTranscript` field by field for 3,591 of 3,591 sessions (D14; `verify-summary`, the other 4 had grown since the ingest).
- **Logs:** `<profile>\sessiondb\sessiondb.log` (`[sessdb]`), hooks.log (`[sessdb]`, `[sessdb-diff]`, `[ai-search]`).

### The tab strip state dot

- **The tab strip carries the state dot.**
- **The side panel's cards carry the SAME icon** (SIDE_PANEL.md §2g, 2026-10-23, NOT BUILT yet): `AgentStatusGlyph` is the code twin of `HeaderAgentStatusDotWrap` — the dot, the 3 dots, the flash ring (one shared blink clock, so a card blinks with its tab) and the spark crown (the shared `AgentMakeCacheWarmGlow`), moving under the same rule (`AgentMaster/StatusIconMotion.h`). ⚠ A change to the wrap's elements or to `TabHeaderControl::_Update*Animation`'s gates must be made in the twin too.
- Every classified tab's header reads `[icon] ● <title>`: a state-colored **Ellipse** (thin black stroke for contrast; the dot-wrap margin is `0,1,6,-1`, and a `TabViewItemHeaderIconMargin` override `0,0,4,0` on the `TabView` in `TabRowControl.xaml` tightens the icon→dot gap) in `TabHeaderControl.xaml`'s indicator row right before the title — one more `x:Bind`'ed element over `TerminalTabStatus` (two new observable properties, `AgentStatusVisible`/`AgentStatusBrush`; `Tab.idl` already projects `TabStatus{get;}`, so no `Tab.{h,cpp}` changes — the page drives it idempotently via `_SetTabAgentDot(tab, color?)`).
- A **managed** session's tab wears its Triage-Board state color (Running blue · Waiting goldenrod · NeedsApproval orange-red · Error crimson · Done green · Idle gray — a MANAGED Codex tab wears the same dot at its 3-state floor: Running blue · Waiting goldenrod · Idle gray); an observed-but-unmanaged tab (pwsh / cmd / unprompted-claude / external codex) a **dim gray** dot (the Agent Manager is a page, not a tab — it has no dot).
- A **red-flash alert** rides the dot too: when a session leaves **Running** for a *needs-you* state — `Running → {Idle · WaitingForInput · NeedsApproval}` (NOT `→Done` / `→Error`) — on a tab that is **not the focused tab of the ACTIVE window** (the toasts' `_activated && tab == focused` rule: the selected tab of a BACKGROUND window rings too, and activating the window clears it — `_OnAgentWindowActivated`, NOTIFICATIONS.md §8.2), that tab's dot grows a **flash ring** (a separate Ellipse drawn behind the dot — ring · the dot's black stroke · its status fill, `TerminalTabStatus.AgentFlashRingVisible`; a ring, NOT a stroke-color change) until you switch to it.
- The ring's **color AND opacity are user-configurable** (the Settings cog's **TABS ▸ Status flashing color** picker — a `muxc::ColorPicker` with its alpha slider enabled — a GLOBAL `AppSettings::flashRingColor` stored `#AARRGGBB`, **default red at 80% opacity** `#CCFF0000`; painted via the per-window shared `_flashRingBrush` bound to `TerminalTabStatus.AgentFlashRingBrush`, applied live on Save + cross-window broadcast — `_RefreshFlashRingBrush`) (`_EvaluateAgentFlash` — the active tab counts as visited so it never flashes; visiting it clears the flash via `_VisitTabClearFlash` from `_OnTabSelectionChanged`).
- All flashing tabs blink in **lockstep**, **600 ms** on / 600 ms off, and a tab joining mid-cycle adopts the current phase; an archived (`!live`) session forgets its last state so a later background restore can't spuriously flash.
  - **How (since UI_THREAD.md §10i, 2026-10-04, NOT BUILT yet):** the page sets `AgentFlashRingVisible` ONCE per flash, and the tab header blinks the ring itself (`TabHeaderControl::_UpdateFlashRingAnimation` → `AgentLockstepBlink`). The blink is a composition expression on the ring's `Opacity` that follows ONE shared clock per UI thread, so the rings of every window blink together.
  - **Before:** one per-window `DispatcherTimer` toggled `_agentFlashPhase` and flipped every ring's `Visibility` every 600 ms, which cost a layout pass and a XAML frame per flip. The timer (`_OnAgentFlashTick`) still runs at 600 ms, but only re-asserts the rings (idempotent), prunes closed tabs and re-publishes the unread census.
  - ⚠ Never set the ring's `Opacity`: a static set cancels the blink, and it is how the blink ends.
- A **selection pill** rides the header too — a translucent accent pill behind the tab (`HeaderAgentSelectionPill`, driven by `TerminalTabStatus.AgentSelectionVisible`/ `AgentSelectionBrush` at ~40% alpha via `_SetTabSelectionPill`) shown while the **Agent Manager page is on screen** and this session is hovered/selected there: the tab-strip half of the Linked-Lenses selection sync.
- The **SPARK CROWN** rides the dot too — the tab-strip twin of the Triage-Board card's amber ⚡ "still server-cached" glyph, shown while the SAME pure `ServerCacheStillWarm` predicate holds (so the two surfaces can never disagree) — i.e. only while the session is **AT REST** (Waiting-for-you · Needs-approval · Error · Idle · Done; **never Running**, where the hint is both uninformative and permanently lit): a soft amber **glow** behind the dot (`HeaderCacheWarmGlow` — two concentric flat discs at different opacities in ONE Grid, faking a falloff because `RadialGradientBrush` appears nowhere in this codebase, and one Opacity animation then breathes the whole halo) plus three **embers** (composition visuals that `AgentEmberSparks` attaches to the `HeaderCacheWarmSparks` `Canvas`; XAML ellipses `HeaderCacheSpark0..2` until UI_THREAD.md §10c) that rise out of the dot and fade, staggered 380 ms. The status dot itself is **deliberately untouched**, so the Triage state color reads exactly as before — the reason this shape was chosen over a flame that replaces the dot.
- Drawn LAST (over the favorite crown, which owns the cell's top-left: the center ember would otherwise flicker *behind* it; over it they read as sparks coming off the crown), while the glow is drawn FIRST so the louder red flash ring is never tinted by a cosmetic hint.
- Each ember rests HIDDEN (opacity 0) and its rise is bounded to 3 DIP, so a stopped crown leaves no frozen spark and the visible arc stays inside the 18 px cell (the clip trap that once truncated the flash ring into a square).
- Color is a contrast-picked FIRE pair (`AgentStatusColors.h` `CacheWarmSparkColorFor` — bright amber `#FFC107` on a dark tab, deep ember `#B02A00` on a light one, off the tab's CURRENTLY RENDERED background) so the sparks never vanish on an amber/gold per-dir tab; unlike the pending-dots pair it is NOT cog-configurable.
- Driven by `TerminalPage::_ScanCacheWarmTabs` → `_SetTabCacheWarm` → `TerminalTabStatus.AgentCacheWarmVisible`/`AgentCacheWarmBrush`, **POLLED** on the scanner's ~2.5 s liveness tick rather than pushed: warmth BEGINS on an event but ENDS on a **clock** — nothing fires when the ~5 min window lapses — so a push-only indicator would stay lit forever on an abandoned tab.
- It is its own sweep (not a rider on `_ScanPendingInput`, whose early-outs are draft-specific) and the crown is started/stopped on the flag through the same one `TabStatus` subscription as the pending pulse, so a fleet whose caches are all cold animates nothing. Since RENDERING.md §4c it also runs only while the header is in the live tree and Windows' "Animation effects" is on (a still crown = the steady glow, embers hidden).
- Since UI_THREAD.md §10c (2026-10-04, NOT BUILT yet) the crown runs on the **Composition API**, not storyboards: a composition `Opacity` loop on the glow Grid (`AgentOpacityLoop`, begun and ended on its still 0.45) and the embers as composition visuals of our own (`AgentEmberSparks`). The embers copy `AgentCacheWarmBrush`'s color in the same `TabStatus` subscription (`_SyncCacheWarmEmberColor` — a composition visual cannot `x:Bind`). See the ambient-animation Gotcha.
- A **FAVORITE marker** also rides the dot (FAVORITES.md §5a) in one of **two user-selectable glyphs** (the Settings cog's **TABS ▸ Favorite marker** dropdown — a GLOBAL `AppSettings::favoriteIcon`, default **Crown**): **Crown** = a small **gold `Path` crown** (`#F5C242`, the Sessions ★ color) perched at the **north-west** of the status dot (peak tilted NW, `RotateTransform`), drawn as the wrap-Grid's last child so it sits ON the dot; **Star** = the state-colored status dot becomes the **foreground of a white, golden-tipped `Path` star** (a 5-point star drawn BEHIND the dot — white fill, gold `#F5C242` stroke whose miter tips read golden — so its points radiate around the dot).
- The one visible "keeper" marker on a LIVE session's tab (`HeaderAgentFavoriteCrown` / `HeaderAgentFavoriteStar`, bound to the **mutually exclusive** `TerminalTabStatus.AgentFavoriteVisible` (crown) / `AgentFavoriteStarVisible` (star), driven by `_SetTabAgentFavorite` (reads `favoriteIcon`, asserts exactly one) / `_RefreshTabFavoriteCrown` / `_RefreshAllFavoriteIcons` from the durable `IsSessionFavorite` at launch / bind / favorite-toggle, and re-asserted live on a Crown↔Star change via the cog Save + cross-window broadcast; same-window-instant, cross-window-on-next-bind).
- Deliberately NOT a title prefix — the one-title invariant (Rule #11: Explorer name == tab title == persisted title) must never carry presentation glyphs through renames/persistence.
- The state palette is shared through **`AgentStatusColors.h`** (`AgentStatusColorFor`): the **per-tab overlay** (`AgentTabOverlay`) + the **tab-strip dot** (`TerminalTabStatus`) read it (the overlay's hand-synced copy folded in — the third consumer, the tab dot, was the cue to factor it).
- **`AgentManagerContent.cpp` holds its own identical `StateColor` copy** for the board cards/columns, so a color change must be made in BOTH places.

### Taskbar unread count ([`NOTIFICATIONS.md`](doc/agentmaster/NOTIFICATIONS.md) §8) — built; in release 0.6.8.13 (2026-09-27), live behavior not yet verified

- A red disc with a white number on Agentmaster's **taskbar button** = the **unread sessions across every window** — exactly the tabs whose status dot carries the flash ring (automatic, or a manual Mark Unread); +1 per newly unread session, −1 as each is visited. Default ON, the cog's Notifications ▸ TASKBAR switch (`AppSettings::taskbarUnreadBadge`, independent of the toast master).
- **An `ITaskbarList3::SetOverlayIcon` overlay, not a `BadgeUpdateManager` badge**: badges need package identity (they throw `0x80070490` in every portable install), outlive the app on a pinned icon, and have an open XAML-desktop-window bug; the overlay works everywhere and rides WT's existing `IslandWindow::_taskbar`. The icon is rendered by the header-only `AgentMaster/TaskbarBadge.h`.
- **The count is PROCESS-WIDE**, because the taskbar combines our windows into ONE button whose overlay is "the last one received": each `TerminalPage` publishes its `_flashingSessions ∪ _manualUnreadSessions` (`_PublishAgentUnreadSessions`) to the engine's census (`Engine::PublishWindowUnreadSessions`, `[unread] N -> M`), which fans the union's size out to EVERY window's sink → `_ApplyTaskbarUnreadCount` → `TaskbarUnreadCountChanged` → `TerminalWindow` → `AppHost` → `IslandWindow::SetTaskbarBadge` (re-applied on `TaskbarButtonCreated`, re-rendered on `WM_DPICHANGED`). A closing window withdraws its set in `~TerminalPage`.
- It changed the flash ring's rule: the selected tab of a **background** window now rings (and counts) when it finishes, cleared by activating the window (§8.2).

### Taskbar preview frame ([`NOTIFICATIONS.md`](doc/agentmaster/NOTIFICATIONS.md) §9) — written 2026-10-06, NOT BUILT

- Hovering the combined taskbar button shows one preview per window; a window that holds an unread tab (its OWN `_publishedUnreadSessions` — the set the count sums) shows its preview inside a **frame** in the flash-ring color (alpha floored at 50%), in the flyout AND in Alt+Tab; the frame goes away when every tab of that window is read. PER WINDOW, unlike the count. Default ON, the cog's Notifications ▸ TASKBAR switch (`AppSettings::taskbarUnreadThumbnailFrame`).
- **The only per-window mark Windows has is the DWM "iconic" thumbnail** (`DWMWA_FORCE_ICONIC_REPRESENTATION` + `DWMWA_HAS_ICONIC_BITMAP`): the window hands DWM its own bitmap — a `PrintWindow(PW_CLIENTONLY | PW_RENDERFULLCONTENT)` capture with the frame painted on (`DwmSetIconicThumbnail`; the hover peek gets an unframed full-size capture). Flashing, the progress state and the overlay icon mark only the combined button (measured on a disposable Windows 11 25H2 desktop, `tools/taskbar-thumbnail-probe`).
- Pipeline: `_PublishAgentUnreadSessions` → `_ApplyTaskbarUnreadFrame` (raised only on a change; also from the cog Save + the broadcast) → `TaskbarUnreadFrameChanged` → `TerminalWindow` → `AppHost` → `IslandWindow::SetTaskbarUnreadFrame` (on: the two attributes + a 20 s timer + a refresh; off: clear them — the live preview is back). Captures run on a **thread-pool worker** (at most one per window, `JobBook`), never on the shared UI thread; refreshes on turn-on / recolor, deactivation, the 20 s timer (not while minimized), and a debounced resize / restore / DPI change. The pure pixel work is `AgentMaster/TaskbarThumbnail.h` (`TestTaskbarThumbnail`). Logs `[taskbar-frame]` (hooks.log, from the EXE).
- ⚠ Every refresh INVALIDATES and then STORES at once — see the Gotcha *DWM iconic thumbnails*.

### Release/dev separation + PROFILES

- The GitHub release now ships its OWN identity (`Package-Rel.appxmanifest`: `Agentmaster`/`agentmaster.exe`, selected by `/p:AgentmasterPackageIdentity=Release`) while the dev loose layout becomes **`AgentmasterDev`**/`agentmasterdev.exe`/"Agentmaster Dev" — so both install side by side; `GetWtExePath`-class launchers and the reopen dispatch (`_AgentmasterReopenTarget`) pick the alias by PFN prefix (Dev first).
- ALL persisted state — engine files AND Terminal's own settings.json/state.json (a `GetBaseSettingsPath` redirect to `<profile>\terminal\`, which now OUTRANKS upstream portable mode's `<exedir>\settings` — a portable's CHOSEN profile owns Terminal's settings too, seeded from a pre-choice `<unzip>\settings`) — lives in ONE **profile folder** per install: resolved env `AGENTMASTER_PROFILE` > the **exe-side `profile.path` POINTER** (one line naming the profile dir — RELATIVE when it sits inside the exe dir so a moved unzip keeps working, absolute otherwise; a PORTABLE copy's per-unzip memory, honored for installed copies too when planted) > `.portable` marker fallback (`<exedir>\profile` — an un-chosen/headless portable; true-portable zips pass `-PortableMode`; a portable NEVER reads the home map — its shared `Unpackaged` slot can't tell two unzips apart) > the per-install slot in `~/.agentmaster.profiles` > per-identity default (`~/.agentmaster` release+unpackaged / `~/.agentmaster-dev` dev).
- An INSTALLED copy's **first launch AUTO-SELECTS the per-identity default WITHOUT prompting** — release → **Default** (`~/.agentmaster`), dev → **Development** (`~/.agentmaster-dev`) — and persists it (`EnsureProfileResolvedAtStartup` → `DefaultProfileDir()` + `SaveChoice` + `SeedTerminalSettings`), running from `WindowEmperor::HandleCommandlineArgs` AFTER the single-instance handoff and BEFORE any state read (no UI, so a `-Embedding` defterm activation takes the same path); a **PORTABLE copy's first launch instead PROMPTS** — the same picker led by a defaulted **"Portable profile (self-contained)"** `<unzip>\profile` option, the migrate checkbox offering a data-holding pre-existing `<unzip>\profile` — and remembers the answer in `profile.path` (`SaveLocalProfilePointer`; Cancel/`-Embedding` lands on `<unzip>\profile` UN-persisted so the next interactive launch re-asks; `allowUi` gates that prompt + the two-instances-on-one-profile warning).
- **A PORTABLE copy's very first launch — BEFORE the profile question, before ANY file is written — asks "Where should Agentmaster live?"** (`AgentMaster/PortableInstall.h`, `EnsureInstallDecidedAtStartup`, run in the EXE prelude right after the single-instance handoff and before `EnsureProfileResolvedAtStartup`; PROFILES.md §2b; reworked 2026-10-21, in the dev 0.0.1.33 build, where a packaged install never runs it, and in no release; that night's update fallback, all-users names, restorable swap, restart marker and old-script adoption are NOT BUILT — §2c is the scenario matrix with its test-desktop evidence): **Install just for you** (`%USERPROFILE%\.agentmaster\bin`, no administrator rights — the PREFERRED kind, the user's rule of 2026-10-21; adapting to a previous user install of ours into **Update the installed Agentmaster (vX → vY)** [an in-place upgrade preserving `settings\`/`profile\`/`profile.path`/`install.path`, replacing only what a release ships] or **Use the installed Agentmaster** [same/newer: the unzip records `install.path` = that dir and becomes a **launcher stub** forwarding every launch to it]) · **Install for all users of this PC** (`%ProgramW6432%\Agentmaster`: the embedded `am-update.ps1 -InstallMachine` runs ELEVATED — one UAC prompt, only that step — while this process waits unelevated and then starts the copy; `install.path` = `machine`, NO `profile.path`, HKLM verbs / Apps & Features / machine PATH, NO shared shortcut since 2026-10-22 (NOT BUILT; an Active Setup component gives every user icons of their own — see *A user's own install wins* below), its Apps & Features entry named **Agentmaster (all users)** under its OWN key name `HKLM\…\Uninstall\AgentmasterAllUsers` (`kMachineUninstallKey`; ⚠ Settings → Installed apps lists an Uninstall key NAME once, so a shared `Agentmaster` key hid the per-user entry; the old key is still read and replaced at the next update); refused for a zip without `.am-manifest`; adapting into **Update / Use the Agentmaster installed for all users**) · **Set it up for <signed-in user>** (only when ANOTHER account with rights runs the copy — UAC credentials of an administrator, an elevated "Run as different user", SYSTEM in a user's session: it restarts as the user signed in to Windows) · **Install to a folder you pick…** (Browse; a non-empty non-ours folder gets `\Agentmaster`; an unwritable one is refused toward the all-users option) · **Install here** (the folder holding the RUNNING binary, never the cwd — registration only) · **Keep it portable, run from here** (no shortcuts/registry; `install.path` = `portable`, never asks again, then the classic §2a picker) · **Ask me later** (= Cancel/X/Esc: writes NOTHING, this launch runs on the self-contained `<exedir>\profile` un-persisted with the §2a picker deferred too — `EnsureProfileResolvedAtStartup(allowUi, deferPortablePicker)` — and the next launch asks again). **The pre-selected option** (`DecidePromptDefault`): hand it to the signed-in user when another account can; else the per-user option whenever this user has a per-user install (use/update it); else a current all-users install; else *Install just for you*. **Who runs it** (`DetectInstallIdentity` → `DecideAccountPlan`: the token user + elevation vs the session's user via `WTSQuerySessionInformation` + `EqualSid`, fail-open): AsIs / AsIsElevated / OtherAccountRelaunch / OtherAccountExplain (the question explains how to set it up for the signed-in user) / NoSessionUser (session 0: never prompt). ⚠ **The app never keeps rights it was installed with:** when the signed-in user runs the first launch ELEVATED, the installed/adopted copy is started through `StartAsSessionUser` → `am-update.ps1 -RunAsMainUser` → a one-shot scheduled task for that user (interactive, limited) that launches it via Explorer or a hidden PowerShell, deleted when the launcher finished (checked: the copy reports `elevated=0`); an elevation that fails says why (`ScriptStartFailureText`: a declined prompt quietly returns to the question; `ERROR_ACCESS_DISABLED_BY_POLICY` 1260 — a standard user under "deny elevation requests" — reads "Windows does not let this account run programs as administrator"). **Machine copies** (`Profiles::IsMachineCopy`: `install.path` = `machine`, or a portable folder THIS account cannot write) run like installed programs: no question, nothing written beside the exe, each user's profile from their own home map / the default. **A user's own install wins for that user** (2026-10-22, NOT BUILT; PROFILES.md §2b — what a user without administrator rights can change was measured first in the test desktop: not the Public desktop, the common Start menu, Program Files, HKLM or the machine PATH; a same-named icon of their own does NOT hide a shared one, the machine PATH comes first, an app may not change a Start pin): the all-users install makes NO shared icons but registers an **Active Setup component** (`HKLM\SOFTWARE\Microsoft\Active Setup\Installed Components\{F8D18BA4-7DD1-4F04-8EE0-C9C7342F3264}`, `kActiveSetupKey` ↔ the core's `$script:AmActiveSetupId`: StubPath `"<exe>" --register-user`, a Version kept by every update) through which Windows gives each user their OWN *Agentmaster* desktop + Start icons once, at their next sign-in (`HandleRegisterUserFlagAtStartup` → `EnsureMachineCopyUserSetup`, before the single-instance handoff; the shared copy's own start and the installer run the same step; a deleted icon stays deleted; none for a user with an install of their own); a user's own install takes those icons over (the same file name, made anew) and re-points the user's taskbar pins of the shared copy at itself IN PLACE (`RepointSharedCopyTaskbarPins`, before `_setupAumid`); the shared copy hands EVERY launch — the app (`StartupPlan::HandoffToUserCopy`, checked first in `DecideStartup`) and `agentmaster-cli` (`ForwardCliToUserInstall`) — to the user's own install (`InstallHandoff.h`; always theirs, also when the shared one is newer; the app hand-off KEEPS the token like a launcher stub's — dropping rights would cost the scheduled-task hop at every start on a UAC-off PC; it runs after the single-instance hand-off, so a shared instance already running keeps its launches and never shares the profile with a second one; loops impossible: never to itself or to another shared copy; never for `-Embedding` / session 0; opt-out `AGENTMASTER_NO_HANDOFF=1`); the user's own uninstall gives its icons and pins back to the shared copy (`Move-AmUserEntriesToMachineCopy`) and then runs the shared copy's own `--register-user` (`Invoke-AmMachineCopyUserCommand`, only when the Active Setup component names that exe), which stamps its AUMID on them (`StampMachineCopyUserLinks`, a no-window process: the app defers a pin's stamping to its exit, GH#20053 — a script cannot compute the per-path id); the all-users uninstall removes the component and sweeps the icons it gave every user (`Get-AmUserShellFolders`; taskbar pins stay, as for any uninstalled program), and the shared copy cleans up the leftovers of a user's own copy deleted by hand (`MachineCopyUserUpkeep` → `CleanStaleUserInstall`; a folder on a drive that is not there now is kept). QA'd in a clean Windows 11 test desktop with the real app (PROFILES.md §2c, *A user's own install wins*); ⚠ in the core, never name a local `$version` in a function with a `-Version` parameter — PowerShell names ignore case (it overwrote the all-users entry's DisplayVersion). An INSTALL (per user) is MSI-like with no MSI/package/cert/admin: copy (or register in place) + a **desktop** shortcut + a **Start-menu** shortcut (Start search; stamped with the unpackaged AUMID the installed copy runs under — `unpackagedAumidForImagePath`, the app's own formula factored out of `WindowEmperor.cpp` — so toasts route) + an **"Open in Agentmaster"** right-click verb on folders/backgrounds/drives (`HKCU\Software\Classes\…\shell\Agentmaster`, `"<exe>" -d "%1|%V"`; Win11 "Show more options") + an **Apps & Features** entry (`HKCU\…\Uninstall\Agentmaster`, `UninstallString` = `"<exe>" --uninstall-portable`) + the dir on the **user PATH**, the work under a marquee TaskDialog, then the INSTALLED copy is launched and the installer process exits — handing it the foreground (`LaunchApp` → `AllowSetForegroundWindow`; the hand-over grants `ASFW_ANY` before its task runs; the question forces itself forward with `ForceForeground` when Windows refuses — a copy started by SmartScreen's *Run anyway* or by a task gets no foreground of its own, and the real app checked 2026-10-21 opened BEHIND the previous window before this). Every installed file loses the browser's download mark (`Zone.Identifier`: `DropDownloadMark` in `CopyTree`, `DropDownloadMarksOfShipped` for *Install here*, `Unblock-File` in the core's `Install-AmLayout`); a user's own file keeps it.
