Included by `IslandWindow.cpp` (the EXE, which calls `SetOverlayIcon`) and the harness (`TestTaskbarBadge`); the census behind the count is `Engine.h`'s `PublishWindowUnreadSessions` (`TestUnreadCensus`).
- `src/cascadia/TerminalApp/AgentMaster/ScreenSnapshot.h` + `ScreenImage.{h,cpp}` — **the Telegram card's 🖼 Screen Image** ([`MESSAGING.md`](doc/agentmaster/MESSAGING.md) §21.24). `ScreenSnapshot.h` is header-only and pure-ASCII, shared by `ControlCore.cpp` (TerminalControl) and the engine: a terminal screen as styled cells (glyph, width, resolved RGB colors, bold / italic / underline / strikethrough), the screen's default colors and font family, and its bounded text format (`SerializeScreenSnapshot` / `ParseScreenSnapshot`, which refuses anything malformed). `ScreenImage.h` holds the pure grid arithmetic (`ComputeScreenImageGrid`, `IsCellStretchGlyph`) and `RenderScreenSnapshotPng`; `ScreenImage.cpp` (no PCH) draws with Direct2D on a WIC bitmap and DirectWrite, encodes a PNG with WIC, initializes COM for the call only, and never throws (Rule #18). It blocks ~90–185 ms for a full screen, so the bot calls it on a worker thread. Tested by `tests_messaging_card.cpp` (`TestMessagingCard`, which decodes a real drawing back through WIC).
- `src/cascadia/TerminalApp/AgentMaster/MessagingFileLinks.h` + `MessagingFileServer.{h,cpp}` + `Messaging.Files.cpp` — **the Telegram FILE LINKS** ([`MESSAGING.md`](doc/agentmaster/MESSAGING.md) §21.32.4). `MessagingFileLinks.h` is header-only and pure (no Windows header; the cog includes it through `AgentManagerContent.h`): the session key (`FileLinkSessionHash`, SHA-256 with the secret's eight initial words — `Sha256.h`'s `Sha256WithInitialState`), the file-URL reader and the URL-path codec with every refusal (a climb, an escaped separator, a stream, a share, broken UTF-8, a device name, a trailing dot or space), the request target, the Markdown rewrite (links outside code only), the names and folders never linked, the request head / ranges / attachment header, the LINKED SET with its JSON, the LAN choices, the bases and `/download`'s argument. `MessagingFileServer.cpp` is the one Winsock TU: the HTTP server (`FileLinkServer`: private peers only, 24 connections, GET/HEAD, the final-path re-check, getters safe from any thread, a loopback-only bind for the tests), the secret in Credential Manager, the adapters' addresses. `Messaging.Files.cpp` is the service's half (one more TU of `MessagingService`): Apply, the server's lifecycle (three locks in one order: lifecycle, save, data), the authorizer, the rewrite of the mirror's and the history's items, the tick (save, prune, retry), `/download` and its "📤 Send as file" worker. Tested by `tests_messaging_files.cpp` (`TestMessagingFiles`: the key against an independent SHA-256's answers, the server end to end on loopback, the secret on a scratch credential). The route home lives in K:\source\proxmox (`cc-sysd/`, `sysd/cloudflare/10-zone.sh` + `45-cc-tunnel.sh`).
- `src/cascadia/TerminalApp/AgentMaster/TaskbarThumbnail.h` — header-only, plain Win32 (the `TaskbarBadge.h` idiom) — the **taskbar preview frame's pixel work** ([`NOTIFICATIONS.md`](doc/agentmaster/NOTIFICATIONS.md) §9): `FitInside` (never larger than DWM's box), `EstimatedBox`, `FrameThickness` / `FrameColor` (the alpha floor), the box-filter `DownscaleBgra`, `DrawFrame`, `LooksBlank`, `SolidCard`, the `JobBook` worker coalescer, and the GDI half (`CaptureClient` = `PrintWindow(PW_CLIENTONLY | PW_RENDERFULLCONTENT)`, `DrawIconCentered`, `CreateTopDownDib`). Included by `IslandWindow.cpp` (the EXE, which owns the DWM calls and the capture worker) and the harness (`TestTaskbarThumbnail`). The VM probe behind it: `tools/taskbar-thumbnail-probe/thumbprobe.cs` (C# 5, the inbox `csc`; run it only on a disposable desktop — it replaces window previews).
- `src/cascadia/TerminalApp/AgentMaster/CmdPathLimit.h` — the **PATH-length guard** ([`CMD_PATH_LIMIT.md`](doc/agentmaster/CMD_PATH_LIMIT.md)): header-only, pure rule (`CompactOverlongPath`: untouched at/under 7167 chars; above it drop empty + later-duplicate entries, then — only if still needed — directories that PROVABLY don't exist; never a live one) + a Win32 probe that never touches network/relative entries. Applied at TWO seams: the first statement of `WindowEmperor::HandleCommandlineArgs` (`CompactProcessPathIfOverlong` — the process env every handoff / window snapshot / managed session / `.cmd` launcher copies; one `[path-guard]` hooks.log line after the profile resolves) and `ConptyConnection::_LaunchAttachedClient` (every tab's FINAL env block — the belt for a reload-from-registry profile; the one Agentmaster touch in `TerminalConnection`). Unit-tested (`TestCmdPathLimit`, incl. a real-cmd.exe end-to-end pin).
- `src/cascadia/TerminalApp/AgentMaster/StartupGuard.h` — the **startup guard** ([`STARTUP_GUARD.md`](doc/agentmaster/STARTUP_GUARD.md)): header-only, plain Win32, used by `WindowEmperor::HandleCommandlineArgs` around `App{}` + `ReloadSettings()`. `PrewarmKnownFolders` replays the startup's `SHGetKnownFolderPath` calls (`kStartupKnownFolderLookups` — keep it in step with any new startup-time caller) on the main thread BEFORE `App{}` starts XAML's GPU thread, so the NVIDIA driver's DLL init can no longer deadlock against the first known-folder definition load; `HangWatchdog` (25 s, one-shot per episode via `<profile>\startup-hang.marker`, never kills a debuggee, race-free Armed → Disarmed | Fired) logs `[startup-hang]` and terminates a stuck launch so the single-instance mutex frees. Unit-tested (`TestStartupGuard`).
- `src/cascadia/TerminalApp/AgentMaster/TranscriptTurns.{h,cpp}` — **the Copy ▸ Transcript levels**: a C++ port of the am-agentmaster-cli skill's `transcript_turns.py` (v9 — the turns view: each turn's user message, its tool calls on one line, the remarks between them, compactions, the user's answers, the final reply; the sessions it was forked / plan-restarted from; an entry per spawned subagent / teammate, read from `<session>\subagents\`) at five detail levels (`TurnsLevel` / `TurnsOptionsForLevel`, the reference's LEVELS). `RenderTranscriptTurnsFile` (never-throw; reads the transcript, the older `.jsonl` relatives in its project folder and the agent transcripts — call it off the UI thread) backs CopySessionField codes 9-13 via `CopyTranscriptTurnsAsync` (`AgentTabOverlay.Actions.cpp`). Pinned byte for byte to goldens the reference script itself writes over the skill's synthetic fixtures (`tests/turns/make_goldens.py` → `tests/turns/expected/`, `TestTranscriptTurns`): a change to the skill's v9 output means regenerating them and porting the change; moving to a newer skill version is a deliberate edit of both (the goldens pin `--preset v9`). The few deliberate divergences (all on input Claude Code never writes) are listed in the header. **Section 8 is NOT part of the port**: `CollectConversationUpstream` — the summary panel's whole-conversation chain, reusing the port's chain walk with Agentmaster-only `ChainOptions` (a default-constructed value IS the reference walk, so the goldens can't move): the fork search only for a transcript that opens with a copied line, plus the recorded `/clear` / `/handover` predecessors (`AttachRecordedParent`); a cheap gate, a 32-slot process-wide cache of FINAL answers only. Its consumer `BuildSummaryConversation` (the numbered rows + session separators) is declared in the light, WinRT-free `AgentMaster/SummaryConversation.h` and defined at the end of `TranscriptTurns.cpp`; both are harness-tested (`TestConversationUpstream`, `TestSummaryConversation`).
- `src/cascadia/TerminalApp/AgentTranscriptOverlay.{h,cpp}` + `TerminalPage.AgentTranscript.cpp` + `AgentMaster/MarkdownBlocks.{h,cpp}` — **the transcript overlay** ([`TRANSCRIPT_OVERLAY.md`](doc/agentmaster/TRANSCRIPT_OVERLAY.md)): a session's conversation at a detail level (the very text Copy ▸ Transcript copies — `TranscriptTurns`, or the Codex readers), rendered as Markdown in a reading card laid over the WHOLE page (90% × 80% of the window via a 3×3 star grid; a dimmed backdrop; a tab per level in the Copy menu's order, Default first). It opens at the level the user LAST PICKED in any window: every tab pick is a freshest-disk RMW of `AppSettings::transcriptOverlayLevel` (the level's NAME, written by the overlay itself — both cog-Save preserve blocks restore it), every open re-reads it from disk, Default until a first pick. The header's ↵ LINE-WRAP toggle (first of its buttons; `_ToggleWrapLines`) is global the same way (`AppSettings::transcriptOverlayWrapLines`, default ON): ON, a line wider than the body wraps; OFF, it keeps its length and the part past the right edge is hidden — one property per level view (`ApplyWrapMode`: the horizontal scroll bar `Disabled` hands the content the viewport's width, `Hidden` measures it at infinite width and clips it; the horizontal scroll mode stays `Disabled`, so it is never a sideways scroll away); a switch keeps the reader on the same passage (`_SyncWrapMode`: the element at the top of the view, found by summing `DesiredSize`s, is scrolled back after an `UpdateLayout`), and a level hidden at the switch catches up when shown. Each turn's TOOL AREA — the bullet list of calls + remarks under `**● Claude**` or an agent entry's header (`MdFindToolAreas`, found off-thread with the parse) — is FOLDED into one summary row ("▸ 4 tool calls: Bash ×3, Write · 2 remarks", `MdToolAreaSummary`) with the reply left open, so Default / Full / Max read like Compact; a click opens one list, "Expand all" every list of the level; Compact's own lists (answers, compactions, messages) never fold, and "(no tool calls)" becomes a one-line note. SESSION CHAIN LINKS (§3d): in a chained transcript (a fork, a plan restart) every "session N" of the opening "Session chain" list — each row's label, "forked / restarted from session N" — is a Hyperlink that scrolls session N's first top-level "Session N of M" heading to the top of the view (`_JumpToSession` → `_ScrollToSessionHeading`: `UpdateLayout` → `TransformToVisual` → `ChangeView`, then focus to the scroll view so BringIntoViewOnFocusChange can't drag the reader back to the clicked link); `MdFindSessionChain` / `MdFindSessionRefs` / `MdSessionHeadingNumber` find the list, the targets and the mentions off-thread with the parse (never inside a word, a code span or a “quoted” title; 1..4096); only a session WITH a heading links (a session with no turns of its own has none — its label stays plain); a click before the time-sliced build reached the heading is kept as the level's pending jump and done by the slice that builds it (dropped by a level switch or the block cap). `MarkdownBlocks` (pure C++, no PCH) parses with **cmark** — the vcpkg port UIMarkdown already uses, auto-linked into TerminalApp.dll — into FLAT render blocks, each carrying its quotes / list items outermost-first with ids (so a quote bar runs through the gap between two paragraphs of one quote); raw HTML is KEPT as text, soft breaks are real line breaks, images are never fetched, ordered lists keep their numbers. The overlay (`winrt::implements`, one per window, built lazily into Root by `_EnsureTranscriptOverlay`) renders off-thread, then builds the XAML in ~12 ms LOW-priority time slices into chunk panels (only the current level builds; each level keeps its own scroll view); speaker labels tint the next quote's bar (❯ blue · ● orange · ✉ purple). It closes on a backdrop press (deferred a tick), Esc (also caught page-wide in `_ManagerPaneNavPreviewKeyDown` — an Esc reaching claude would interrupt it), its ✕, and every tab switch / move / drag (`_CloseTranscriptOverlay` from `_ApplyAgentTabSelection`, `_TryMoveTab`, `_TabReorderActivate`, `_sendDraggedTabToWindow`); while open a `LosingFocus` guard keeps focus inside the card, so every close hands focus back via `_FocusCurrentTab(true)`. Harness: `tests_markdown.cpp` (`TestMarkdownBlocks`, incl. the tool areas over an inline fixture + five goldens, and the session chain links over an inline fixture + the edge cases + four chain goldens), compiled only when the vcpkg cmark is present (`AM_HAVE_CMARK`), else a skip.
- `src/cascadia/TerminalApp/AgentMaster/DraftCarry.h` — header-only, pure (the `PendingPaste.h` idiom, pure-ASCII source) — the **FORK CARRY + PASTE JOURNAL** core (PENDING_INPUT.md §11 / §2c): claude's own paste rules mirrored from its 2.1.280 bundle (`NormalizePastedTextLikeClaude`, `ClaudePasteNewlineCount`, `ClaudeWouldCollapsePaste`), the wrap-tolerant placeholder scan (`FindDraftPlaceholders` — all five claude forms, incl. the bare `[Pasted text #N]`), the journal-first resolver `ResolveDraftCarry` (→ `DraftCarryPlan` of Typed/Block segments; LIVE vs STRICT unresolved policy), the piecewise fill plan `BuildDraftFillPastes`, and the fill verifier `VerifyDraftFillPastes` (full or prefix). Included by `SessionRegistry.h` (the `PasteJournalEntry` journal), `ClaudeSpawn.h` (the `ResolvePendingDraftCarry` cache adapter) and `TerminalPage.h`; unit-tested (`TestDraftCarry`).
- `src/cascadia/TerminalApp/AgentToastActivator.h` — **System notifications' TOAST COM ACTIVATOR** (NOTIFICATIONS.md §4a): the per-identity ToastActivatorCLSID pair (release/dev, matching the two manifests' `<desktop:ToastNotificationActivation>` + `<com:Class>`), an `INotificationActivationCallback` whose `Activate` routes the toast's `launch` payload (the session id) through `ActivateSessionInOtherWindows` → foreground + jump, its `IClassFactory`, and `Register()`/`IsRegistered()`. Header-only + included by exactly ONE TU (`TerminalPage.AgentEngine.cpp`, which `std::call_once`s `Register()` at engine init) — the `PromptAnchor.h` idiom, so no vcxproj entry. It is what makes the shell activate the RUNNING instance in-process instead of launching a second process.
- `src/cascadia/TerminalApp/AgentMaster/LaunchPresets.h` + `LaunchPresetsOs.h` — **PRESETS** ([`HARNESSES.md`](doc/agentmaster/HARNESSES.md)). `LaunchPresets.h` is the PURE, header-only core (the `ModelCatalog.h` idiom, harness-tested in `tests/tests_presets.cpp`): the field table that drives the Settings editor, the resolver, the snapshot and the compile (`kPresetFieldSpecs`); the built-in providers (OpenRouter, Xiaomi MiMo, NanoGPT, RunPod — `kBuiltInLaunchProviders`; RunPod has no default URL, `baseUrlExample` shows its URL shapes, and `modelRequired` refuses a preset with no model, `PresetLaunchRefusal`) and custom ones; the shipped templates, added only by the user and linked (`AddLaunchPresetsFromTemplates` / `LaunchPresetTemplatesNotInList`; `ShouldOfferAddPresets` = the empty list that makes every launch menu offer "Add presets…", whose dialog text is `AddPresetsHelpText`; `DropUntouchedAutoAddedPresets` = the one-time cleanup of the presets 0.6.8.18 added by itself); `launch-presets.json`; the value tiers (`ResolvePresetField`); the per-session snapshot `LaunchArgs` (tiers 1–2 frozen, Windows-env NAMES re-read per launch, never a key); the refusal (`PresetLaunchRefusal`); the compile to the `--settings` env block + command-line additions (`CompileClaudeLaunch`); the menu entries (`LaunchMenuEntriesFrom`, `"@<presetId>"` tokens). `LaunchPresetsOs.h` is the OS half, TerminalApp-only: the env lookups (Agentmaster's list; Windows = process env, then the registry), the provider keys in Windows Credential Manager, the stamp-cached config loader, `LaunchMenuEntries` / `LaunchMenuSpec`, `ShouldOfferAddPresets()` (the one question every host asks before it hands a menu its "Add presets…" opener), `LaunchArgsForTarget`, `Load/StoreLaunchArgs` (session-store key `launchArgs`) and `CompileLaunchForSpawn`. The launch seams decide through ONE `TerminalPage::_ResolveLaunchForSpawn`; `ClaudeSpawn.cpp` writes the content-addressed `launch-settings\<preset>-<hash>.json` (`MaterializeLaunchSettingsFile`, verified + atomic) and scopes the key in the pwsh host script (`BuildPwshHostedCommandline`'s `authTokenEnv`).
- `src/cascadia/TerminalApp/AgentMaster/AgentHarness.{h,cpp}` + `AgentHarnessCore.{h,cpp}` + `ClaudeSdkUnity.cpp` + `ClaudeSdkProcessWin32.cpp` — **the agent harness** ([`SDK_HARNESS.md`](doc/agentmaster/SDK_HARNESS.md), 2026-10-10; in the v0.7.9 pre-release, without the permission policy of 2026-10-12). `AgentHarness` is the conversation (`HarnessConversation`: `Prewarm` / `Send` — the newest wins — / `Interrupt` / `Reset` / `ParkAfter` / `Shutdown`, `Inventory` — the process's own report of its permission mode and of everything its model can use —, events through a sink, tools as in-process MCP servers; `EnsureProcess` / `StartOnce` / `SettlePermissionMode` carry out the permission policy); only its `.cpp` includes the SDK and nlohmann/json. `AgentHarnessCore` is the model-free half that the m5 suite compiles: the record (`HarnessRecord`, caps, JSON, atomic save), the cold-start digest, the `--settings` text (⚠ `autoCompactEnabled` beside the window), the PERMISSION POLICY's decisions (`HarnessPermissionMode`, `DecideHarnessPermissionStep`, `HarnessStartFailureNamesPermissionMode`, `NextHarnessPermissionMode`: bypass, then auto, then `dontAsk`), the child's environment (removals + the `AM_HARNESS` mark), and the readers of Claude Code's `system` (start with its whole inventory, a refused tool call, compaction) / `result` / usage lines. `ClaudeSdkUnity.cpp` is the ONE TU that compiles the SDK sources (inside a warning push/pop; the SDK's `atexit` redirected); `ClaudeSdkProcessWin32.cpp` is Agentmaster's implementation of the SDK-internal process interface (overlapped pipes with a real timed wait — the SDK's own spins a core —, `CreateProcessW`, an empty environment value removes the variable, a kill-on-close job). The SDK itself is the submodule `refs/claude-agent-sdk-cpp`.
- `src/cascadia/TerminalApp/AgentMaster/sessiondb/` — **the session database service** ([`SESSIONS_DB.md`](doc/agentmaster/SESSIONS_DB.md); `agentmaster-sessiondb.exe`, its own console exe: `agentmaster-sessiondb.vcxproj`, the standalone twin `_compile.bat [out] [x64|arm64]`, `duckdb.props` = the pinned libduckdb's folder). `DuckDbWrap` (the C API, RAII), `SessionDbSchema` (tables, views, ART indexes, meta), `SessionDbExtract` + `SessionDbFold` + `SessionDbWalk.h` (a line's facts, the session fold, the live-branch walk), `SessionDbStore` (the ingest, `upsertSessions` / `resync`, merges in slices, the two-part re-cluster, `RebuildSummaries`), `SessionDbSummary` (the summary SQL, D14, `kSummaryVersion`), `SessionDbIndex` (dictionary, postings, `Merge`, `ReclusterPostings`, the resumable `ReclusterLines`), `SessionDbSearch` (the engine), `SessionDbTools` (the tool registry), `SessionDbServer` (the pipe service), `SessionDbMcp.cpp` (the stdio MCP server), `SessionDbVerify` (the summary parity check), `sessiondb_main.cpp` (the modes: `serve`, `mcp`, `resync`, `search`, `sql`, `recluster`, `verify-summary`, `tool`, `call`). Shared with the app: `AgentMaster\SessionDbProtocol.h` (names, framing, methods), `SessionDbToolDefs.h` (the tools' text, one copy), `SessionDbTokenizer.h`, `SessionDbClient.{h,cpp}` (the pipe client), `SessionDbRefresher.{h,cpp}` (the app's refresh tick). Tested by `tests\tests_sessiondb.cpp` (`TestSessionDb`).
- `src/cascadia/TerminalApp/AgentMaster/SessionSearchTools.{h,cpp}` + `SessionSearchAgent.{h,cpp}` + `TerminalPage.AgentSessionsAi.cpp` — **the Sessions page's AI search** ([`SESSIONS.md`](doc/agentmaster/SESSIONS.md) §9). `SessionSearchTools` (SDK-free, in the m5 suite): the catalog (`SessionCatalog`, `GatherSessionCatalog` with a time budget, a cancel check and reuse of the catalog before), one search (`ParseSessionSearchParams`, `RunSessionSearch`, `FormatSessionSearchResult`), and the two tool servers (`SessionSearchToolset`: short ids, `search`, `show`, the system prompt). `SessionSearchAgent`: the process's one conversation (`SharedSessionSearchAgent`), its index keeper (the background, stepwise catalog read), the request generation that cancels a running search, listeners by token, the view file. `TerminalPage.AgentSessionsAi.cpp`: the page's view of it (the toggle, Enter / Escape, the ⊘ reset, the strip, the list at the `_RenderSessionsTable` chokepoint, the `aiExtra` rows outside the page's window, the in-use tally that releases the index and parks the process).
- `src/cascadia/TerminalApp/AgentModelPrompt.h` — the shared **"Specify a model" prompt** card (`AgentBuildSpecifyModelCard`): an editable-ComboBox id box seeded from the configured list + the durable `model-list.json` droplist and PRE-FILLED with the model last committed in any window (`LoadLastSpecifiedModel`; Enter / Escape in it act on the key-up), links to the two published model lists, and a bounded off-thread **Fetch** (Updater.h's `HttpsGet` + `AgentMaster/ModelCatalog.h`'s pure parsers). Deliberately SPLIT from `AgentModelMenu.h`: that header is included by `Tab.cpp` (core WT code) and this one drags winhttp/comctl32 in through Updater.h's `#pragma comment(lib)`. Included by exactly the two HOSTS that show it — `AgentManagerContent` (modal over `_root`) and `TerminalPage` (Root() popup, which also serves the WT tab menu's submenus).
- `src/cascadia/TerminalApp/AgentStatusColors.h` — the ONE shared `SessionState` → color table (Triage-Board dot, per-tab overlay, and the tab-strip status dot all read it). Also the shared `#AARRGGBB` color parse/format (`ParseArgbHexColor` / `FormatArgbHexColor`, for `flashRingColor` + the pending-dots colors), the pending-dots contrast pick (`BackgroundIsLight` / `PendingDotsColorFor` — WCAG luminance, used by the tab strip + board card so the unsent-draft "3 dots" are never invisible; PENDING_INPUT.md), and the **bookmark-tag** color palette + resolvers (`kTagPalette`/`TagPaletteColor` — the picker's swatches; `TagColorFor` — the stable name-hash fallback; `ResolveTagDisplayColor` — the ONE user-picked > name-hash resolution every tag renderer shares: the `AgentTagsSpec` producer, the Tags panel/hover panel, and the Sessions Tags column).
- `src/cascadia/TerminalApp/AgentTipHelpers.h` — the ONE islands-safe hover-tooltip recipe (`AgentSetTip` / `AgentCloseTipOn`; `ToolTipService`'s auto-dismiss is unreliable under XAML Islands), shared by `AgentManagerContent` + the Sessions page + the per-tab overlay + the tab strip (thin TU-local wrappers like `SessSetTip` delegate here). Architecture: ONE per-UI-thread tip HOST (one shared `ToolTip` + one one-shot open timer + a 1s while-open watchdog); per element only attached DPs (text + delay + optional placement/title) + three capture-less pointer handlers, and `SetToolTip` is attached only for the duration of a real hover-open — see the per-element-ToolTip LEAK gotcha (the 68 GB prod freeze) before touching this.
- `src/cascadia/TerminalApp/AgentAmbientAnimation.{h,cpp}` — the **ambient animations on the Composition API** (UI_THREAD.md §10c): `AgentOpacityLoop` (one XAML element's `Opacity` through `UIElement::StartAnimation`, a linear forever loop that begins and ends on the element's still value), `AgentDotsPulse` (the unsent-draft dots' three staggered loops, shared by the tab header and the board / side-panel cards' `BuildPendingDots`), and `AgentEmberSparks` (the spark crown's rising embers as composition visuals attached with `SetElementChildVisual`), plus (§10i) `AgentLockstepBlink` (the unread flash ring's 600 ms on / off blink: a composition `ExpressionAnimation` on the element's `Opacity` that follows ONE shared clock per UI thread — a ref-counted `CompositionPropertySet` loop that runs only while some ring follows it — so every ring of every window blinks in lockstep; it ends with a static `Opacity` set of 1.0, the facade's no-write-back cancel). `AgentCompositorOf` caches the UI thread's compositor in the per-thread `AgentAmbientThread` (leaked like the tip host), so only the thread's FIRST anchor ever gets a hand-out visual. The header is LIGHT (declarations; the member types come through the pch); the `.cpp` is the ONE TU that includes the 1.1 MB `winrt/Windows.UI.Composition.h`. See the ambient-animation Gotcha before adding any looping animation.
- `src/cascadia/TerminalApp/AgentLaunchBar.{h,cpp}` — **the shared LAUNCH BAR** (2026-10-06, SIDE_PANEL.md §2e): the agent element (the Claude <-> Codex toggle, or a fixed `● Claude` chip for a Claude-only host), the working-dir box with its validation underline and its path-picker drop-down (Browse…, RECENT / RECENT MATCHES, GIT WORKTREES, MATCHES / SUBFOLDERS, sized against the window), Launch with its PRESETS hover menu (a mouse hover opens it at once — 2026-10-10, 952c246fa, in production 0.6.8.19; it waited 450 ms before — while a touch keeps the 450 ms hold so a tap launches without it; since 2026-10-19 (in production since 0.6.8.23 and dev since 0.0.1.31), ONE click on Launch launches while that menu is up — the menu's light dismiss eats the press, so its `Closing` arms the press, `_OnLaunchButtonReleased` completes it on the release over Launch, and `ClickLaunch` ignores a second click within the double-click time; before, Launch needed a double-click — see the Gotcha *A light-dismiss flyout eats the press that dismisses it*; since 2026-10-23 (SP-K1, NOT BUILT yet) in EVERY host — the side panel's "+" card still took two clicks on 0.8.0: the arm also reads the pointer MESSAGE being processed (`GetPointerInfo`), and a release WATCH (`_OnLaunchReleaseWatchTick`, a 15 ms UI-thread timer from the arm to the release) completes an armed press whose release never reached Launch, judged with the last pointer sample on Launch (`_NoteLaunchPointerOnButton`); every arm, completion and decline is a `[launchbar]` line; since bd9ba2eae — in dev since 0.0.1.6, production since 0.6.8.20 — a programmatic focus or text write opens no folder list, and an open list re-anchors after the next layout pass; since 2026-10-19 (in production since 0.6.8.23 and dev since 0.0.1.31), a host may open the list itself, `OpenPathPicker` — the side panel's card does, right after a layout pass that places the card), and Fork — moved VERBATIM out of `AgentManagerContent.Launch.cpp` and `_BuildLayout`, so the Agent Manager page and the side panel's `+` launcher card run ONE implementation. A host (`AgentLaunchBarHost`) supplies the callbacks (spawn / resume / fork / codex / claude-missing / Specify… / layoutChanged / launched / textEdited, each capturing its host weakly), the drop-down's parent panel + row span, the dialog anchor, and the window's ONE recent-folders list (`AgentLaunchRecentDirs`, shared through `AgentManagerContent::LaunchRecentDirs`). The bar's handlers hold it weakly (`enable_shared_from_this`); `Shutdown()` (noexcept, guarded) stops its UI-affine hover timer and closes an open hover menu and drop-down. `SeedButtonStateBrushes` puts the Manager's six Button state brushes on a dictionary (forced-Dark null brushes). `AgentManagerContent.Launch.cpp` is now the Manager's HOST half (`_MakeLaunchBarHost`, three forwarders, `LaunchBoxText` / `SetLaunchBoxText`).
- `src/cascadia/TerminalApp/AgentMaster/PointerCardPlacement.h` — header-only, pure — where a card that opens AT the pointer goes (`PlacePointerCard`): its corner at the click, extending toward the window's center (each axis by the click's half), clamped 8 DIP inside the window, a too-large card pinned to the near margin, an unmeasured window never clamped, non-finite inputs neutralized. The side panel's `+` launcher uses it; unit-tested (`TestPointerCardPlacement`, tests_sidepanel.cpp).
- `src/cascadia/TerminalApp/AgentSessionToolTip.{h,cpp}` — **`AgentSessionToolTipHost`**, the RICH session tooltip's LIFECYCLE (2026-10-05, extracted verbatim from `Tab` — [`HANDOVER_tab-tooltip.md`](doc/agentmaster/HANDOVER_tab-tooltip.md)): one framework-managed `ToolTip` per OWNER element (never writes `IsOpen`), `Content` swapped only while closed (+ the one-shot open swap for the summary arrival), the owner-loaded guard, owner `Unloaded → Detach` / `Loaded → Update` (attach outside a dwell), the hover hook (`PointerEntered` → closed-only re-anchor → the page's build), the wheel hook read on the owner (`handledEventsToo`, `[tooltip-wheel] <label>: …`), and an optional per-owner PLACEMENT PROVIDER (none = the tab's `Bottom` under its own bounds). Held in a `shared_ptr` whose handlers capture it weakly; the owner is held weakly; no destructor touches XAML (owners call `Shutdown()` / `Detach()`). TWO owners: `Tab` (its old `SetAgentToolTip` / `ClearAgentToolTip` / `EnsureAgentToolTipHoverHook` are forwarders) and each side-panel card in the **Sum** OFF mode (SIDE_PANEL.md §2d — docked beside the panel, `_SidePanelDropCardToolTip` shuts it down before a card leaves the list). The content side is the shared `TerminalPage::_PushSessionToolTipCard` (signature + scroll state per SURFACE key, the Summary cache + `_tabTooltipSummaryWaiters` per session); an element with a host must not also carry an `AgentSetTip` floating tip (`AgentSuppressTip` silences one). ⚠ Per the WinUI 2.8 source, the `ToolTip` style caps every card at 320 px (`ToolTipMaxWidth`) — the 660 px card budget does not show (HANDOVER_tab-tooltip.md §6).
- `src/cascadia/TerminalApp/AgentLocalTooltip.h` — **LocalTooltip**: a DESIGNATED-AREA tooltip surface — the hovered element's `AgentSetTip` text renders in ONE fixed panel (title auto-derived from the control's Header/Content, `AgentSetTipTitle` overrides) instead of a floating ToolTip chasing the pointer. `AttachScope(root)` = ONE bubbling PointerMoved per SCOPE (never per element — the 68 GB lesson; multiple roots may feed one panel), immediate + STICKY (gaps between controls don't strobe it), and marks the root so the floating host's open tick YIELDS inside (`LocalTipScopeProperty`, a live switch); three change-gated anchors — `AnchorTopLeftOutside` (left of the anchor, tops aligned) · `AnchorTopRightAbove` (right edges aligned, top at the HOST's top) · `AnchorTopRightInside` (nested in the anchor's top-right corner) — all growing only leftward; `SetClickThrough(true)` for placements floating OVER content (never eats a click; the default swallows taps for over-a-modal-dim placements). THREE consumers: the **Settings cog** (`_settingsLocalTip` — left-outside the card, tap-swallowing), the **Sessions page** (`_sessionsLocalTip` — the header's empty right column above the detail pane, click-through, kept across tab-switch restore like the scroll offset), and the **Agent Manager page** (`_managerLocalTip` — nested in the Triage Board's top-right, click-through, scoped to the toolbar/board/bottom regions — NOT `_root`, so the settings/claude-missing overlay cards stay outside it; also retires the board cards' 4s tip hold-back *intrusiveness* rationale there — the side panel updates immediately). All ~200 existing `AgentSetTip` call sites on these surfaces feed the panels UNCHANGED; a window too narrow to host a panel falls back to the classic floating tips automatically.
- `src/cascadia/TerminalApp/AgentCopyActions.h` — the ONE shared `CopySessionField` action (Session Id · working-dir Path · Branch · **Current Prompt** · Claude/Codex Launch CLI · Summary · the **Transcript ▸** levels [codes 9-13 — Minimal / Compact / Default / Full / Max, rendered off-thread by `TranscriptTurns` (`CopyTranscriptTurnsAsync`, which honors the copy generation so a slow render never lands on a later copy; a Codex rollout falls back to the plain-text readers); every copy menu builds the submenu from the ONE shared `kTranscriptCopyMenu` table; the older codes 5 Transcript and 8 Transcript Followup (`ReadConversationFollowupText`, the per-turn `❯ user + ● final-reply` brief) still work, but no menu offers them]) behind the per-tab overlay's copy menu (`AgentTabOverlay`), the Triage Board / Explorer-tree session menu's Copy submenu (`AgentManagerContent`), AND the WT tab menu's `Copy >` (`Tab` → `TerminalPage`), so the three copy menus can never drift apart. **Current Prompt** (code 7, PENDING_INPUT.md §8) is the one case with two sources: an OPTIONAL `liveDraft` provider — the hosting window's wrapped live input-box read (`TerminalPage::_ReadLiveDraftForSession` → `ControlCore::ReadPendingInputDraft`), absent/failing/`""` for a session hosted in another window, a dormant tab or a torn-down control — falling back to the observer's recorded `SessionInfo::pendingInput` (possibly the persisted memory), chosen by the pure, unit-tested `PickCurrentPromptText` (`PendingInput.h`). Claude only (Codex has no `❯` input box). Also **`CopyPromptTextExpandingPastes`** — the ONE shared "copy a PROMPT" action (PENDING_INPUT.md §8e) behind Copy Current Prompt (code 7) AND the Prompt Queue row menu's Copy: a `[Pasted text #N +M lines]` / truncated placeholder is copied as the pasted text itself (newline-folded, expanded off-thread from the paste-cache by the all-or-refuse `ExpandPendingDraftPastes`, the clipboard written back on the UI thread; as rendered on any refusal; a process-wide copy generation lets a newer copy supersede a slow expansion), a placeholder-free prompt still copies synchronously + verbatim. Also **`OpenSessionFolder`** — the ONE shared "open a session's working folder in explorer.exe" action (resolves `EffectiveWorkingDir(mode, s)` — the INFERRED dir while the session infers, else the launch cwd — falling back to the live PEB cwd; opens off-thread + the click chime + `[nav] open-path`). Both the per-tab overlay's **Open Path** folder button (`AgentTabOverlay::_OpenFolder` now delegates to it) AND the **WT tab menu's "Open In Explorer"** item (`Tab::OpenInExplorerRequested` → `TerminalPage::_RegisterTabEvents` → `OpenSessionFolder`) route through it, so they can never resolve a different folder — session-only, shown at flyout-open via `Tab::SetAgentOpenInExplorerVisible` (the `SetAgentMarkUnreadVisible` idiom). Implemented in `AgentTabOverlay.Actions.cpp` beside `CopySessionField`.
- `src/cascadia/TerminalApp/TerminalPage.Agent{Engine,Sessions,Observer,WindowRecord,SessionsPage}.cpp` — the TerminalPage-side Agentmaster *implementation* in five same-class TUs (the upstream `TabManagement.cpp` pattern). **Engine** (`~TerminalPage`, `_InitAgentmasterEngine`, the Agent Manager page — `_CreateAgentManagerPage` / `_ShowManagerPage` / `_HideManagerPage` + the Home button and its jump-back face — `_WireAgentManagerContent`), **Sessions** (spawn/launch/restore/close/adopt-external for **both** Claude and Codex — `_LaunchCodexSession`/`_SpawnCodexSession`/`_AdoptExternalCodex` mirror the Claude seams — tab-title sync, smart naming + per-dir tab color), **Observer** (the per-tab overlay/badge, bind/reconcile/ liveness incl. the managed-Codex state reconcile `_ReconcileManagedCodex`, the UI lane `_ObserverProbe`; also the **bookmark-tag UI** — `_SetTabAgentTags`/`_RefreshTabTags` badge spec, the Tags editor panel + color picker `_OpenTagEditorAt`/`_CommitTagEditorAdd`/`_RemoveGlobalTag`, and the rich `_ShowTagHoverPanelNow` hover panel), **WindowRecord** (capture/flush/restore + reopen saved windows; Codex tab refs), **SessionsPage** (the full-window Sessions browser — SESSIONS.md — incl. the ★ favorite column + filter + `_ToggleSessionFavorite`, FAVORITES.md). Declarations stay in `TerminalPage.h`.
- small touches in `TerminalPage.{h,cpp}` (~20 integration seams left in the `.cpp`: `_OnFirstLayout` startup, `_MakePane`'s `agentManager` branch, close/quit record-flush + teardown-archive, tab-move detach, title/color sync hooks, `_restartPaneConnection` injector re-point), `Tab.{h,cpp}` (a `TabColorChanged` event + `GetRuntimeTabColor`), `TabManagement.cpp` (incl. the generic `_DismissAgentPageOverlays` tab-switch seam), `TabHeaderControl.xaml` + `TerminalTabStatus.{h,idl}` (the tab-strip status dot: an `AgentStatusVisible`/ `AgentStatusBrush`-bound Ellipse in the indicator row, plus the **FAVORITE marker** `Path`s — the `AgentFavoriteVisible`-bound gold **crown** at the dot's NW *or* the `AgentFavoriteStarVisible`-bound white, golden-tipped **star** behind the dot, mutually exclusive, chosen by the cog's `AppSettings::favoriteIcon` — FAVORITES.md §5a; **and the bookmark-TAG badges** — an always-open parented `HeaderTagBookmarksPopup` (the below-tab 30/70 overhang) whose ribbons are (re)built by `_UpdateTagBadges` from `TerminalTabStatus::AgentTagsSpec` and placed by the coalescing `_PositionTagBadges` → `_PositionTagBadgesNow` scheduler; hover raises `TagBadgeHoverBegin/End`); registrations in `TerminalAppLib.vcxproj`.
- `src/cascadia/wt/shim.cpp` + `wt.vcxproj` (the `agentmaster <verb>` overload, CLI.md §2): the alias-target launcher shim is now **console-subsystem + dual-mode** (`SubSystem=Console`) — it execs `agentmaster-cli.exe` for a CLI verb / leading CLI-flag and forwards everything else to `Agentmaster.exe` byte-for-byte; the `agentmaster-cli.vcxproj` reference + the `wt`-mirrored entry in `OpenConsole.slnx` + `CascadiaPackage.wapproj` ship the CLI in the package. Which command lines are a CLI call is `src/cascadia/TerminalApp/AgentMaster/CliDispatch.h` (header-only, plain Win32; 2026-10-23, NOT BUILT), shared with the app exe's `WindowsTerminal/main.cpp`, whose first step answers a CLI call that reaches `Agentmaster.exe` directly (CLI.md §2a); `TestCliDispatch` (tests_spawn_sched.cpp) covers it.
- `src/cascadia/WindowsTerminalShim/` (`shim.cpp` + `WindowsTerminalShim.rc` + `resource.h` + `WindowsTerminalShim.vcxproj`, GUID `{34770444-…}`) — the **`WindowsTerminal.exe` BACKWARDS-COMPAT shim**. The GUI binary is now `Agentmaster.exe`; this tiny **GUI-subsystem** forwarder (project `WindowsTerminalShim`, **`TargetName=WindowsTerminal`** so the output leaf is the old name) keeps a by-name launch of `WindowsTerminal.exe` working — it `CreateProcessW`s the neighbor `Agentmaster.exe` with the **verbatim commandline tail** and exits, so old shortcuts / scripts / muscle-memory land in the ONE real app. Deliberately a shim, **not a copy of the binary**: WindowEmperor derives its single-instance identity from the process IMAGE path (unpackaged), so a second exe named `WindowsTerminal.exe` would hash to a DIFFERENT identity and spin up a SECOND instance on the same profile (the profile mutex would warn) — forwarding keeps exactly one identity, the real one. Its `.rc` embeds the SAME branding-conditional terminal icon block as `WindowsTerminal.rc` (`WT_BRANDING_DEV` ⇒ `images-Dev\terminal.ico`), verified byte-identical to `Agentmaster.exe`'s icon, so it LOOKS identical too. NOT referenced by either manifest (defterm/COM + AUMID activation target `Agentmaster.exe` directly — only a raw by-name launch hits the shim); it flattens into the package root as `WindowsTerminal.exe` and rides the portable zip automatically (the wapproj flatten + `New-UnpackagedTerminalDistribution.ps1`'s keep-list). Wired via the `wt`-mirrored `OpenConsole.slnx` entry + a `CascadiaPackage.wapproj` `ProjectReference`.
- `Package-Rel.appxmanifest` + `Package-Dev.appxmanifest` (the two identities; selection in `CascadiaPackage.wapproj` via `AgentmasterPackageIdentity`; each also declares its OWN **toast-activator CLSID** — `<desktop:ToastNotificationActivation>` + a `<com:Class>` under an `Agentmaster.exe` ExeServer with `Arguments="-ToastActivated"` — see `AgentToastActivator.h` / NOTIFICATIONS.md §4a; a change here needs a **re-register**), a comctl32-v6 dependency in `WindowsTerminal.manifest` (the profile picker's TaskDialog), the `AGENTMASTER_PROFILE` redirect in `TerminalSettingsModel/FileUtils.cpp` (Terminal's own settings → `<profile>\terminal\`), the profile bootstrap call in `WindowEmperor.cpp`, `doc/agentmaster/`, `tools/Build-Agentmaster.ps1` (incl. `-ReleaseIdentity` and `-LayoutDir`), `tools/Swap-AgentmasterLayout.ps1` (the hot-deploy swap of either installed identity — see Deploy & run → *The swap script*), `tools/am-lock.sh` (the global build/launch mutex — see Deploy & run → *Concurrency lock*), `tools/warpbench.cpp` + `tools/_build_warpbench.bat` (the GPU-vs-WARP terminal frame + RAM benchmark behind RENDERING.md; standalone).
- `.github/workflows/ci.yml` + `.github/workflows/release.yml` — the GitHub Actions build gate + public-release pipeline (see *Releasing a public version*), with `tools/New-AgentmasterSourceLink.ps1` (the Source Link map every release PDB embeds) and `tools/Export-AgentmasterSymbols.ps1` (each binary's PDB by GUID + age: checks, copies, zips) for the release symbols ([`SYMBOLS.md`](doc/agentmaster/SYMBOLS.md)). The **only** CI in the repo; Windows Terminal's upstream Azure-Pipelines / OneBranch under `build/pipelines/` is Microsoft-internal and never runs for this fork.

### Runtime state dir = the ACTIVE PROFILE

- (PROFILES.md; the release/unpackaged default `%USERPROFILE%\.agentmaster\`; dev-package default `%USERPROFILE%\.agentmaster-dev\`; resolution: env `AGENTMASTER_PROFILE` > the exe-side `profile.path` pointer (a PORTABLE copy's remembered choice, relative-when-inside-the-unzip) > `.portable` marker fallback (`<exedir>\profile`, un-chosen portable) > the `%USERPROFILE%\.agentmaster.profiles` per-install choice file > the per-identity default; an INSTALLED first launch auto-selects silently, a PORTABLE first launch first asks where Agentmaster should LIVE — install to `~/.agentmaster\bin` / a picked folder / here, keep it portable, or ask later (PROFILES.md §2b; the exe-side `install.path` decision file) — and a kept-portable copy then prompts — Portable / Default / Development / Browse… (PROFILES.md §2a) — and everyone changes it from the cog's PROFILE row, applied on restart).
- Contents: `hooks-settings.json` + `agentmaster-hook.ps1` (the shared hooks config Claude is pointed at via `--settings`), `hooks.log` + `autorunner.log` + `scanner.log` (engine traces — `hooks.log` carries the hook event stream [`[SessionStart]`/`[Stop]`/…], the engine-mechanism tags [`[fork]`/`[resume]`/`[rehome]`/`[spawn]`/`[archive]`/`[restore-fresh]`/…], the `[observer]` census, **and the `[nav]` USER-NAVIGATION AUDIT TRAIL** — `grep '\[nav\]' hooks.log` reconstructs the user's whole journey; `autorunner.log` the Prompt Queue Runner's decisions [`[send]`/`[advance-skip]`/`[enter-retry]`/…]; `scanner.log` the PULL reconciler's `[recon-*]` synths, `[decay-waiting]` and the transcript watcher's `[watch]`; see the *Logging & observability* bullet above for which tag lands where), `forwarder-errors.log` (the hook forwarder's local silent-drop trace — a delivery that never reached the bridge: no sid / no pipe / a dead pipe's connect timeout; the bridge-side hooks.log only sees lines that ARRIVED), `sessions.json` (persisted fleet), `templates.json` (saved plans), `recent-dirs.json` (path-picker MRU), `model-list.json` (the "Specify a model..." droplist — every model id specified in that prompt, newest first, plus whatever its "Fetch models" last loaded; that button REPLACES the list, and the configured `launchModels` are never in it — they are merged in from settings at display time — plus its `last` key: the id last committed in the prompt, which every prompt opens pre-filled with; a Fetch keeps it), `dir-colors.json` (the **permanent** per-working-directory tab color map, schema **v2** — both user picks and auto-assigned colors, so a folder keeps its color across restarts), `session-store/<sid>.json` (the durable per-session KV — the `title` / `favorite` / `tags` / `draft`+`draftAtUnixMs` keys, the last pair being the unsent input-box draft + when it was last seen — PENDING_INPUT.md §8c — and `launchArgs`, the session's flattened preset / model snapshot, HARNESSES.md §7), `launch-presets.json` (the PRESETS list: the user's presets with their sparse typed fields, provider overrides + custom providers, and the seeded-template record — HARNESSES.md; never a key: typed keys live in Windows Credential Manager, target `Agentmaster:provider:<id>`), `launch-settings/<preset>-<hash>.json` (a preset launch's content-addressed `--settings` file: the hooks + the compiled env block; immutable, shared by identical launches, not cleaned up yet), `tags.json` (the **bookmark-tag registry** — the durable CI-deduped name list that keeps a 0-carrier tag alive until its ✕ deletes it), `tag-colors.json` (the tag color picker's folded-name → `#AARRGGBB` map), `sessions-index/<sid>.json` (the Sessions browser's per-session search/stats sidecar cache — `(size,mtime)`-keyed, incrementally re-accumulated from the stored byte offset), `settings.json` (the Settings cog's `AppSettings`, incl. the updater's `allowUpdatePrerelease` / `updateSkippedVersion` / `updatePostponedUntilUnixMs` keys), `am-update.cmd` + `am-update.ps1` (the in-app updater's baked-in installer, written here on "Update now"; *Updater.h*), `harness/` (the agent harness, SDK_HARNESS.md §6: `<name>.json` = a hosted conversation's record, `<name>.view.json` = the AI search's last shown list, `<name>\system-prompt.md` = the prompt file of the running child; today the one name is `sessions-search`), `windows/<id>.json` (per-window UI-state records — one file per window; captured + autosaved + restored), `open-windows.json` (the open-at-exit manifest — the live window-id set the next launch reopens), `bridge.json` (live-bridge discovery for the shim), `shim/` (the transparent `claude` PATH shim — `claude.cmd` + a POSIX `claude` — that auto-wires hand-typed sessions; see *Adopt any `claude`*), and `locks/` (the `build-launch` mutex — `tools/am-lock.sh`; see Deploy & run → *Concurrency lock*; the lock dir stays at the FIXED `%USERPROFILE%\.agentmaster\locks\` — it guards the ONE build tree, not a profile), and `terminal/` (Terminal's OWN settings.json + state.json — the `AGENTMASTER_PROFILE` redirect in `GetBaseSettingsPath`, seeded from the install's previous location on first profile claim).
- Deliberately NOT under `%LOCALAPPDATA%` — see Gotchas (MSIX).

## Integration points (1.24 pluggable pane-content model)

- New tab content implements **`IPaneContent`** (like `ScratchpadContent` — no `.idl`). Ours: `AgentManagerContent`, compiled via `src/cascadia/TerminalApp/TerminalAppLib.vcxproj`.
- Content dispatch by type string in `TerminalPage::_MakePane` (`TerminalPage.cpp`): we add an `else if (paneType == L"agentManager")` branch → `make_self<AgentManagerContent>()`.
- The Agent Manager is a **PAGE, not a tab** (it was the pinned, non-closable, non-movable tab 0 until 2026-09-25). **`TerminalPage::_CreateAgentManagerPage()`** (from `_OnFirstLayout`, before the side panel) builds the window's ONE `AgentManagerContent`, owned by the `_managerPage` member for the window's life and never inserted into `_tabs` — so every pinned-tab guard the old tab needed (the index-0 insert clamp, `_updateAllTabCloseButtons` skip, `_PinManagerTabFirst`, the `_TryMoveTab` / drag / dock floors, the rename and middle-click guards, `Tab::DisableCloseAndMoveMenuItems` / `DisableTabRename`) is gone. The tab strip's **Home** button (`TabRowControl.xaml` `ManagerHomeButton`) calls `_ShowManagerPage`, which swaps the page's root into `_tabContent` (the same host a tab's content uses) and deselects the strip; `_managerPageVisible` is the authority every "is the Manager on screen?" check reads (`_GetFocusedTabIndex` → `nullopt`, `_UpdateManagerSelectionHighlight`, `_RevealClickedSessionTab`, `_ManagerPaneNavPreviewKeyDown`, `Title()`, `_updateThemeColors`). See the *Agent Manager page* section at the top.
  - *Zero tabs is a legal state now*: an Agentmaster window with no tab shows its page instead of closing (`_RemoveTab`, `_CompleteInitialization` — which must still raise `Initialized`, or the window stays cloaked). The upstream paths that assumed ≥1 tab are guarded (`_SelectTab`, `_SelectNextTab`, `_TryMoveTab`, `_WindowSizeChanged`), and `_UpdateTabView` keeps the tab row visible while a page exists (Home must stay reachable).
- Tab placement primitive: `_CreateNewTabFromPane(pane, insertPosition)` (`TabManagement.cpp`).

### Engine wiring (`TerminalPage`):

- `_InitAgentmasterEngine()` (from `_OnFirstLayout`, before the Manager page is built) **consumes the process-wide `::Agentmaster::SharedEngine()`** — copies the shared `SessionRegistry` + `HooksBridge` + `Scheduler` + `SessionScanner` + `ProcessObserver` `shared_ptr`s and registers THIS window's adoption handler + liveness probe (tokens, detached in `~TerminalPage`).
- The once-per-process wiring (the logging / scheduler / persistence observers, the pipe, bridge discovery + hook files + the PATH shim, the **`AM_SESSION`** mint + export, and the `ProcessObserver` start) lives in `Engine.cpp` and runs on first access.
- The Fleet Observer's UI lane is `_ObserverProbe()` (ticked by the scanner's liveness probe alongside `_ReconcileClaudeTabs` + `_SweepClaudeLiveness`; it replaced `_DiscoverClaudeTabsByCwd`): publish this window's tab roster, then bind via the observer's Correlation table.
- `~TerminalPage` calls `_observer->UnpublishWindow(_windowId)` (Rule #10).
- `_WireAgentManagerContent()` hands the content the shared registry + spawn / activate / archive / restore / rename / adopt-external / **codex-launch** / pause / confirm callbacks + the cog's settings seed/persist (`SetSettings`/`SetSettingsHandler`).
- `_LaunchClaudeSession(dir, title, restored)` builds a claude `ConptyConnection` (cmdline/cwd/env ours) and opens it as a normal terminal tab via `_MakePane(args, …, existingConnection)`; `_SpawnClaudeSession` = fresh, `_RestoreClaudeSessions()` = load the persisted fleet **as Archived** (process-once via the `Engine::restoreMutex` load barrier — a 2nd window blocks until it's loaded, then skips), `_RestoreArchivedSession()` = the on-demand resume.
- **Codex mirrors these seams (lifecycle + state only — NO injector/Prompt Queue Runner):** `_LaunchCodexSession(dir, title, restored, forkFromCodexUuid)` builds a `codex` / `codex resume <uuid>` / `codex fork <uuid>` `ConptyConnection` (`BuildCodexCommandline`, launcher resolved to a FULL PATH — `ResolveCodexLauncher` / `Engine::codexExePath`, a `.cmd`/`.bat` run via `cmd /c`; `AM_SESSION` stamp, no `CCMGR_*`) and opens it as a normal tab; `_SpawnCodexSession` = fresh, Restore + `_RestoreWindowTabs` re-launch a Codex record by `SessionInfo::kind`, and `_AdoptExternalCodex(pid,cwd, fork)` forks (`codex fork`) or resumes an external codex's rollout (the Fork-a-copy vs Resume-anyway choice).
- The launch bar's **Claude⇄Codex toggle** retargets the cwd box's Launch button to `_SpawnCodexSession`.
- A managed codex's rollout state (the C2 deriver) is folded onto the registry by the UI lane's `_ReconcileManagedCodex` (which also fills `codexSessionId` + `tabToken` on first prompt) — `ObserveClaude` is never used for it (Rule #13).
- `sessionId → Tab` lives in `_claudeTabs` (per window) for Activate / Archive / retitle.
- A session's **title is one value** (Explorer name == tab title == persisted `SessionInfo.title`): `_LaunchClaudeSession` **pins** it onto the tab (`Tab::SetTabText`); an Explorer rename routes through the `rename` callback → `_RenameClaudeSession` (registry + tab in lockstep); a WT tab rename (double-click / right-click **Rename Tab** / `renameTab` action, all via `Tab::SetTabText`) flows back through `_UpdateTitle` → `_SyncClaudeTitleFromTab`, which writes the registry (Rule #11).
- `_LaunchClaudeSession`/`_AdoptExternalSession` also **smart-name** an untitled session (`DeriveSessionTitle`) and **color the tab per working dir** (`_ApplyDirColorToTab` — the dir's persisted color, else `AssignDirAutoColor`'s collision-free auto color, which is then **persisted** so the folder→color mapping is permanent); a user color change flows `Tab::SetRuntimeTabColor` → the new `Tab::TabColorChanged` event → `_OnClaudeTabColorChanged`, which persists it to `dir-colors.json` and recolors every live tab in that dir (Rule #12).

### Shared stdin:

- the registry holds a per-session injector bound to that session's `ConptyConnection::WriteInput`, so the user's keystrokes and the scheduler's prompts both reach the same `claude.exe` stdin (Correctness Rule #3 binds the injector to the id).

## Building FAST

- This machine: **i9-13900K — 32 threads (8 P + 16 E cores), 64 GB RAM.**
- The stock `Invoke-OpenConsoleBuild` is slow because it runs msbuild with **no `/m`** (projects build serially → 31 threads idle), builds the **whole** solution (tests/tools/samples), and re-runs `nuget restore` every call.
- Per-file `/MP` is already enabled (`src/common.build.pre.props:145`).

> ⚠️ **Hold the `build-launch` mutex before any full exe build, launch, or deploy** — see
> Deploy & run → *Concurrency lock*. (A lib-only compile-check, #6, doesn't relink the running
> exe and so needs no lock.)

> 🚀 **THE single biggest lever — skip the `.appxsym` symbol package (the wrapper now does, by
> default).** A binlog `PerformanceSummary` of a normal Debug package build measured
> `GenerateAppxSymbolPackage` at **~156s of a ~204s build = 77%**: it zips every full Debug PDB
> (hundreds of MB) into a Microsoft-Store symbol-upload bundle we **never consume** — Agentmaster
> ships the `.msix` + portable zips via GitHub, not the Store, and `release.yml` only ever uses the
> `.msix`. The PDBs still emit next to the binaries, so local debugging is unaffected; it is a FIXED
> ~156s tax paid on every build regardless of how much source changed. `Build-Agentmaster.ps1` now
> passes `/p:AppxSymbolPackageEnabled=false` by default, dropping the same build to **~20–50s (4–10×,
> measured)**; a RAW `msbuild` invocation must add the flag itself. Opt back in with
> `-WithSymbolPackage` only when you genuinely need a Store symbol bundle.

1. **Every build writes to Q:, never to K: (the user's rule, 2026-10-19).** `K:` is a nearly full,
   DRAM-less SATA SSD (a WT build is tens of thousands of tiny files and 32 threads thrash it); `Q:`
   is a Kingston Fury Renegade Gen4 NVMe with DRAM and ~250 GB free. Only the source tree stays on
   K:. The setup (checked 2026-10-19):
   - **Package layouts** go to `Q:\agentmaster-layouts\<Release | Release-B | Debug | Debug-B>`,
     always through `-LayoutDir`: the instance's pair, the folder it does NOT run from (see *Hot
     deploy*). The old K: layout slots under `src\cascadia\CascadiaPackage\bin\x64\` were deleted on
     2026-10-19.
   - **Build trees are junctions to Q:**, so every build writes there, with or without
     `-LayoutDir`, through the wrapper, raw msbuild or VS: `obj\x64\Debug` →
     `Q:\tmp\agentmaster-dev-build`, `obj\x64\Release` → `Q:\tmp\agentmaster-release-build`, `bin` →
     `Q:\tmp\agentmaster-bin`, `src\cascadia\CascadiaPackage\bin` → `Q:\tmp\agentmaster-package-bin`,
     `…\CascadiaPackage\obj` → `Q:\tmp\agentmaster-package-obj`, `…\CascadiaPackage\AppPackages` →
     `Q:\tmp\agentmaster-apppackages`.
   - **Still on K:**, by design: the `Generated Files` folders of the source tree (~1.1 GB, shared by
     both configurations and deleted by purges), `packages\` (the NuGet input, ~0.9 GB) and
     `obj\x64\vcpkg` (the installed vcpkg packages, ~70 MB).
   - **Before a build**, check the junctions (`cmd /c dir /AL K:\source\Agentmaster`, `…\obj\x64`,
     `…\src\cascadia\CascadiaPackage`). A missing one puts that build's output on K:; recreate it
     with `New-Item -ItemType Junction -Path <K: path> -Target <Q: folder>`, after moving to Q: what
     a build wrote at the K: path. Remove a junction only with `cmd /c rmdir <path>`, which deletes
     the link and never its target. A new build folder (a configuration, platform or project output
     that does not exist yet) gets its Q: folder and its junction before its first build.

2. **Use the wrapper** — parallel `/m` + per-file `/MP`, scoped to just the app target
   (`Terminal\CascadiaPackage`), skips the redundant double restore on rebuilds:
   ```powershell
   pwsh -ExecutionPolicy Bypass -File .\tools\Build-Agentmaster.ps1            # first build
   pwsh -ExecutionPolicy Bypass -File .\tools\Build-Agentmaster.ps1 -NoRestore # inner loop
   ```
   Raw equivalent (note the appxsym-skip flag — the wrapper adds it for you):
   `msbuild OpenConsole.slnx /m /p:Configuration=Debug /p:Platform=x64 /p:AppxSymbolPackageEnabled=false /t:Terminal\CascadiaPackage /v:m`
   64 GB handles unbounded `/m`; if it ever pages, add `-ClMpCount 6`.

3. **Windows Defender exclusions** (Admin, once — 20–40% on cold builds):
   ```powershell
   Add-MpPreference -ExclusionPath (Resolve-Path .)
   'MSBuild.exe','cl.exe','link.exe','cppwinrt.exe','midl.exe','mc.exe','VBCSCompiler.exe','nuget.exe','tracker.exe' |
     ForEach-Object { Add-MpPreference -ExclusionProcess $_ }
   ```

4. **Iterate incrementally.** The cold build (restore + cppwinrt projection) is the
   expensive one; afterwards `-NoRestore` rebuilds (our edits touch only `TerminalApp`)
   are quick thanks to MSBuild's up-to-date check. Reference times on this box, **with the
   default appxsym-skip**: a typical code-change deploy rebuild is **~20–50s** (a 1-TU change
   that doesn't touch a hot header validated at **20.5s**); a wide recompile (a hot header like
   `ProcessInspect.h`, included by dozens of TUs) is bounded by `CL` (~15s) + `Link`/`Lib`
   (~16s). The remaining variable cost is **header
   fan-out** — tightening a hot engine header shrinks the recompile set.

5. **Optional — MSBuildCache** for clean-rebuild / branch-switch cache hits: add
   `-p:MsBuildCacheEnabled=true` (uses file copies, not hardlinks).

6. **Compile-check without relinking the exe.** `TerminalAppLib` is a **static lib**, so you
   can validate code changes (and catch all our compile errors) while the app is still
   running — build just the lib (after `vcvars64.bat`):
   ```
   msbuild src\cascadia\TerminalApp\TerminalAppLib.vcxproj /m /p:Configuration=Debug /p:Platform=x64 /p:SolutionDir=K:\source\Agentmaster\
   ```
   `/p:SolutionDir=` (trailing `\`) is **required** when building a `.vcxproj` directly —
   otherwise `$(SolutionDir)build\rules\*.targets` imports fail (MSB4019). The full exe link
   is `msbuild OpenConsole.slnx /m /p:Configuration=Debug /p:Platform=x64 /p:AppxSymbolPackageEnabled=false /t:Terminal\CascadiaPackage`
   (**~20–50s with the appxsym-skip flag**; without it ~3–3.5 min, dominated by the symbol-package
   zip — SolutionDir is implicit for the `.slnx`).

## Deploy & run

- A packaged app can't be launched by running `Agentmaster.exe` (the GUI binary — the renamed WindowsTerminal-project output) directly (WT #926/#4043); it must be deployed.
- Deploy the **loose layout** (what VS F5 does) — no signing/cert/admin:

```powershell
# first registration on a machine only: register a loose layout built into a Q: folder (every build
# writes to Q:, Building FAST #1). After that a new build is always SWAPPED in (the hot deploy below
# moves dev between Q:\agentmaster-layouts\Debug and Debug-B), never registered by hand.
Add-AppxPackage -Register "Q:\agentmaster-layouts\Debug\AppxManifest.xml" -ForceUpdateFromAnyVersion
```

- Launch any of these ways (the loose layout registers the **DEV identity**, `AgentmasterDev` — the release MSIX owns `Agentmaster`/`agentmaster`; see PROFILES.md):

- execution alias: **`agentmasterdev`**
- Start menu: **“Agentmaster Dev”**
- `Start-Process "shell:appsFolder\AgentmasterDev_56k4f06dsfp9r!App"`

- ⚠️ **One-time migration on this machine** (first deploy after the identity split): the old loose registration still owns `Agentmaster_56k4f06dsfp9r` (now the RELEASE family) and its on-disk manifest no longer matches it — `Remove-AppxPackage Agentmaster_56k4f06dsfp9r`, then register as above.
- First launch then shows the **profile picker** (current data lives in `~/.agentmaster`: either Browse… to it, or pick Development + the migrate checkbox to copy it to `~/.agentmaster-dev`).
- PROFILES.md §3 has the full recipe.

### Concurrency lock (multi-agent) — REQUIRED before any build, launch, or deploy

- There is exactly ONE dev instance and ONE build tree (`bin` + `obj`, junctions to Q: — Building FAST #1), so the close→build→relaunch cycle is **process-global and destructive**.
- Two actors running it at once — two AI agents, or an agent + a human — collide: one closes the instance the other just launched, two msbuilds race on the same outputs, and the **exe link fails because a running `Agentmaster.exe` locks the very `Agentmaster.exe` being relinked**.
- **REQUIREMENT: hold the global `build-launch` mutex for the WHOLE cycle before you build (full exe), launch, deploy, OR close our instance.**
- (A lib-only compile-check — Building FAST #6 — doesn't relink the running exe, so it needs no lock.) The mutex is a filesystem lock (`tools/am-lock.sh`, built on `mkdir(2)` atomicity) under `%USERPROFILE%\.agentmaster\locks\`, so it **persists across separate Bash calls** (each Bash tool call is a fresh shell) and is shared by every agent.

```bash
TOKEN=$(bash tools/am-lock.sh acquire --wait 600 --label "deploy $(git rev-parse --short HEAD)") || exit 1
# ... entire close → build → relaunch cycle while holding $TOKEN ...
bash tools/am-lock.sh release --token "$TOKEN"
```

- `acquire` prints the token to **stdout** (capture it) and logs to stderr; `--wait 600` **queues** behind another agent's build (picking up within ~1s of its release) instead of failing.
- Without `--wait`, a contended `acquire` exits **3 (`BUSY`)** with the holder printed — back off, don't spin.
- A crashed holder can't wedge it: a lock past its TTL (default 30 min) is auto-broken on the next `acquire`.
- Token-checked `release` means one agent can't drop another's lock.
- Helpers: `status` (who holds it + age), `refresh --token T` (extend a long hold), `acquire --force` (steal now), `with --wait 600 -- <cmd>` (acquire → run one command → release).
- `bash tools/am-lock.sh --help`.

### Inner loop.

- The loose layout is live (binaries update in place), but you **cannot relink `Agentmaster.exe` while the app is running** — it locks the exe.
- So: close *our* dev instance (spare the Store WT), rebuild, relaunch. The **hot deploy** below avoids the close: it builds while dev runs, and its only downtime is a swap of seconds.
- ⚠ **Dev can run from either of TWO layout folders** (`Q:\agentmaster-layouts\Debug` or `Debug-B`), because the hot deploy alternates them. Read `(Get-AppxPackage AgentmasterDev).InstallLocation` first; the recipe below closes and builds THAT folder (`$devDir`). A hard-coded folder is a silent no-op while dev runs from the other one: the close finds nothing, the build writes the unregistered folder, and the relaunch starts the old binaries.
- **Building, deploying, and installing require the user's permission — always ask first** (see *Development Rules*; the prior "always auto deploy, run without prompting" standing authorization is **revoked**).
- Just never touch the Store WT (it's not under our path — see Gotchas).
- **Hold the `build-launch` mutex around the whole cycle** (acquire at step 0, release at step 4 — see *Concurrency lock*).

```bash
# 0. acquire the global mutex — REQUIRED (queues behind another agent's build)
TOKEN=$(bash tools/am-lock.sh acquire --wait 600 --label "deploy $(git rev-parse --short HEAD)") || exit 1
```

```powershell
# 0b. the folder dev is REGISTERED from: Q:\agentmaster-layouts\Debug or Debug-B (the hot deploy
#     below alternates them). Every path filter and the build target below use it.
$devDir = (Get-AppxPackage AgentmasterDev).InstallLocation.TrimEnd('\')
# 1. close ONLY our dev instance — the GUI PROCESS ONLY (path filter spares the Store WT AND the
#    release layout under Q:\agentmaster-layouts\Release / Release-B — see Gotchas). ⚠ NEVER Stop-Process OpenConsole.exe:
#    a ConPTY host killed outright skips its CTRL_CLOSE broadcast, so that tab's pwsh + claude.exe
#    (+ MCP node children) live on forever with a DEAD console (35 such pairs + ~140
#    children from ONE such kill, alive six days — OBSERVER.md §13a). Killing the GUI alone closes
#    its pipe handles; every OpenConsole then ends its clients itself and exits.
#    The trailing '\*' keeps ...\Debug from matching ...\Debug-B.
Get-CimInstance Win32_Process -Filter "Name='Agentmaster.exe' OR Name='WindowsTerminal.exe'" |
  ? { $_.ExecutablePath -like "$devDir\*" } | % { Stop-Process -Id $_.ProcessId -Force }
# (the GUI exe is Agentmaster.exe since the rename; WindowsTerminal.exe stays in the filter for a
#  STILL-RUNNING pre-rename instance during the transition deploy)
# 1b. WAIT for the layout's ConPTY hosts to drain before relinking (they lock OpenConsole.exe; each
#     ends its clients — ≤5 s per stubborn client — then exits; a host still alive after ~30 s is wedged)
$deadline = (Get-Date).AddSeconds(30)
do { Start-Sleep -Milliseconds 500; $hosts = Get-CimInstance Win32_Process -Filter "Name='OpenConsole.exe'" |
  ? { $_.ExecutablePath -like "$devDir\*" } } while ($hosts -and (Get-Date) -lt $deadline)
# 2. build INTO the registered folder (full exe link; the wrapper skips the ~156s appxsym by default —
#    see Building FAST). -LayoutDir sends the layout to the registered Q: folder; without it the
#    wapproj's default folder (src\cascadia\CascadiaPackage\bin\x64\Debug, a junction into
#    Q:\tmp\agentmaster-package-bin) gets it, and dev never sees the new binaries.
pwsh -File .\tools\Build-Agentmaster.ps1 -NoRestore -LayoutDir $devDir      # or: msbuild OpenConsole.slnx /t:Terminal\CascadiaPackage /m /p:Configuration=Debug /p:Platform=x64 /p:AppxSymbolPackageEnabled=false /p:AgentmasterLayoutDir=$devDir
# 3. relaunch
Start-Process "shell:appsFolder\AgentmasterDev_56k4f06dsfp9r!App"   # or: agentmasterdev
```

```bash
# 4. release the mutex (always — even if a step above failed)
bash tools/am-lock.sh release --token "$TOKEN"
```

- Re-register **only** when `Package-Dev.appxmanifest` changes.
- (VS F5 on `CascadiaPackage` also builds + deploys.) Runtime/session state lives in the dev install's **profile** (default `%USERPROFILE%\.agentmaster-dev\`, or wherever the picker pointed it — check `%USERPROFILE%\.agentmaster.profiles`); tail `hooks.log` there to confirm the engine is live (`[engine] bridge listening …`) and that spawned sessions' hooks arrive (`[SessionStart]`, `[Stop]`, …).

### Hot deploy of the local RELEASE install (build while it runs)

- The registered release install (`Agentmaster`) runs from one of two **layout folders** on Q:: `Q:\agentmaster-layouts\Release` or `Release-B` (until 2026-10-12 the K: slots `…\CascadiaPackage\bin\x64\Release\` and `Release-B\`, deleted on 2026-10-19 — every build writes to Q:, Building FAST #1). Read `(Get-AppxPackage Agentmaster).InstallLocation`; never assume one.
- **A layout can also live outside the repo, on another drive.** `-LayoutDir` takes any folder, the swap registers whatever folder it is given, and K: is chronically short of space. Since 2026-10-12 production runs from Q: (first **`Q:\agentmaster-layouts\Release`**, 0.6.8.19, the user's request; the first registration from Q: took 2.2 s at the first attempt), and since 2026-10-13 dev runs from Q: too. Each instance alternates between two folders there: dev between **`Q:\agentmaster-layouts\Debug`** and **`Debug-B`**, production between **`Release`** and **`Release-B`** (since 2026-10-17 production runs 0.6.8.20 from **`Release-B`**: the Release build of 2026-10-14, staged and checked that day; its binaries name dd8e97e35, which is 07d9c3f25 on the branch since the 2026-10-15 rebase onto origin/agentmaster, but the build does NOT hold the two upstream commits that the rebase put before it, 3b03d07b0 + 7248d2592; `Release` kept 0.6.8.19, the rollback target, until 2026-10-17 11:53, when a purged Release build of 46e3aad42 replaced it for the production swap of MESSAGING.md §21.12.5 — the Telegram feature and everything on the branch, those two commits included; since 2026-10-17 12:01 production runs that build as 0.6.8.21 from `Release`; since 2026-10-19 `Release-B` holds a staged Release build of ec966a2ca instead of 0.6.8.20). Read the InstallLocation before every build: the next build targets the folder the instance does NOT run from, and the folder it left holds the previous build, the rollback target. The K: slots are gone: deleted on 2026-10-19 with dev 0.0.1.5 and production 0.6.8.18 in them, when every build moved to Q: (Building FAST #1).
- A running instance locks only its own layout (loaded images + the mapped `resources.pri`), never the intermediate `bin\x64\<Config>\<Project>\` / `obj\` trees. Building the package layout into the slot it is NOT registered from (`tools\Build-Agentmaster.ps1 … -LayoutDir <slot>` → `/p:AgentmasterLayoutDir`, which `CascadiaPackage.wapproj` turns into DesktopBridge's `OutputPath` before its props import) therefore needs no close.
- The icon files follow the slot too: `CopyAgentmasterImagesToLayout` copies `res\terminal\images-Dev` into `$(TargetDir)Images\`. Until 2026-10-02 it hard-coded `bin\x64\<Config>\`, so slot B never had them. A layout without its `Images\` registers and runs, but its taskbar and Start icons are blank; production ran that way from slot B, and its taskbar button showed only the unread-count disc. The swap now refuses a layout that is missing an icon file its manifest names.
- The only downtime is the swap, `tools\Swap-AgentmasterLayout.ps1 -LayoutDir <slot>`: it stamps the registered version +1 revision, stops the GUI only (never `OpenConsole.exe`), waits for the ConPTY hosts to drain, re-registers, launches, and reports the measured downtime. The register cannot succeed while any process of the old package lives (`0x80073D02`), and a workspace with many live tabs drains slowly (72 hosts took ~87 s on 2026-10-01), so the script drains for up to 120 s, then retries the register while the old package still reads in use. Only after that does it relaunch the old version. Rolling back is the same script pointed at the previous slot.
- Still hold the `build-launch` mutex across build + swap, and refresh it while a long build runs. Full runbook: the `build-install` skill §4a. Every switch, refusal and output of the swap script: *The swap script* below.

### Hot deploy of the DEV instance (build while it runs)

- The same mechanism and the same script serve the dev identity. Dev's two folders are `Q:\agentmaster-layouts\Debug` and `Debug-B` (since 2026-10-13; the K: slots `…\bin\x64\Debug\` and `Debug-B\` were deleted on 2026-10-19; see the bullet above).
- Build the Debug package into the slot dev is NOT registered from (`tools\Build-Agentmaster.ps1 -NoRestore -LayoutDir <slot>`, no `-ReleaseIdentity`), then swap: `tools\Swap-AgentmasterLayout.ps1 -LayoutDir <slot> -PackageName AgentmasterDev`. The swap bumps the revision (0.0.1.1 → 0.0.1.2) and refuses a release-identity layout.
- Run the swap from a shell OUTSIDE dev (it refuses otherwise): the release instance, Windows Terminal or a plain console.
- First run (2026-10-05, dev 0.0.1.1 @ `Debug` → 0.0.1.2 @ `Debug-B`, 42 tabs, 16 live claudes, all at rest): a purged build ran for 295 s (`-MaxCpu 4 -ClMpCount 6`) while dev kept serving. The swap's downtime was **8.2 s**: the 16 ConPTY hosts drained by themselves in 5.8 s, and the register took 2.1 s at the first attempt. The window restore then re-homed all 42 tabs.
- After a swap, dev runs from the other slot. The next deploy targets the old slot (hot), or uses the inner loop above, which follows `$devDir`.

### The swap script — `tools\Swap-AgentmasterLayout.ps1`

- **What it does:** it moves an installed loose registration (`Agentmaster` or `AgentmasterDev`) to a freshly built layout folder, with seconds of downtime. It is the swap half of a hot deploy. The build half is `tools\Build-Agentmaster.ps1 -LayoutDir <slot>`.
- **What it does not do:** it builds nothing, and it takes no lock. Hold the `build-launch` mutex around the build AND the swap yourself.
- **Run it with PowerShell 7** (`pwsh`; the script has `#Requires -Version 7`), from a shell OUTSIDE the instance that it swaps.
- **The reference** is the script's comment-based help. `Get-Help` does not show that help (cause not found), so read the script header.

#### The six steps

Nothing stops before step 3, so a refusal in step 1 costs nothing.

1. **Validate.** The script refuses (it throws; exit code 1 when run as a script) when:
   - `-LayoutDir` has no `AppxManifest.xml`, or the manifest does not parse.
   - The manifest's Identity Name is not `-PackageName`. So a dev layout never replaces the release family, and the reverse.
   - An image that the manifest names (`Images\X.png`) is missing, both as that file and as every MakePri variant (`X.*.png`). Such a layout registers, but its taskbar and Start icons are blank.
   - `-LayoutDir` is the folder that the package already runs from.
   - The version is not newer than the registered one, or the revision would pass 65535.
   - The calling shell descends from a GUI process that the swap stops (the self-hosting guard). The stop would kill the script after step 3 and leave the package stopped and unregistered. The guard runs under `-WhatIf` too.
2. **Stamp.** It writes the new version into the layout's manifest. A build always writes `0.0.1.0`, and development-mode re-registration accepts only a newer version.
3. **Stop.** It stops only the GUI processes under the old folder. It reads their image names from the OLD layout's manifest (fallback: `Agentmaster.exe`, `WindowsTerminal.exe`). It never stops `OpenConsole.exe`.
4. **Drain.** It waits for the old folder's ConPTY hosts to exit by themselves. Each host first ends its tab's shell and claude with `CTRL_CLOSE`. The register cannot succeed while one of these hosts lives.
5. **Register.** `Add-AppxPackage -Register <manifest> -ForceUpdateFromAnyVersion` moves the InstallLocation to the new folder. On `0x80073D02` (the old package is still in use) it tries again every 3 s.
6. **Launch.** It starts `shell:appsFolder\<PFN>!<AppId>` and waits for the new GUI process under the new folder.

#### Parameters

| Parameter | Default | Effect |
|---|---|---|
| `-LayoutDir <folder>` | (required) | The built layout folder to switch to. A relative path resolves against the PowerShell location. |
| `-PackageName` | `Agentmaster` | `Agentmaster` (release) or `AgentmasterDev` (dev). It must be the layout manifest's Identity Name. |
| `-Version <a.b.c.d>` | the registered version, revision + 1 | The version to stamp and register. It must be newer than the registered version. Keep `Major.Minor.Build`: the in-app updater compares those three parts with the GitHub releases. |
| `-DrainTimeoutSec` | 120 (0–600) | How long to wait for the old hosts. The hosts are never killed: a killed host skips its `CTRL_CLOSE`, and its tab's pwsh + claude stay alive on a dead console (OBSERVER.md §13a). When the time runs out, the register's retries cover the remaining hosts. |
| `-RegisterRetrySec` | 120 (0–600) | How long to retry the register on `0x80073D02`, counted from the first attempt. `0` disables the retry. Any other register error rolls back at once. |
| `-LaunchTimeoutSec` | 60 (1–600) | How long to wait for the new GUI process. This affects only the report: the app starts either way. |
| `-NoLaunch` | off | Register, but do not start the app. The downtime lasts until someone starts it. |
| `-WhatIf` | off | Dry run: resolve, validate (the icons and the self-hosting guard included) and print the plan. It stamps, stops and registers nothing. |

#### Output, failure and undo

- **Progress:** one `[HH:mm:ss.fff]` line per step: `package`, `target`, `running`, `stopped`, `drained`, `register attempt N … retrying`, `registered`, `launched pid N - downtime Xs`. On a failure: `REGISTER FAILED: …` and `rollback: relaunching …`.
- **Result object** (printed last): `Package`, `OldVersion`, `OldLocation`, `NewVersion`, `NewLocation`, `StoppedPids`, `DrainSeconds`, `HostsLeft` (old hosts still alive when the drain ended), `RegisterSeconds` (the retries included), `RegisterAttempts`, `NewPid` (0 = not started, or not seen within `-LaunchTimeoutSec`), `DowntimeSeconds` (from the GUI stop to the new GUI's kernel start time; empty when unknown).
- **Rollback:** the script never changes the old folder. When the register fails with another error, or still reads "in use" after `-RegisterRetrySec`, the script starts the still-registered OLD version (unless `-NoLaunch`), then throws. So a failed swap costs one restart, not an outage.
- **Undo** a successful swap: run the script again with `-LayoutDir` set to the previous slot. That folder gets the next version number, like any swap.

#### What a swap ends, and who can run it

- **The stop ends every claude of that instance:** each host sends `CTRL_CLOSE` to its tab. The window restore then re-homes the tabs, and each Claude tab resumes its conversation (dormant until focused). A turn in flight is cut off. Before a swap, make sure that no session is Running: `agentmaster sessions` (production) or `agentmaster sessions --instance dev`. Read the state (`derivedState` in `--json`), not `turnInFlight`: that flag reads only the transcript tail (`agentcli.cpp`: the last stop reason is not terminal and no interrupt follows), so it is true on some sessions whose own heartbeat reads idle (11 of the 66 at-rest sessions on 2026-10-09).
- **Walk your shell's ancestry before a swap.** A session that runs inside production cannot run the release swap, and a session inside dev cannot run the dev swap: the self-hosting guard refuses. Run the other instance's swap yourself, and give the user the command for the swap of your own host. The user runs it from Windows Terminal, from the other instance, or from a plain console.
- **When the user tells you to run the swap of your own host anyway, start it through a one-shot scheduled task** (`Register-ScheduledTask` with `-LogonType Interactive -RunLevel Limited`, then `Start-ScheduledTask`; the script deletes its task as its first step). The Task Scheduler service becomes the script's parent, so the guard passes and the stop cannot kill it, and it runs on the user's desktop, where it can relaunch the app. A plain `Start-Process` does not work: the child descends from the session that the stop ends, and shares its job object. The script must wait until your own turn has ended (no session `Running`, checked through `derivedState`) before it stops the GUI, because your session ends with the stop. First run: 2026-10-09, dev 0.0.1.4 → 0.0.1.5 (see the table below).
- **The same task can run the build too**, so that no step depends on your session: on 2026-10-13 one task built dev (`Build-Agentmaster.ps1 -LayoutDir`, 167 s), checked the layout, released the mutex, waited for the starting session's turn to end, took the mutex again, and swapped. While your turn is still open, you can watch the build and check the layout yourself; end the turn when the build is ready. A task starts from the registry environment, not from an Agentmaster tab, so it gets no PATH compaction: shorten a PATH over 7,167 characters before the build (vcvars runs through cmd.exe; see the PATH-length Gotcha). Call Git Bash by its full path for `am-lock.sh`, because a bare `bash` can resolve to WSL's.

#### Traps

- **Never register a fresh build by hand.** Its manifest says `0.0.1.0`. Swap it.
- **`-WhatIf` noise:** it prints a `What if: Performing the operation "Set Alias"` line for each alias. Filter these lines out.
- **The new instance can stop on the "Reopen your N previous Agentmaster windows?" dialog.** The measured downtime ends when the new GUI starts, not when someone answers. Someone must click it.
- **After a swap, the instance runs from the OTHER slot.** The next build targets the slot that it left.

#### The build half, and both instances in one deploy
