- **`Build-Agentmaster.ps1 -LayoutDir <folder>`** writes ONLY the package layout to that folder (`/p:AgentmasterLayoutDir`). Every other project keeps its usual `bin\` and `obj\`. It registers nothing. The switches that matter for a swap build:
  - `-NoRestore` (the packages exist already);
  - `-Configuration Release -ReleaseIdentity` for production. Without `-ReleaseIdentity` the layout is `AgentmasterDev`, and the swap refuses it for `Agentmaster`;
  - `-MaxCpu N` / `-ClMpCount N` to cap the parallel work under memory pressure (`4` / `6` with ~80 live claudes);
  - `-WithSymbolPackage` only when a Store symbol bundle is necessary (the `.appxsym` costs ~156 s, and it is off by default).
- **To deploy both instances** ("build release and debug for swap"):
  1. Walk your shell's ancestry, and acquire the mutex.
  2. Read both InstallLocations. Each build targets the slot that its instance does NOT run from.
  3. Apply the purge rule (the `build-install` skill §5.8) to each configuration. Its baseline is the time of `bin\x64\<Config>\TerminalApp\TerminalApp.dll`, the last build of that configuration.
  4. Build Release (`-Configuration Release -ReleaseIdentity -LayoutDir <release slot>`), THEN Debug (`-LayoutDir <dev slot>`). Never run the two builds at the same time: both write the same `src\cascadia\TerminalApp\Generated Files` folder (one flat folder for both configurations), and two builds double the memory load.
  5. Swap each instance from a shell outside it (`-PackageName AgentmasterDev` for dev), then release the mutex.

## Releasing a public version

### Only when the user agrees / requests it.

- Cutting a release builds, self-signs, and publishes a GitHub Release — it is outward-facing, so confirm the **version** with the user first and don't do it on your own initiative.
- (Local dev deploys via *Deploy & run* above; this is the public path.)
- Two GitHub Actions workflows are the repo's only CI:

#### `.github/workflows/ci.yml`

- — build-only gate on `pull_request` + push to `agentmaster`/`main` (+ `workflow_dispatch`): restores vcpkg + nuget (cached) and builds `Terminal\CascadiaPackage` x64 Release.
- No packaging / signing / release.
- It builds and checks the release SYMBOLS exactly as `release.yml` does (2026-10-23, [`SYMBOLS.md`](doc/agentmaster/SYMBOLS.md)): the same `/SOURCELINK` + `/PDBSTRIPPED` links, the same srcsrv indexing, and it fails when a binary we build lacks its full, indexed PDB or its public PDB (nothing is uploaded).
- **No path filter**, so a docs-only push still triggers a full ~20-min build (add `paths-ignore: ['**.md','doc/**','LICENSE']` if that matters).

#### `.github/workflows/release.yml`

- — the public-release pipeline: triggers on a **tag `v*`** push OR **`workflow_dispatch`** (a `version` input).
- No scheduled cron, no Azure, no NuGet *publish* (NuGet *restore* stays — it's a build dependency).
- A **NIGHTLY release** is still cut by tag/dispatch: tag `vX.Y.Z-prerelease-nightly` (any tag containing `nightly`; bump `X.Y.Z` — the updater compares numerically) — prep strips each version component to digits so the MSIX `Identity Version` stays numeric while the tag/release name/installer `-Version` keep the full suffix (`rawver`), and the release is **auto-marked prerelease** with a ⚠ NIGHTLY warning in its body; the in-app updater offers it ONLY to users who accepted the cog's warning-gated nightly opt-in (see the *In-app auto-updater* Status block), and `Install-Agentmaster.ps1` mirrors the tiers (`-Nightly`; `-Prerelease` alone never installs a nightly).
- **Symbols** (2026-10-23, the owner's "maximum pdbs"; [`SYMBOLS.md`](doc/agentmaster/SYMBOLS.md)): every release ships the PDB of every binary it builds. The portable zips carry each binary's PUBLIC PDB beside it (function names + addresses, ~8% of the size; written by the same link with `/PDBSTRIPPED`, listed in `.am-manifest`), and `Agentmaster_<ver4>_<arch>_symbols.zip` holds the FULL PDBs (~190 MB per arch), Source-Linked (`/SOURCELINK`, the map from `tools/New-AgentmasterSourceLink.ps1`) and srcsrv-indexed (`build/scripts/Index-Pdbs.ps1`) to the release's commit. `tools/Export-AgentmasterSymbols.ps1` matches each binary to its PDB by GUID + age and fails the run on a missing, stale or unindexed one. The build passes `/p:AgentmasterSourceLinkFile` + `/p:AgentmasterPublicPdbDir` (read only by `src/common.build.post.props`; a local build sets neither) and `/p:AppxSymbolPackageEnabled=false` (DesktopBridge's `.appxsym` was always thrown away). The msixbundle carries no PDBs. Local builds keep a PDB beside every binary (full in Release, FASTLINK in Debug) and never ship them.

### To cut a release (happy path):

```bash
# agree X.Y.Z with the user, then either tag-push (real release) ...
git tag vX.Y.Z && git push origin vX.Y.Z          # auto-triggers release.yml
# ... or dispatch a DRAFT dry-run without creating a tag:
gh workflow run release.yml --repo Nucs/Agentmaster -f version=X.Y.Z
# find + watch the run (background it; it pings on exit; --exit-status => failure is non-zero):
gh run list --repo Nucs/Agentmaster --workflow release.yml --limit 3
gh run watch <run-id> --repo Nucs/Agentmaster --exit-status
```

- The pipeline is **prep** (version from the tag/input, stamped into `Package-Rel.appxmanifest`'s `Identity Version`) → **build** matrix **x64 + arm64** (Release, `WindowsTerminalBranding=Dev` + **`/p:AgentmasterPackageIdentity=Release`** = the `Agentmaster` RELEASE identity — never the dev `AgentmasterDev` manifest, `AppxPackageSigningEnabled=false`) → **bundle** (`build/scripts/Create-AppxBundle.ps1` merges both arches → `.msixbundle`, then self-signs; `New-UnpackagedTerminalDistribution.ps1 -PortableMode:$true` makes the portable zips — TRUE portable: `.portable` marker ⇒ first launch asks WHERE Agentmaster should live — install to `~/.agentmaster\bin` (desktop icon, Start menu, folder right-click menu, Apps & Features, PATH; no admin) / a picked folder / here, keep it portable, or ask later (PROFILES.md §2b, the `<unzip>\install.path` decision) — and a kept-portable copy then which profile to use (default: the self-contained `<unzip>\profile`; also Default / Development / Browse…), remembered in the `<unzip>\profile.path` pointer; Terminal's own settings ride the chosen profile (`<profile>\terminal\`, seeded from a pre-choice `<unzip>\settings`) — PROFILES.md §2a) → **release** (`softprops/action-gh-release` creates a **DRAFT** GitHub Release with the `.msixbundle`, `Agentmaster.cer`, both portable `.zip`s (public PDBs inside) and both `_symbols.zip`s (the full PDBs)).
- It lands as a **draft** — review the assets, then **the user publishes it** (a draft creates no git tag until published; a `workflow_dispatch` draft is safe to delete).
- After publishing, refresh the release notes + README (capabilities / Download) if the feature set moved.

### Signing.

- The release self-signs with a **`CN=Agentmaster`** code-signing cert minted at runtime whose subject is **read from the manifest**, so it always equals the Publisher (this is *why* the Publisher is `CN=Agentmaster` — see Repo facts).
- Being self-signed, users must trust `Agentmaster.cer` once to install the `.msixbundle`; the **portable `.zip` needs no cert** (unzip + run `Agentmaster.exe` — its first launch offers the §2b per-user install [default `~/.agentmaster\bin`] or stays fully self-contained: settings + profile inside the unzip dir).
- To sign with a real cert instead, set repo secrets **`SIGNING_PFX_BASE64`** + **`SIGNING_PFX_PASSWORD`** (its subject must still equal the Publisher).

### One-time repo settings

- (already done for `Nucs/Agentmaster`; a fresh fork needs them — Actions is **OFF by default on forks**, and the release job needs write to create the Release):

```bash
gh api -X PUT repos/<owner>/<repo>/actions/permissions -F enabled=true -f allowed_actions=all
gh api -X PUT repos/<owner>/<repo>/actions/permissions/workflow -f default_workflow_permissions=write
```

- The pipeline runs on a stock `windows-2022` runner (UWP workload + Windows SDK + vcpkg all present); CI and release build x64+arm64 in parallel.
- On failure the build **binlog uploads as an artifact** to diagnose the first run.

## Gotchas (learned the hard way)

### `nuget.exe` can't parse `.slnx`.

- The bundled `dep\nuget\nuget.exe` errors with "file type was not recognized" on `OpenConsole.slnx`, and a bare `packages.config` restore needs `-PackagesDirectory`.
- The wrapper restores `dep\nuget\packages.config` into `packages\` and is non-fatal; use `-NoRestore` once packages exist. (Stock `Invoke-OpenConsoleBuild` has the same latent bug — it only "works" because VS already restored.)

### `Grid`/`Panel` has no `Focus(FocusState)`

- in this XAML projection — only `Control`-derived types do (this caused error C2039).
- `IPaneContent::Focus` must focus a `Control` child; `AgentManagerContent::Focus` focuses its cwd `TextBox`.

### `TextBox` has no `VerticalScrollBarVisibility`

- in this projection (WPF puts it on the TextBox; UWP doesn't) — set the **attached** `ScrollViewer.VerticalScrollBarVisibility` instead (`ScrollViewer::SetVerticalScrollBarVisibility(box, …)`; also a C2039, hit on the multiline Prompt Queue compose box).
- Related glyph-alignment quirk: a bare symbol rides differently per element — a **`TextBlock`** reserves descent space in its line box (so a centered `"!"` sits high), while a **`FontIcon`** centers the glyph's ink.
- Render compose-bar symbols as `FontIcon` (even a text-font one, e.g. `Segoe UI` glyph `"!"`) so an icon row lines up; mixing `TextBlock` + `FontIcon` siblings misaligns them.

### Building a `.vcxproj` directly needs `/p:SolutionDir=K:\source\Agentmaster\`

- (trailing `\`), else `$(SolutionDir)build\rules\*.targets` imports fail with MSB4019. The `.slnx` build sets it implicitly.
- (See Building FAST #6.)

### Imperative XAML name clashes:

- a `using namespace winrt::Windows::UI;` pulls the nested `Text` namespace into scope and collides with a `Text(...)` helper (C2872/C2882) — prefer narrow `using`-declarations (`Color`/`ColorHelper`/`Colors`).
- The `.cpp` can't run-time test here, so the compiler is the safety net; build the lib (#6) after UI edits.

### `small` is a macro under `<windows.h>` — never name a variable `small`

- `rpcndr.h` (which `<windows.h>` pulls in) has `#define small char`, so `const PixelSize small{ 6, 6 };` reads as `const PixelSize char{ 6, 6 };` and fails with C2628 / C2144 / C2059 cascades that point nowhere near a macro. It hit twice on 2026-10-06 (the m5 harness and `IslandWindow.cpp`). The same header family also defines `hyper`, and `<windows.h>` defines `near`, `far` and `interface`: never use any of them as an identifier.

### Never name a two-parameter static `Set<X>(a, b)` on a XAML type — the XAML compiler makes it an attached property

- The XAML compiler reads every public static `Set<X>` with two parameters on a XAML runtime class (`TermControl`, any control) as the setter of an **attached property** `<X>`.
- When the first parameter is not an element (a `Boolean`, say), the generated `XamlTypeInfo.g.cpp` gets `SetAttachableMember_<X><…, ::winrt::System::Void>` and does not compile: C3083 'System', C2039/C2065 'Void', C2737 `MemberInfos` "const object must be initialized". The errors point at generated code in the project that owns the type (`TerminalControlLib`), never at the `.idl` line. Find the cause with a search for `SetAttachableMember_` in that file.
- Give such a static another verb (`Configure<X>`, `Apply<X>`). Hit 2026-10-03 by `TermControl::SetHiddenRenderingPolicy`, renamed `ConfigureHiddenRenderingPolicy` (the warning sits in `TermControl.idl`). A C++-only static that never reaches the `.winmd` (`ControlCore::SetHiddenRenderingPolicy`) is safe.

### A text box inside a `ContentDialog` gets no keypresses in XAML Islands.

- The dialog's PopupRoot sits outside our island's input path, so a hosted `TextBox`/`NumberBox` takes focus but receives no typing (first hit with the tree-rename box).
- So: use a **buttons-only** ContentDialog for confirms (`_OnDeleteSession`), and build anything that needs typing **into the main visual tree** instead — the in-place rename editor, and the Settings cog's **in-content modal overlay** (a dimmed `Grid` over `_root`, `RowSpan`-all; the card swallows taps via a handled `Tapped`, a backdrop tap = cancel).
- Don't reach for a ContentDialog when a field needs keyboard input.

### A ContentDialog DECLARED in XAML is invisible to UI Automation — build every dialog in code

- WinUI decides a dialog's placement at its first `ShowAsync` (`ContentDialog_Partial.cpp`): a dialog in the LIVE tree — declared in `TerminalPage.xaml` (`x:Load="False"`) and realized by `FindName` — is shown `TransplantedRootInPopup` (only its layout moves into a popup parented inside the dialog's own template, in the page), while a dialog outside the tree is shown `EntireControlInPopup` (a window-level popup named by its title).
- Only the second reaches UI Automation: on 0.8.0 the declared quit confirmation "Do you want to close all windows?" read as an empty Popup with no title or buttons, and a hit test at its "Close all" returned the terminal behind it (flows TAB-K3) — no screen reader could use it, and the e2e suite answered it blind.
- Since 2026-10-23 (NOT BUILT) the page declares NO dialog: all seven are built in code (`_ShowDialogHelper` takes the dialog; the `x:Uid` strings are read as `RS_(L"<Uid>/<Property>")`). ⚠ Never add a ContentDialog to `TerminalPage.xaml` again; build it like `"Close tab?"` (TabManagement.cpp) and show it through `_dialogPresenter`. ⚠ A resource key read in code must exist exactly: a Debug build FAIL_FASTs at launch on a missing `RS_` key (`EnsureAllResourcesArePresent`).

### Accessibility metadata goes through `AgentAutomation.h` — and never renames a control that has a word

- `AgentSetAutomationId` / `AgentSetAccessibleName` / `AgentSetItemStatus` (change-gated plain property values — nothing registered, so the per-element ToolTip leak class cannot happen) and the `AgentItemStatus` facet builder with ONE spelling per facet (`kAgentStatus*`, `AgentStateWord`). Ids follow `am.<surface>.<element>[.<key>]` (key = the session id's first 8 characters); the full list is E2E_TESTING.md §6.3 "As built" — the e2e driver depends on them, so add ids, never rename one.
- ⚠ Name only a control that has NO name of its own (a FontIcon button, a button whose content is a panel). A control whose content is a word is named by that word, and it is often a VALUE ("LOCAL", "MOST ACTIVE", "Auto"): naming it after its tooltip title ("Board scope") would hide the value from a screen reader and break the suite's reads — and many titled tips are set BEFORE the content, so "name it when unset" cannot tell. A TextBox keeps its placeholder as its name.
- Every `AgentSetTip` text also becomes the control's help text (`SyncHelpTextFromTip`), unless its builder set one.
- A card or row that rebuilds on every change sets its item status at build time; one edited IN PLACE (the side panel's cards) sets it in every path that changes what it shows (the model apply AND the selection restyle — `SidePanelApplyCardItemStatus`). A tab's status is composed by `Tab` from its `TerminalTabStatus` on each property change; its state word comes with the dot (`_SetTabAgentDot`'s two new arguments).

### A panel opened from a menu loses the keyboard to the menu's late `Closed` — and closing a plain `Popup` drops the keyboard anywhere

- **The order** (the WinUI source, read 2026-10-23): `MenuFlyoutItem::Invoke` raises Click, THEN closes the menu. The menu's popup closes in that call and returns the focus to what had it before the menu opened (`CPopup::Close`, its light-dismiss branch). The menu's `Closed` EVENT comes only after the close animation (`FlyoutBase` raises it from its presenter's `Unloaded`). So deferring a panel's open and focus by a dispatcher tick (the `_OpenTagEditorAt` / `_PromptForModel` recipe) beats the popup's own focus restore, but NOT a `Closed` handler that moves the focus.
- **The tab's menu has such a handler:** upstream's GH#5750 hand-back (`Tab.cpp`, `RequestFocusActiveControl` → `_FocusCurrentTab`). Until 2026-10-23 it pulled the keyboard out of the Tags panel's name box and out of the "Specify a model" prompt opened from the tab's model submenus (TCM-K2: a typed tag name went into Claude's input box, and Esc interrupted claude). It now stands aside while the keyboard is inside an open popup (`AgentFocusInOpenPopup`, `AgentPanelFocus.h`; fixed in code, NOT BUILT yet). ⚠ Any new `Closed` (or other late) handler that moves the focus must ask the same question.
- **Closing a plain `Popup` that holds the focus drops the keyboard anywhere:** the focused element leaves the tree, and the focus manager moves the focus to the next focusable element it finds (`CFocusManager::SetFocusOnNextFocusableElement`). Only light-dismiss popups and flyouts restore the focus when they close. A `Root()`-parented panel gives the keyboard back itself: `AgentCaptureFocusReturn` right before it focuses its own box, `AgentPanelHoldsKeyboard` right BEFORE its close (after it, the answer is gone), then `TerminalPage::_ReturnKeyboardAfterPanel` (the saved Control, else the current tab; a tab header counts as "the current tab", GH#5750's rule).
- **Close such a panel on the KEY-UP of Escape / Enter, never the key-down**, when the keyboard then goes to a terminal: Windows delivers the key's character (ESC, CR) after the key-down, to whatever holds the focus then, and claude reads ESC as an interrupt and CR as a submit. Arm on the key-down (a `handledEventsToo` hook; any other key-down disarms), act on the key-up: the Tags panel, the "Specify a model" prompt and the side panel's "+" card do this, and so does upstream's tab renamer.

### A pinned tab's `CloseButtonVisibility::Never` is silently undone by `_updateAllTabCloseButtons()`.

- (Historical — learned on the old pinned Manager tab, which is now a page behind the Home button; still true for ANY tab you pin.) Setting `Never` on a tab once (as the old `_OpenAgentManagerTab` did) is NOT enough: that method loops EVERY tab and re-applies the theme's *global* close-button setting (`tab.CloseButtonVisibility(theme…)` → `IsClosable`), and it runs on every theme/ settings apply — which happens *after* the Manager tab is created — so the X reappeared.
- Fix (then): **skip the pinned tab in that loop.**
- Note hiding the X (`IsClosable`) is independent of drag-reorder: a non-closable tab can still be dragged/torn out, so non-movable needs its own guard (`TabViewItem.CanDrag(false)` + a snap-back to index 0 after any reorder + a `_TryMoveTab` refusal — the old Manager tab's `_PinManagerTabFirst`).
- Context-menu Close/Move is a third axis (`DisableCloseAndMoveMenuItems()`).

### Never reuse the `WindowsTerminalDev` package identity.

- It belongs to the separate `K:\source\windowsterminal` checkout; registering the same identity tries to *replace* it and fails with a file-in-use lock (`0x80073CF6 / 0x80070020`) when its `OpenConsoleProxy.dll` is loaded.
- Our distinct `Agentmaster` identity sidesteps this.

### Upstream "wt identity" assumptions are package-BLIND and break under our rename

- (per-identity since the profiles split).
- Several WT helpers hardcode the `wt.exe`/`WindowsTerminal` identity and silently misbehave for our packages: (1) **`GetWtExePath()`** (`WtExeUtils.h`) resolves `<PFN>\wt.exe`/`wtd.exe`, which **doesn't exist** (our manifests register the **`agentmaster.exe`** / **`agentmasterdev.exe`** execution aliases) — so every launcher built on it (`_OpenNewWindow`, the Reopen-Windows button, Jump List shortcuts) silently no-ops; (2) **`windowClassName`** (`WindowEmperor.cpp` — it seeds BOTH the single-instance **mutex** and the **window class**) is built from `WT_BRANDING` only, so our Debug "Windows Terminal Dev" branding **shared one single-instance identity with the real `WindowsTerminalDev`** (a launch could hand its commandline to the *other* window — defeating the whole point of the rename).
- Fix (both, package-aware): `GetWtExePath` picks the alias by PFN prefix — **`AgentmasterDev` FIRST, then `Agentmaster`** (the former contains the latter as a prefix; same ordering rule in `_AgentmasterReopenTarget`) — and `windowClassName` appends `GetCurrentPackageFamilyName()` for packaged builds.
- So coexistence (with `WindowsTerminalDev` AND between our own release/dev pair) needs distinct package identities (above) AND package-distinct **runtime** identifiers.
- Launch the alias by **name** (`agentmaster.exe` / `agentmasterdev.exe` — per-identity, so it can never activate the other install) — it resolves on PATH + follows the APPEXECLINK reparse and hands off; a full reparse-path `CreateProcess`/`Start-Process` bypasses the alias and cascades a fresh window instead (ShellExecuteEx on the full `<PFN>\<alias>` path is the verified exception — see `GetWtExePath`).

### Closing instances to relink.

- Closing **our** dev instance for the deploy inner loop **requires the user's permission first** (see *Development Rules*; the prior "always auto deploy, no prompt" authorization is **revoked**).
- Once permitted, kill **the GUI process ONLY** (`Agentmaster.exe`; `WindowsTerminal.exe` pre-rename), path-filtered to the layout you are actually redeploying (the instance's InstallLocation, e.g. `Q:\agentmaster-layouts\Debug-B\` for dev) — a broad `Q:\agentmaster-layouts\*` filter (or, before 2026-10-12, `K:\source\Agentmaster\*`) ALSO matches the **Release** loose layout (the registered release install), which can host the very session you're running in (`AM_SESSION` / `CCMGR_HOOK_PIPE` set), so it would **self-kill** it.
- **⚠ NEVER `Stop-Process` the `OpenConsole.exe` ConPTY hosts** (the old recipe did): a host killed outright never runs its graceful shutdown (`PtySignalInputThread::_Shutdown → CloseConsoleProcessState → CTRL_CLOSE_EVENT` to every attached client), so that tab's `pwsh` wrapper + `claude.exe` (+ its MCP `cmd`/`node` children) keep running forever with a **DEAD console** — unreachable by any terminal, feeding a NEWER instance phantom hooks via `bridge.json`.
- (OBSERVER.md §13a — the in-app orphan reaper ends such orphaned sessions, but don't create them.)
- Killing the GUI alone closes its pipe handles; every host then ends its clients and exits by itself — **wait for the layout's `OpenConsole.exe` set to drain** (they lock the binary the build copies) before relinking.
- Then build, relaunch.
- And **never** touch the running **Store** Windows Terminal — it's the user's live session (under `Program Files\WindowsApps\…`, not our path).
- Never blanket-`taskkill` by image name; always path-filter so the Store WT is spared.

### Packaging (`PRI210 / 0x800704c8`) can fail to overwrite `resources.pri`.

- The registered loose-layout package keeps its layout's `resources.pri` (e.g. `Q:\agentmaster-layouts\Debug-B\resources.pri`) memory-mapped, so a build into that folder dies in MakePri's final overwrite-move with `0x800704c8` (ERROR_USER_MAPPED_FILE) — and `handle64` shows *no* owning user process (it's a registered-package/kernel mapping).
- The exe/dll already linked by then (your code is in the fresh `TerminalApp.dll`, copied into the layout *before* this step), so it's purely the PRI.
- Fix: **delete that `resources.pri` and rebuild** — MakePri then *creates* it fresh instead of overwriting a mapped target.
- (The Defender exclusion in Building FAST #3 also reduces the transient-lock variant.)

### MSIX virtualizes a packaged app's `%LOCALAPPDATA%`

- to the package LocalCache, but the spawned **`claude.exe` is external** and resolves paths against the real filesystem.
- So the hooks files + `--settings` path **must** live somewhere un-virtualized that both agree on — Agentmaster anchors the **profile defaults under `%USERPROFILE%`** (`AgentmasterStateDir()` == the ACTIVE PROFILE: default `~\.agentmaster`, dev package `~\.agentmaster-dev`; a Browse…-picked profile inherits this constraint — it must be a real filesystem folder, which `FOS_FORCEFILESYSTEM` enforces).
- Using `%LOCALAPPDATA%` here silently breaks hooks for spawned sessions (the app writes to LocalCache; Claude reads the empty real path).
- Verified live: with the fix, a spawned session's `SessionStart`/`UserPromptSubmit` reach the registry (`<profile>\hooks.log`).

### ⚠ `--dangerously-skip-permissions` does NOT skip the startup workspace-TRUST dialog

- **⚠ `--dangerously-skip-permissions` does NOT skip the startup workspace-TRUST dialog.**
- Reading claude's own gate, **the permission mode is never consulted** — the workspace is trusted iff (a) env **`CLAUDE_CODE_SANDBOXED`** is truthy [its ONLY two consumers in the whole CLI are the two trust functions], (b) an **in-memory** session flag is set — which non-interactive `-p`, background-agent mode, and accepting the dialog all do, (c) background-agent mode, or (d) `~/.claude.json` `projects.<key>.hasTrustDialogAccepted === true` for the cwd's key **or ANY ANCESTOR** of the cwd.
- The key is the **git toplevel** of the cwd (else the cwd), `path.normalize`d with `\` → `/`, no trailing separator, **case-sensitive**.
- The whole step is also skipped by the internal **`CLAUBBIT`** env var.
- There is **NO settings.json / managed-policy knob** (the CLI's entire trust-related string table was enumerated).
- Accepting at your **home dir never persists** — the accept handler branches `if (isHomeDir) setSessionTrustAccepted(true); else persistProjectFlag()`, so `%USERPROFILE%` (our empty-`defaultLaunchDir` fallback!) re-prompts on **every** launch, forever.
- What the flag DOES do is auto-accept tool prompts; it still does NOT suppress the one-time **global** "Bypass Permissions mode" acceptance (`~/.claude.json` `bypassPermissionsModeAccepted` — shown once, ever, until accepted).
- Toggling skipPermissions OFF drops the flag and instead pins `permissions.defaultMode:"default"` in the hooks-settings file (normal prompts apply).
- **Agentmaster's actual fix** is `EnsureClaudeWorkspaceTrusted` — an unattended ConPTY tab cannot answer a modal, so it must never be asked.
- Full write-up + the deployed-app verification checklist: [`HOOKS.md`](doc/agentmaster/HOOKS.md) *Workspace trust*.

### An unanswerable startup modal is invisible-by-design in our UI

- **An unanswerable startup modal is invisible-by-design in our UI — budget for that when adding spawn flags.**
- A tab parked on claude's trust dialog fires no `UserPromptSubmit`, so it mints no transcript id: the Fleet Observer can only classify it as a §11d *started-but-not-yet-prompted* claude and it wears the dim `○ claude · unlinked` observe badge.
- No card, no state dot, no autorunner, and a `/handover` content injection is typed straight into the menu.
- The symptom ("tabs don't auto-activate, every one needs a manual click") reads like an Agentmaster bug and is not one.
- Any future claude flag/version bump that reintroduces a startup prompt lands here again — the cheap check is a PTY probe (`pywinpty`, 45×160, ~18 s) in a throwaway directory — recipe in [`HOOKS.md`](doc/agentmaster/HOOKS.md) *Workspace trust → Verifying it*.
- ⚠ When probing with an isolated **`CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR`**, note it also relocates the USER-memory root, which reclassifies `~/.claude/CLAUDE.md`'s `@imports` as *project* external includes and raises a second dialog ("Allow external CLAUDE.md file imports?", gated on the per-project `hasClaudeMdExternalIncludesApproved` / `…WarningShown`).
- That is an **artifact of the harness, not a real-config behavior** — verified: all 64 live project entries have both keys `false` and no such dialog appears.
- Don't "fix" it.

### `claude --resume <id>` dies if there's no conversation.

- A session that was opened but never prompted has no saved transcript; resuming it exits code 1 ("No conversation found") and the tab is dead.
- **Don't gate resume-vs-fresh on the persisted `SessionState`** — a just-resumed session reads `Idle` until its first new turn, so state is useless as a "was-it-used" signal.
- (Restore DOES seed a reopened session's DISPLAY state from the persisted one — the at-rest "needs you" states WaitingForInput/NeedsApproval survive a crash via `RestoredSessionState`, Rule #16 — but that is triage display, never the resume decision.) Gate on the transcript on disk: `ClaudeConversationExists(id)` globs `<CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR | ~/.claude>/projects/*/<id>.jsonl` (ids are unique UUIDs, so no need to reproduce Claude's cwd→dir encoding).
- No transcript ⇒ launch fresh (`[restore-fresh]` in `hooks.log`) instead of `--resume` (`[resume]`).

### Native-exe-only policy: launch the native `claude.exe` by FULL PATH, gate everything else.

- Launch/Restore hands a command line to ConPTY, which launches it via `CreateProcessW` — and `CreateProcessW` appends only `.exe` and **never consults `PATHEXT`**.
- So a bare `claude` token finds a native `claude.exe` (the `~/.local/bin` installer) but **silently misses the npm `claude.cmd`**, failing with `0x80070002` / `ERROR_FILE_NOT_FOUND` — *even though a hand-typed `claude` works* (a shell honors `PATHEXT` + the shim).
- **The fix is a deliberate policy, not a `.cmd` workaround:** Agentmaster is so `claude.exe`-coupled (the entire Fleet Observer + PEB enrichment key on the `claude.exe` image) that a pure-Node `claude` (`node.exe`, no native binary) is **unsupported**.
- `ResolveClaudeExe` resolves a *real `claude.exe`* — Settings override (must be `.exe`) → PATH `claude.exe` (exact leaf, never a `.cmd`) → `~/.local/bin\claude.exe` → **follow a `claude.cmd`/`.bat` to its npm native binary** (`<dir>\node_modules\@anthropic-ai\…\claude.exe`; the `.cmd` is a breadcrumb, never launched).
- Run once at engine init **before the PATH shim is prepended** (so a PATH `claude.cmd` resolves to the real binary, not our shim) → cached in `Engine::claudeExePath`; `hooks.log` prints `[engine] claude.exe: …`.
- Empty ⇒ `ClaudeAvailable()` false ⇒ the Manager gates launch/new/fork/resume behind the "Claude not detected" modal (Browse… via `IFileOpenDialog` + `claude install`), `TerminalPage::_LaunchClaudeSession` hard-refuses (`[launch-blocked]`) as the backstop for non-UI restore paths, and the Settings cog shows the detected path + an `.exe`-only override (`AppSettings::claudeExePath`, re-resolved live via `RefreshClaudeExe`).
- Because both npm and the native installer ship the SAME native `claude.exe` today (npm just delivers it via a per-platform optional dep), this gates only the legacy Node CLI.
- (Codex's `BuildCodexCommandline` still emits a bare `codex` — same latent bug, not yet fixed.)

### A ConPTY connection's process spawns on the control's first non-zero layout

- **A ConPTY connection's process spawns on the control's first non-zero layout — never eager-`Start()` it before the control initializes.**
- `TermControl::_InitializeTerminal` (run by the control's first non-zero layout) is what calls `_core.Connection().Start()`, so a background/unfocused tab's `claude.exe` doesn't launch until the tab is first shown (hence "Restore all" re-opens lazily).
- **The start happens in the layout pass that first sizes the SwapChainPanel** — its `SizeChanged` (`_SwapChainSizeChanged` → `_StartTerminalInLayoutPass`, 2026-10-05, NOT BUILT yet, UI_THREAD.md §10k). ⚠ Never move the start to `Loaded` alone: XAML raises `Loaded` only in its frame tick, but it also lays the tree out OUTSIDE the tick, before every pointer hit test (`CUIElement::BoundsTestEntry` → `UpdateLayout()`), and our own `UpdateLayout()` calls are more such passes. A pass like that makes a new control hit-testable while `Loaded` is still pending. With the start on `Loaded` only (071031fdf), a mouse move processed between a new tab's content swap and the next tick delivered `PointerMoved` to a terminal with no text buffer, and dev and release both crashed after a fork (`TextBuffer::GetSize` on a null buffer, via the hyperlink hover lookup; `ControlCore::_updateHoveredCell` now also returns until `Initialize` has run). A terminal whose start failed stays hit-testable, so `TermControl`'s pointer, wheel (`OnMouseWheel` too) and scroll-bar handlers forward nothing to `ControlInteractivity` until `_initializedTerminal` is set — the INPUT GATE (§10k): a press reads the buffer in its first step (`GetHyperlink`), a wheel or scroll-bar scroll in `UserScrollViewport`. Keep any new input path behind it.
- The `LayoutUpdated` hook is the belt behind that start. It is held only while the control is LOADED and not yet started (`TermControl::_UpdateLayoutInitHook`, called from the §4c `Loaded` / `Unloaded` handlers; it reads `IsLoaded()`, because a stale `Unloaded` can follow the next `Loaded`), and `Loaded` also tries the start at once. XAML calls every `LayoutUpdated` handler of the thread after every layout pass, so the old constructor-time subscription made ~537 never-opened restored tabs run a no-op handler on every pass (UI_THREAD.md §10a #3, §10d); `SizeChanged` is raised only for an element whose size changed, so a never-shown control costs nothing. The start reason (`_layoutInitReason`: Create, or Reattach after a tear-out) is a member, and both starts refuse a closing or detached control (`Detach` nulls `_interactivity`).
- Trying to force a background session to run by calling `connection.Start()` early from the app layer **crashes the app**: the connection's output then reaches `ControlCore`'s output handler before `_core.Initialize()` (gated on that same layout) has run → **AV `0xc0000005` in `Microsoft.Terminal.Control.dll`**, on every startup restore.
- The safe way to start a background session is to let its control initialize first (select the tab) — which is *why* startup ARCHIVES instead of auto-launching (Rule #6): no startup tabs ⇒ nothing to lazily-not-start, and a user Restore opens one focused tab that initializes normally.

### A parented `Popup` that OVERHANGS its parent's clip renders

- **A parented `Popup` that OVERHANGS its parent's clip renders — but mutating it wrong fail-fasts twice (`0xC000027B`).**
- The bookmark-tag badges hang BELOW the tab's bottom edge by living in an always-open `Popup` parented into the tab header (a popup child draws in the island's POPUP ROOT, outside the tab strip ScrollViewer's clip — the only way in-tree-invisible pixels below the tab can paint).
- Two ways it killed the app, both diagnosed from the dumps (`tools/dumpstowed2` — the stowed backtraces named the exact frames): **(1) opening an UNROOTED popup throws** (`IsOpen(true)` → `E_UNEXPECTED`, no `XamlRoot`) — a restored tab asserts its badges (via a `TabStatus` property → PropertyChanged) BEFORE its header enters the visual tree, so the open had no root; gate every open on `HeaderRootGrid().IsLoaded()` + `.XamlRoot()` and re-arm on the header's `Loaded`.
- **(2) mutating the popup SYNCHRONOUSLY inside a layout-driven handler is a LAYOUT CYCLE** (`E_LAYOUTCYCLE 0x88000FA8`): the placement triggers are all layout passes (`SizeChanged` / `LayoutUpdated` / the strip `ScrollViewer.ViewChanged`), and writing `HorizontalOffset`/`IsOpen` re-invalidates layout → re-enters the pass; while the strip is still settling the mid-pass geometry reads shift each iteration so the "unchanged" gates never latch and XAML's ~250-iteration detector fail-fasts.
- Fix: never touch the popup from inside the pass — make the trigger a **coalescing scheduler** (a `bool` + one `Dispatcher().RunAsync`) and do the reads + gated mutations on the CLEAN tick after layout settles (the `_ApplyRenamerMaxWidth` cycle-safety lesson, again).
- Both guards + a `CATCH_LOG` on the whole popup tail now live in `TabHeaderControl::_PositionTagBadgesNow`.

### MUX TabView drag-start null-deref (WinUI 2.8)

- **MUX TabView drag-start null-deref (WinUI 2.8) — dragging ANY tab on a scrolled, overflowed strip crashes INSIDE Microsoft's DLL, before any event reaches us.**
- **ROOT CAUSE:** the dragged "item" WUX reports is the tab's CONTENT — WT's BODGY unique `Border` — which MUX can never resolve, so EVERY drag walks a null-unsafe fallback loop over ALL containers; any virtualized-out container before the dragged tab is an instant AV.
- The chain: WUX `ListViewBase::GetDraggedItems` (ListViewBase_Partial_Reorder.cpp) puts `container.Content()` — NOT the container — into `DragItemsStarting.Items` for an items-are-their-own-containers list (WT's `TabItems` of `TabViewItem`s), and upstream WT plants a unique empty `Border` as every tab's Content (Tab.cpp `TabViewItem().Content(Border{})`, its "BODGY" disambiguator for exactly this MUX lookup).
- MUX's `TabView::OnListViewDragItemsStarting → FindTabViewItemFromDragItem` (TabView.cpp:823) then misses both fast paths — `ContainerFromItem(border)` (a Border is no item of TabItems) and `VisualTreeHelper::GetParent(border)` (the Content is never rendered) — and falls into the loop `ContainerFromIndex(i).Content() == item` from index 0 with NO null check: the first virtualized-out container (index 0 = the pinned Manager tab, whenever the overflowed strip is scrolled) is `null.Content()` → `0xC0000005` at `Microsoft.UI.Xaml.dll+0xB605C` (+ the `0xc000041d` death-rattle at the same offset), raised BEFORE `TabDragStarting` reaches our handlers (unguardable at the event, uncatchable under /EHsc).
- **⚠ DEAD END (REMOVED — `_NeutralizeTabStripVirtualization()`): "make the strip non-virtualizing by swapping its `ItemsPanel` for a plain `StackPanel`" is NOT legal and made things far WORSE.**
- It fixed the drag AV but replaced it with a **deterministic startup crash on EVERY launch** — `0xC0000005` read of a `0xD0` field in `Windows.UI.Xaml.dll+0x591677`, a deferred **render** pass (0 of our frames on the faulting thread).
- Root reason: a **system-XAML `ListView` (ListViewBase) REQUIRES its `ItemsPanel` to be a `ModernCollectionBasePanel`** — an `ItemsStackPanel` or `ItemsWrapGrid`.
- A plain `StackPanel` is a legal host only for a bare `ItemsControl`; inside a `ListView` the framework's internal panel casts return null and the render pass null-derefs.
- (The removed comment's claim "MUX has ZERO code dependency on `ItemsStackPanel`" missed that the dependency is the *system* `ListViewBase`, not MUX.) There is **NO legal non-virtualizing `ItemsPanel` for a `ListView`**, so de-virtualization is unreachable at our layer — don't retry it.
- **Current state: native MUX tab drag is PERMANENTLY DISABLED and the gesture is OURS.**
- `CanReorderTabs(false)` + `CanDragTabs(false)` (`TerminalPage::Create` re-asserting `TabRowControl.xaml` — the exact configuration WT itself ships for elevated windows, so a supported TabView mode) makes the broken lookup UNREACHABLE — no native item-drag can start, so neither the drag-start nor the drop-side call ever runs; every `TabViewItem` is additionally pinned `CanDrag(false)` (`_ApplyTabDragPolicy`, the renamed successor of `_GuardTabDragUntilRegistered` — its defer-to-`Loaded` re-ENABLE was the one hole left in the disable, and its per-Loaded log line stormed at ~30 Hz during a big window-restore).
- Reorder + tear-out are re-implemented as the **pointer-owned gesture** (`_WireTabReorderGesture` / `_TabReorder*` in `TerminalPage.AgentEngine.cpp`; pure decision math in `AgentMaster/TabDragMath.h`): press a header (mouse/pen; touch keeps native strip panning) → 6-DIP threshold captures the pointer ON THE TABVIEW → an accent INSERTION CARET (parented in the TabView's TEMPLATE ROOT, not `Root()` — with showTabsInTitlebar the tab row leaves `Root()`) tracks the midpoint rule over the REALIZED headers only — a caret-commit design: NOTHING moves until release (no per-crossing selection/content churn), then ONE `_TryMoveTab` (UIA announce + settle + Manager floor) — with edge auto-scroll (per-move + a 50 ms hold-still `SafeDispatcherTimer`); release clear of the strip TEARS OUT via the exact native downstream (`_sendDraggedTabToWindow` → new window); Esc cancels (nothing moved).
- `[nav]` taxonomy preserved: `tab-drag-begin` at activation, `tab-drag-end` with `from=N to=M` / `(docked into window <id> at slot N)` / `(no same-window reorder)` / `(torn out to a new window)` / `(cancelled: esc|capture lost|tab closed mid-drag)` / `(tear-out refused: not a terminal tab)`.
- **Cross-window DOCKING is REBORN as the same-process hit-test replacement** (the OLE pipeline's docking died with `CanDragTabs=false`; this closes that noted follow-up): a release over ANOTHER Agentmaster window's **tab-strip row** moves the tab INTO that window at the aimed slot, with the TARGET's own insertion caret previewing the slot while the drag hovers its strip.
- Per-window **dock-target sinks** on the shared engine (`Engine::RegisterWindowDockTarget` — top-level HWND + a probe; `FindWindowDockProbe`; detached in `~TerminalPage`, Rule #10): the dragging window resolves the top-level window under the cursor (`WindowFromPoint` → `GA_ROOT` — z-order-true, so an overlapped background window is never mis-targeted; its own hwnd excluded) and invokes the target's probe SYNCHRONOUSLY — every window lives on the Emperor's ONE UI thread (`WindowEmperor::CreateNewWindow`), the same fact the native OLE receive path relied on (`AppHost::_handleMoveContent` calls the target's `AttachContent` directly).
- The probe (`TerminalPage::_DockProbeHit`) maps screen → client px → island DIPs → TabView-local, hit-tests the strip ROW (y-only + a tight 12-DIP slack — x is not tested, so a drop on the titlebar right of the tabs APPENDS instead of surprising with a new window; a collapsed strip is never a target), and answers the midpoint-rule insertion slot over its realized bands — the SAME `TabDragMath` the local caret uses (`DecideTabInsertionSlot` + Manager floor), so a dock and a local reorder can never disagree; Preview mode also lights/hides the target's own caret (`_UpdateDockPreview`/`_ClearDockPreview` track target changes per move/tick, cleared on reset/commit/Esc).
- The send rides the EXACT native downstream — `_sendDraggedTabToWindow(<numeric window id>, slot)` → `_MoveContent` → `AppHost::_handleMoveContent` routes by id → the target's `AttachContent` lands the tab + `_TryMoveTab`s it into the slot (enforcing ITS Manager floor) — then the target window is foregrounded (attention follows the tab, like a tear-out's new window).
- Any failure (foreign-process window — the dev install beside release; torn tree; threading surprise) reads as a MISS and degrades to the classic new-window tear-out — never guess a drop.
- (`AllowDropTabs` + the `_onTabStripDragOver/Drop` OLE receive side stay wired but dead — no in-process OLE drag source exists.) The old native handlers (`_TabDragStarted`/`_TabDragCompleted`/`_onTabDragStarting`/`_onTabDroppedOutside`) also stay wired — inert, and they'd matter again if the knobs were ever re-enabled.
- ⚠ Such a MUX AV is not always fatal — the SWALLOWED variant leaves a live-but-WEDGED app (the content area keeps presenting the dragged tab's pixels regardless of the selected tab) that reads as a freeze, so an event-1000 AV does NOT imply the process died: check hooks.log for post-event liveness before reading a "restart" as a crash.
- The shipped resolution is deliberately LOAD-IMMUNE: no step of the pointer-owned gesture depends on realization timing (bands read only what IS realized; the caret-commit applies exactly the slot last shown — under load the caret merely updates less often).
- The **Content-trick family is dead too**: the parent probe is a SINGLE-level `VisualTreeHelper::GetParent(fe)` (no walk-up), and WT uses the STOCK TabView template (no retemplate in-repo), whose `TabView::UpdateTabContent` re-parents `tvi.Content()` into the template's `TabContentPresenter` on every selection change — so a Content that already has a parent (self-as-Content / template-root-as-Content / a header-parented Border) is a guaranteed "already child of another element" crash. ⟹ **That one flawless class — lookup unreachable + our own gesture — is what SHIPPED (the "Current state" block above).**
- The remaining belts: **`_SettleTabStripLayout()`** — `UpdateLayout()` after EVERY `TabItems()` mutation (`_InitializeTab` / `_RemoveTab` / `_TryMoveTab` / `_TabDragCompleted`) so the pump never sees an unsettled strip (our own gesture + `_RevealTabInStrip` probe the same item->container map) — plus **`_ApplyTabDragPolicy(tvi)`** — settle + pin EVERY (re)inserted tab `CanDrag(false)`, unconditionally (a `CanDrag(true)` element could still start a raw XAML drag into the broken WUX `GetDraggedItems` path even with the list-level knobs off).
- **Don't add a `TabItems()` mutation without routing through these.**

### A TabViewItem never clamps its children's desired size — an unbounded header child makes the tab lay out wider than it is and clip itself

- XAML treats each item of a ListView on an ItemsStackPanel as a NON-CLIPPING subtree, and a TabViewItem is a ListViewItem. Inside one, an element's DesiredSize is never cut down to the space it was offered (WinUI `FrameworkElement::MeasureCore`: "currently only listviewitems do not want clipping"; `ListViewBase::PreProcessContentPreparation` turns it on for each container of an ItemsStackPanel).
- `Width` / `MaxWidth` still clamp, and a `ScrollContentPresenter` clamps its own desired size (so a TextBox bounds itself), but a TextBlock in a horizontal StackPanel reports its whole text width.
- The tab then arranges its content at that unclipped width (`ArrangeCore` arranges at the larger of the desired size and the `Width` MUX wrote) and clips the result at its own width (`UpdateLayoutClip`'s local clip). The content is laid out wider than the visible tab.
- The 2026-10-05 report: a long tab title ran to the tab's edge and seemed to go on under the next tab. The close button, the right separator and the selected tab's right corner, laid out past that edge, disappeared. The rename box was cut off the same way.
- Bound every tab-header child that can grow: a Grid star column (measured with the room really left) plus `TextTrimming`, or an explicit `MaxWidth`. Never put an unbounded child in a horizontal StackPanel inside a tab header (`TabHeaderControl.xaml` `HeaderTitleRow`).

### A TabViewItem takes every mouse LEFT press before your handler — a plain `PointerPressed` handler on it never sees one

- XAML runs an element's class handler (its `OnPointerPressed` override) BEFORE the handlers added to that element (`CEventManager::Raise`: `RaiseControlEvents`, then the element's own handler list), and a plain `PointerPressed` handler is skipped for an event already marked handled.
- A MUX `TabViewItem` is a `ListViewItem`: `ListViewBaseItem::OnPointerPressed` queues the selection tap for a mouse or pen LEFT press (touch too) and marks the press handled; right and middle presses stay unhandled. So a plain handler on a tab sees right and middle presses only. `_OnTabPointerPressed`'s Shift+Click "Activate Tab" branch was dead code from the day it was written, its selection veto with it (TAB-K4, found by the e2e suite on 0.8.0; fixed in code 2026-10-23, NOT BUILT yet — flows/07).
- Register such a handler with handledEventsToo (`AddHandler(UIElement::PointerPressedEvent(), box_value(PointerEventHandler{…}), true)`), and tell a press an inner control took (the close button = a ButtonBase, the rename box = a TextBox) from a press on the tab by walking `OriginalSource` up to the item (`ResolveTabPressTarget`, TabManagement.cpp).
- The selection happens on the RELEASE: `ListViewBaseItem::OnPointerReleased` performs the queued tap (focus the item, THEN select it) unless the release reaches the item already handled, so marking the press handled stops nothing. To keep a click from selecting a tab, mark its release handled below the item — a one-shot `PointerReleased` handler on the item's template root (its visual child), as `_ArmTabShiftActivateRelease` does. Reverting the selection in `SelectionChanged` instead also scrolls the reverted-to tab into view (the Selector's own `ScrollIntoView` on every selection change) and moves the keyboard onto a tab header.
- Every ListView / GridView item behaves the same.

### The tab strip VIRTUALIZES — never `StartBringIntoView` a tab; reveal through `_RevealTabInStrip`

- The strip's `ItemsStackPanel` derealizes a tab scrolled far off (release windows reach **355 tabs**), and `StartBringIntoView` silently NO-OPS on a derealized `TabViewItem`. Upstream's funnel used exactly that, so every selection of a far tab — a card double-click, Jump Back (right after Home's `ChangeView(0)`), a toast click, `switchToTab`, a close successor, Move to start/end, a reopened window's focused tab — swapped the content in without scrolling the strip.
- `_UpdatedSelectedTab` (the ONE selection funnel) calls `_RevealTabInStrip(tab, leaveFullyVisibleInPlace=true)`: ScrollIntoView + a precise pass on Low-priority retries for a far tab, and a tab already wholly on screen never moves (a click must not slide it from under the pointer). The placement math is the pure, tested `DecideTabStripRevealOffset` (`TabDragMath.h`, `TestTabStripReveal`), measured **viewport-relative**: MUX 2.8.4's `TabScrollViewerStyle` puts the `<`/`>` buttons in their own grid columns BESIDE the viewport, not over it (an old comment claimed overlay). `_revealTabStripGeneration` stops an older retry chain from scrolling back after a newer selection.
- Two cases raise no selection event and reveal explicitly: `_FocusClaudeSessionTab` on a tab that is ALREADY selected, and a board card / tree row / side-panel card click on the Manager (`_RevealClickedSessionTab` — even a re-click of the selected card, which `_SelectSession` early-outs on).
- **The strip's scroll extent is an ESTIMATE.** It only lays out the headers near the view and guesses the width of the rest: on 2026-10-01 (416 tabs) the headers it hadn't laid out were counted at ~175 DIP each against a real 130 (read off UI Automation: the selected tab's x against the list's left edge). So a scroll offset doesn't map steadily to a tab index, and a re-layout can leave the same offset on a different stretch of tabs. Place tabs with `_RevealTabInStrip` (it measures the realized header), never with an offset computed from an index, and use `_KeepTabHeaderInView` after any action that must leave the strip where it was.
  - **Where the error comes from:** the panel records one width per tab index each time it lays a tab out (`StackingLayoutStrategyImpl::RegisterSize`) and estimates every tab it has not laid out at the average of those records (`EstimateNonGroupedExtent`). A record goes stale when the width changes while that tab is not laid out: a cog Save of the tab width, or a tab first measured before MUX gave it its equal width. Scrolling lays out tabs again, which replaces their records, so the estimate, and the content size with it, changes WHILE the user scrolls. Measured 2026-10-05 (UI Automation, read-only, release 0.6.8.16): a 476-tab window an hour after two tab-width Saves had its content estimated at 67,800 px for about 66,650 px of tabs; a 31-tab window, every tab laid out, was exact.
- **MUX scrolls its SELECTED tab into view whenever the strip grows, so a new tab is selected BEFORE the strip's first layout pass with it.** MUX 2.8.4's `TabView::OnItemsPresenterSizeChanged` calls `BringSelectedTabIntoView` (skipped after a removal until the pointer leaves the strip), and XAML routes that bring-into-view ASYNCHRONOUSLY: it lands after the current call stack, so nothing in that stack can undo it. `_SettleTabStripLayout` (a synchronous whole-window `UpdateLayout`) fires it at once. `_InitializeTab` used to settle while the user's previous tab was still selected. So a tab opened from ANOTHER tab's context menu ("New Session Here" / "Fork session" / "Duplicate tab") scrolled the strip back to the previous tab whenever that tab was still realized (the strip keeps ~2 viewports of tabs realized on each side), and the new tab ended off screen (2026-10-03, release: 28 tabs away in a 453-tab window).
  - **The fix:** `_InitializeTab` selects the new tab with the tab-switch funnel HELD (`_holdTabSwitchForNewTab`), settles, then runs the held switch by calling `_OnTabSelectionChanged` directly.
  - **The rule:** settle only with the tab that must end up on screen already selected. Never settle right after a NEW tab's content was swapped in: that content's first layout, which starts its terminal, would run inside the settle, before `_LaunchClaudeSession` wires the session up.
  - **No effect at startup:** inside `_OnFirstLayout` (a `LayoutUpdated` handler) every settle is a no-op (the layout manager's reentrancy guard), which is why a window restore stays fast and its tabs stay dormant.
  - **Since 2026-10-05 the strip ignores MUX's request** (the next bullet), so the select-first order is a belt for a strip whose ScrollViewer is not bound yet. The settle-before-the-switch half of the rule still holds on its own.
- **The strip ignores bring-into-view requests, so `_RevealTabInStrip` is the ONLY way it scrolls to a tab (2026-10-05, NOT BUILT yet).** `_EnsureTabStripScrollViewer` sets `BringIntoViewOnFocusChange(false)` on the strip's ScrollViewer when it binds it (one `[strip] bring-into-view off` line per window in hooks.log).
  - **Why:** MUX's `OnItemsPresenterSizeChanged` → `BringSelectedTabIntoView` runs on EVERY content resize, and a big strip's content resizes while the user scrolls (the estimate above). So the strip snapped back to the selected tab as long as that tab was still laid out (~2 viewports on each side) and could never be scrolled away from it. Report: 2026-10-05, release 0.6.8.16, the 476-tab window: "The scrolling now does not allow me to move away from the selected tab's view".
  - **How:** XAML's `ScrollViewer::OnBringIntoViewRequested` acts on a request from inside it only when the request is FORCED or `BringIntoViewOnFocusChange` is true, and `UIElement::StartBringIntoView` never forces. `ChangeView` (the reveal's precise pass, Home, the drag auto-scroll, the `<` / `>` buttons) and `ListView::ScrollIntoView` (the panel changes the offset itself) are not such requests.
  - **The rule:** anything that must bring a tab into view calls `_RevealTabInStrip`. A `StartBringIntoView` inside the strip does nothing, and an element that gets focus inside the strip is no longer scrolled into view. A tab's rename box is the one such element that matters: `_TrackRenamingTabInStrip` (on `Tab::RenamerOpenChanged`) reveals the renaming tab when its box opens and after every size change while it is open.
- **Never `ScrollIntoView` a tab the strip has not laid out yet.** The panel runs the request at the start of its next measure (`DetermineWindowAndAnchors`), and a tab that isn't connected yet takes the ESTIMATE path: every header is recycled and the tab is anchored at index × the average width from the first realized header, which lands far off on a strip of hundreds of tabs. A just-inserted or just-re-inserted tab stays unconnected until the next layout pass. `_TryMoveTab` still reveals before its settle (its switch runs from `SelectedItem`); that may make a reorder jump, but no such jump has been reported, and it is not fixed.
- `_TryMoveTab` suppresses the funnel while the tab is out of `TabItems` (`_movingTab`): removing the SELECTED item makes the TabView select its neighbor for an instant, and the funnel used to mark that neighbor read, clear its flash / Mark Unread, wake it if Deactivated, and log a false `tab-focus` (hooks.log: `tab-move` → `tab-focus <neighbor>` → `tab-focus <moved>`).
- `_RemoveTab`'s successor selection bypasses the funnel (`_removing`), so it calls `_ApplyAgentTabSelection` itself — the ONE shared body of the funnel's Agentmaster steps. **Add any new per-selection effect to `_ApplyAgentTabSelection`, never inline in `_OnTabSelectionChanged`**, or close successors silently miss it (before this, closing onto the Manager left its lens flagged hidden — a frozen board).

### A running XAML storyboard walks the window's whole composition tree on EVERY display frame — ambient loops go through `AgentAmbientAnimation`

- While ANY storyboard runs, XAML draws a frame on every display refresh, and each frame walks the window's composition tree on the UI thread (`CXcpBrowserHost::OnTick` → `SubmitPrimitiveCompositionCommands` → `HWCompTreeNode::UpdateTree*`). On the 555-tab release that was ~2.7 ms a frame at 65 frames/s: 50–70% of the UI thread's busy time, from a handful of Forever pulses (UI_THREAD.md §10). The cost is the LOOP, not the animation count: one running storyboard costs as much as a hundred.
- So a looping or long-running decorative animation must never be a storyboard. Use `AgentAmbientAnimation.h`: `AgentOpacityLoop` / `AgentDotsPulse` for a XAML element's `Opacity` (`UIElement::StartAnimation`; XAML attaches no per-frame listener to `Opacity`), and composition visuals of your own (`SetElementChildVisual`, like `AgentEmberSparks`) for anything that MOVES.
- Never animate a XAML element's position (the RS5 `Translation` property, or a hand-out visual's transform): XAML follows it on every frame for hit-testing (`PropertySetListener` → `NWSetTransformDirty`), which brings the per-frame walk back.
- A loop on a XAML element must BEGIN AND END on the element's still value: `StopAnimation` applies `SetToFinalValue`, and XAML then writes the stopped value back into the property. Never set that element's `Opacity` from code or XAML while it loops — a static set cancels the loop.
- Why a hand-out visual is not used for `Opacity`: XAML culls an element whose own `Opacity` is 0 (`hwwalk.cpp` `skipRenderWhileTransparent`) unless IT knows the opacity animates, which only `UIElement::StartAnimation` tells it.
- **A timer that flips a XAML property is a frame per flip** — and a layout pass too when the property is `Visibility`. A repeating blink is `AgentLockstepBlink` (UI_THREAD.md §10i), never a `DispatcherTimer` that toggles visibility. Elements that must blink TOGETHER follow one shared clock through an `ExpressionAnimation`; separately started loops drift apart.
- **End an expression with a STATIC set, never `StopAnimation`.** An `ExpressionAnimation` has no stop behavior, so on `StopAnimation` XAML writes the value of that moment back into the property (0 in a blink's "off" half). A static set cancels the facade animation with no write-back (`CUIElement::OnSetLocalOpacity` → `FacadeAnimationHelper::CancelSingleAnimation`, even for an unchanged value in the WinUI source). A key-frame loop that ends on its still value may still use `StopAnimation`.
- **Every `ElementCompositionPreview::GetElementVisual` forces a composition node on that element for life** (`m_isUsingHandOffVisual`), and the per-frame walk visits every node (UI_THREAD.md §10h). Get the compositor through `AgentCompositorOf`, which asks once per UI thread and caches the answer.
- Source: the WinUI source (`FacadeAnimationHelper.cpp`, `PropertySetListener.cpp`, `UIElement.cpp`, `hwwalk.cpp`), which is the lifted system XAML. The live effect is to be measured after the build: the XAML frame thread at ~3 wake-ups/s while a pulse runs (`tools/threadcensus.cs`).

### A per-element `ToolTip` on rebuilt elements is a process-killing LEAK under XAML Islands

- **A per-element `ToolTip` on rebuilt elements is a process-killing LEAK under XAML Islands — never `ToolTipService.SetToolTip` at BUILD time on any Manager-rebuilt surface.**
- The framework-side registration `SetToolTip` creates is never torn down under islands (the same broken bookkeeping that makes its auto-dismiss unreliable — the reason `AgentTipHelpers.h` exists), so every tipped element a board/tree/page rebuild recreates stays PEGGED with its whole element tree.
- Left unchecked, tip clusters accumulate into tens of GB of committed heap → system commit exhausted → XAML's composition pipeline took fatal allocation failures and latched dead → the "window pumps messages but never repaints and reacts to nothing" freeze (`IsHungAppWindow` stays FALSE — the app logic, hooks, autorunner all keep running; only pixels stop).
- `AgentTipHelpers.h` therefore keeps ALL tip state in ONE per-UI-thread host (one shared `ToolTip`, one open timer, a while-open watchdog); per element only two attached DPs + three CAPTURE-LESS handlers, and the service is touched only for the duration of a real hover-open (`SetToolTip(owner, tip)` right before `IsOpen(true)`, `SetToolTip(owner, nullptr)` on close — bounded by human hovers, not rebuild churn).
- Don't reintroduce per-element `ToolTip` objects, per-element capturing handlers, or build-time `SetToolTip` — and treat any per-element allocation on a per-refresh-rebuilt surface as a leak until proven torn down.

### A handler that captures its own element is never freed — and a rebuilt surface multiplies it

- **C++/WinRT never collects a reference cycle.** An element owns the delegates of its events; a delegate that captures that same element BY VALUE owns the element. Neither is ever freed, nor anything the element holds (its children, brushes, other handlers' captures).
- **The freeze of 2026-10-14** (UI_THREAD.md §12): every per-tab overlay's three resize grips captured themselves in their hover handlers; a re-bind loop (OBSERVER.md §20) built ~175,000 overlays, and the release held 194,698 grip sets. The board's column ScrollViewers, made new on every rebuild, were not freed either (2,920, each with its cards; the holder is not proven). XAML's event manager searches its per-element lists linearly, so the leaked elements made every rebuild slower (~4 s).
- **The rule:** a handler reaches its own element through `sender` (`sender.try_as<T>()`) or a weak reference (`winrt::make_weak(x)`), and the page / content through `get_weak()` when the element lives as long as the content. A capture-list entry that names the element the handler is registered on is a leak.
- **A surface that rebuilds on every registry change must not build a long-lived element per rebuild:** keep the containers (the board's `BoardColumnView` columns, the side panel's cards — SIDE_PANEL.md §2c) and refill only what changed.

### Per-tab XAML is multiplied by ~700: a measured TextBlock keeps ~17 KB, a string tooltip is a whole `ToolTip`

- **Measured on production's dumps (2026-10-19 → 20, [`MEMORY_LEAKS.md`](doc/agentmaster/MEMORY_LEAKS.md))**, 701 tabs, 1.8 GB of live heap. Nothing here is a leak; every cost below is paid again for each tab, card or row and stays until it closes.
- **A TextBlock that has been measured keeps its DirectWrite layout** (`CTextBlock` +0x1F8 → `DWriteTextLayout` → `TextLayout` → `DWriteTextAnalyzer` → a 16 KB scratch block): ~17 KB per TextBlock, even on a hidden page or a collapsed element. 30,683 such blocks = 479 MB, a quarter of the live heap, 98.7% of them held by a live analyzer. Prefer fewer live TextBlocks (virtualized lists, a hidden page's rows released) over per-element micro-savings.
- **`ToolTipService::SetToolTip(element, box_value(text))` creates a XAML `ToolTip` at once** (with template settings and a size-changed handler, ~1.8 KB). `Tab::_CreateContextMenu` builds ~97 menu items and ~45 such tooltips in every `Tab`'s constructor: 70,952 MenuFlyoutItems and 47,976 ToolTips (68 a tab) alive for menus that are rarely opened (~205 MB). A per-tab or per-row menu should be an empty flyout filled in its `Opening` handler, with tooltips through `AgentSetTip` (no `ToolTip` object until a hover).
- **Measure with `tools/leakhunt`** (snapshot dump of the running app, vftable census, segment-heap walk, owner attribution) before and after a change of this kind; a single dump also counts freed objects, so compare LIVE objects across two dumps. ⚠ Check the walk against objects whose number you know (one live `Tab` per open tab) first: the walker's first version misread the 26200 LFH bitmap and called half of them free.

### A rounded element WITH children is a composition node that every frame walks — round a childless background instead

- XAML gives a `FrameworkElement` whose `CornerRadius` is non-zero AND that has children its own composition node, so it can clip the children to the curve (`CFrameworkElement::UpdateRequiresCompNodeForRoundedCorners`, WinUI `framework.cpp`), and every frame of the window walks every node (UI_THREAD.md §10h). A childless rounded `Border` is plain render data: no node.
- Production's 1,466 side-panel cards had two each (the card `Border` holding the body, the band holding the title): 81% of the window's 3,634 nodes, ~7 ms per frame (UI_THREAD.md §13c). Fixed in code on 2026-10-20 (SIDE_PANEL.md §2f, in production since 0.6.8.23 and dev since 0.0.1.32).
- For a rounded look on a list item: a `Grid` with a CHILDLESS rounded `Border` behind the content in the same cell. ⚠ A `Grid` / `StackPanel` / `RelativePanel` / `ContentPresenter` has a `CornerRadius` too, and the compiler won't object: setting it on the container brings the node back.
- Stock control templates are rounded with content (a `Button`, a `TabViewItem`'s container): one node per instance. Don't put one per row in a long list. The Triage Board's cards (`AgentManagerContent::_MakeCard`) still have the old two shapes.

### Working-dir comparison must be filesystem-aware

- (else the Explorer Tree forks one dir into multiple roots).
- Windows is case-INsensitive (`C:\…\Desktop` == `…\desktop`) and treats `/`≡`\`; POSIX is case-SENSITIVE with `\` a literal char.
- Route every dir grouping/scope/match through `PathEq`/`NormPath` (`#ifdef _WIN32` → `CompareStringOrdinal` ignoreCase; `#else` → exact) — never raw `==`/`find`.

### The hook wire's `prompt` field must be escaped

- **The hook wire's `prompt` field must be escaped — and the PowerShell escape must mirror `WireEscape` byte-for-byte.**
- The forwarder appends the `UserPromptSubmit` prompt as the trailing TAB-separated wire field; a real prompt has embedded TAB/newline, which would break the single-line, TAB-split, newline-framed record.
- Both the PowerShell forwarder and `BuildWireLine` escape it identically — `\ → \\` FIRST, then tab/CR/LF → `\t \r \n` — and the bridge `WireUnescape`s it.
- The PowerShell side is the one seam the C++ tests can't cover, so verify it with a parity check (`C:\src` → `C:\\src`, `https://` survives, no raw newline/tab remains).
- PowerShell gotcha: `-replace '\\','\\'` (pattern is regex = one backslash; replacement is literal = two) does the backslash-doubling, and it MUST run before the tab/CR/LF replacements or it would double the backslashes they introduce.

### A light-dismiss flyout eats the press that dismisses it — a transient ShowMode does not change that here

- A `MenuFlyout` / `Flyout` is light-dismiss: a press outside it closes it, and XAML gives that press to NO element (`CPopupRoot::OnPointerPressed` closes the topmost light-dismiss popup and marks the press handled). The 2026-10-10 hover menu over "Launch Claude" (`ShowMode` `TransientWithDismissOnPointerMoveAway`, opened by PointerEntered) relied on the WinUI 3 source, where a transient flyout makes the root visual its `OverlayInputPassThroughElement` so every press passes through. The installed app does not behave so: Launch needed a DOUBLE-click (the user's report, 2026-10-19). The first press only closed the menu, the next pointer move reopened it, and only a double-click with no move between its presses reached the button.
- An explicit `OverlayInputPassThroughElement` (the documented fix) cannot reach an element inside ANOTHER popup, such as the side panel's "+" card: WinUI's pass-through re-hit-test disables the whole popup root, so it finds only the main tree under the card.
- What works (`AgentLaunchBar`, 2026-10-19, in production since 0.6.8.23 and dev since 0.0.1.31): arm the press in the flyout's `Closing` (raised synchronously during the press) when `GetAsyncKeyState` shows the physical primary button down, and complete the click on the button's `PointerReleased` (handledEventsToo) when the press point — the release point moved by the cursor's screen delta, in DIPs — lies on the button. Don't reopen a hover flyout while such a press is armed. Read `GetAsyncKeyState`, not `GetKeyState`: the island's pointer input may never update the message-time state.
- **That was not enough inside another popup** (SP-K1, measured by the e2e suite on 0.8.0: the side panel's "+" card still needed two clicks while the page's row took one; the test build's `[launchbar]` lines of 2026-10-23 showed why — the press is armed in the card too, but NO release reaches its Launch, which sits in a Popup). Since 2026-10-23 (NOT BUILT yet): the arm ALSO accepts the pointer message being processed when it is the primary button going down (`GetPointerInfo` for the pointer last seen on the button — the app calls `EnableMouseInPointer`, so the mouse is a pointer; this one holds even when a busy UI thread processes the press after the physical release), and a release WATCH (a 15 ms DispatcherTimer from the arm until the release) completes an armed press that no release event completed: once the physical button has read up twice, it judges the press and release screen points with the last pointer sample on the button (a point at that sample is on the button; any other is mapped by the screen delta). A click that did not move needs no coordinate mapping at all. Every arm, completion and decline is a `[launchbar]` line — read hooks.log before theorizing about a lost click.
- Any new hover-opened flyout over a clickable element has the same trap: check that a click on the element still lands.

### Path-picker Popup placement.

- Parent the `Primitives::Popup` into the content root (top/left aligned) so its `Horizontal/VerticalOffset` is root-relative; the popup's own layout anchor already carries the root's offset within the XAML island, so the two compose to the box's on-screen position.
- Open it only on `FocusState::Pointer`/`Keyboard` (not `Programmatic`) so it doesn't pop on tab activation, and keep it open across row clicks via a **deferred** `LostFocus` check (re-focus the box on pick; bail if it regained focus).
- ⚠ **Read that focus state in `GettingFocus`, never in `GotFocus`.** XAML coerces a programmatic focus before `GotFocus`: `Focus(FocusState::Programmatic)` sets the element's `FocusState` to **Pointer** after a mouse / touch / pen input and to **Keyboard** after a key (WinUI `CFocusManager::UpdateFocus` → `CoerceFocusState`), and `GotFocus` is raised later, asynchronously. `GettingFocus` is raised synchronously with the uncoerced state. `AgentLaunchBar` records it there (`_cwdBoxFocusArrival`). Before 2026-10-12 it read `GotFocus`, so the side panel's "+" card, which focuses its box programmatically right after the "+" click, opened the folder list on every "+" (the Agent Manager page met the same coercion earlier and focuses an invisible sink instead of its box).
- ⚠ **`TextChanged` is asynchronous too** (WinUI `CTextBox::UpdateTextProperty` posts it through the event queue). A handler that opens the list "because the box has focus" also opens it for a programmatic `Text` write whose event arrives after a later focus. `AgentLaunchBar::_WriteBoxTextProgrammatically` marks such a write (`_programmaticTextPending`): its `TextChanged` re-lists an open list but opens no closed one.
- ⚠ **A popup's child moves to new offsets only in the NEXT layout pass.** A `HorizontalOffset` / `VerticalOffset` write only invalidates the popup root's arrange (WinUI `CPopup::SetValue`; the child is placed in `CPopupRoot::ArrangeOverride`). A `TransformToVisual` read before that pass returns the child's previous place, or (0,0) for a child never laid out. So anchoring one popup to an element inside another popup that just opened or moved needs a read after the next layout pass: `AgentLaunchBar::_ArmPathPickerReanchor` (a one-shot `LayoutUpdated`, then a queued re-fit). Without it, the "+" card's folder list was anchored at the card's top-left, over the box and the Launch button (2026-10-12; in dev since 0.0.1.6, production since 0.6.8.20). To be right in the FIRST frame, run that pass yourself before the read: `UIElement::UpdateLayout()` arranges popup children like any element (it does nothing when nothing is dirty) — the "+" card calls it on itself before it opens its folder list (`_SidePanelFinishLaunchCardOpen`, 2026-10-19, in production since 0.6.8.23 and dev since 0.0.1.31). Never from inside a layout-driven handler: XAML skips the call there (the layout manager's reentrancy guard).

### Erasing a map node invalidates references to its key — copy the key first.

- `_BindClaudeSession- ToTab`'s re-home block did `for (const auto& [oldId, w] : _claudeTabs) { … _claudeTabs.erase(oldId); _claudeOverlays.erase(oldId); }` — `oldId` is a reference INTO the `_claudeTabs` node, so the first erase freed it and the second `erase(oldId)` hashed **freed memory** → AV `0xc0000005` in `std::_Fnv1a_append_bytes` (`std::hash<wstring>`).
- Latent for ages (re-home was rare); the Fleet Observer's far more frequent correlation/re-home **exposed it**.
- Fix: `const std::wstring superseded = oldId;` BEFORE any erase.
- (General rule: never use a `[key, val]` structured-binding ref after erasing that element.)

### A `+`-tab claude does NOT inherit our runtime env

- (`AM_SESSION` / `CCMGR_HOOK_PIPE` / the PATH shim are all absent), so a hand-typed `claude` there fires **zero hooks** and is classified `wt` by `AM_SESSION` alone.
- WT regenerates a tab's child env from the **registry** (`til::env::regenerate`) unless `reloadEnvironmentVariables` is genuinely OFF, which drops these runtime-only vars — so the "Adopt any `claude`" hook fast-path is **degraded for `+` tabs** (a Manager-Launched session is fine: it sets env explicitly via `spec.env`).
- This is **why the observer keys binding on the roster correlation, not `AM_SESSION`** (Rule #13): a claude under one of our tabs' shells is ours regardless.
- The observer + the transcript-tail reconciler give it full detection + state without hooks; restoring the hook fast-path for `+` tabs (fixing the env regeneration) is a separate, non-blocking follow-up.

### A PATH longer than 8191 chars makes cmd.exe resolve NO command — Claude's Ctrl+G dies "exit code 1"

- **cmd.exe cannot resolve ANY external command once its PATH exceeds 8191 chars** — not "the tail is ignored": nothing, System32 included, wherever it sits (measured cliff: 8173 resolves, 8227 fails; `cmd /c` exits **1** with "'x' is not recognized").
- Claude Code's Ctrl+G (`chat:externalEditor`) runs `cmd.exe /d /s /c "<EDITOR> "<tmp>""` inside its alternate screen, so an overlong PATH reads as a one-frame "flash" (cmd's error) + **"Edit quit unexpectedly (exit code 1)"** — `edit.exe` never even starts. Every other `cmd /c` breaks the same way, incl. our own `am-update.cmd` / `am-uninstall.cmd` (bare `powershell`).
- The 2026-09-25 incident: 101 DEAD `%TEMP%\docfx-hub-runtime-tests\<guid>\data\dotnet-home\.dotnet\tools` entries in the registry **User Path** (the .NET SDK first-run appends `<DOTNET_CLI_HOME>\.dotnet\tools` per fresh `DOTNET_CLI_HOME`) pushed every process to ~14.3K chars. It surfaced right after the side panel shipped and was **misattributed to the panel** for a whole investigation session — before blaming Agentmaster code for a console-app failure, reproduce the exact spawn (`cmd /d /s /c ...`) with the tab's real environment.
- Agentmaster now guards it (`AgentMaster/CmdPathLimit.h`, [`CMD_PATH_LIMIT.md`](doc/agentmaster/CMD_PATH_LIMIT.md)): the EXE prelude compacts the process PATH first thing + `ConptyConnection` compacts every tab's final block — dropping only duplicates/empties, then provably-missing dirs; a healthy PATH is untouched. It never writes the registry: the machine-wide cure is removing the dead entries from the User Path (and `DOTNET_ADD_GLOBAL_TOOLS_TO_PATH=0` in whatever sets a per-run `DOTNET_CLI_HOME`).
- Build corollary: the same wall is why a clean build from this machine's bash needs a docfx-stripped PATH (vcvars / `GenerateFeatureFlags.proj` shell out through cmd.exe).

### A GPU driver's DLL init can DEADLOCK the startup — the known folders are pre-warmed before `App{}`

- The 2026-09-30 release launch hung forever: no window, and every re-launch silently failed the single-instance handoff (it waits ~29 s for a message window that a stuck instance never creates, then exits).
- `App{}` spawns XAML's GPU thread, which loads the display driver. NVIDIA's `NvMemMapStoragex.dll` (driver 32.0.16.1656) calls `SHGetKnownFolderPath` from its DLL init while the loader is busy. At the same moment the main thread's `ReloadSettings` → PowerShell profile generator did the process's FIRST known-folder lookup, which holds `windows.storage`'s definition-cache lock and then needs the loader for a delay-loaded import. That is an ABBA deadlock.
- Agentmaster made it likely: the `AGENTMASTER_PROFILE` redirect in `GetBaseSettingsPath` skips upstream's early LocalAppData lookup.
- Fixed by `StartupGuard.h`. The pre-warm resolves every startup folder/flag combination on the main thread before `App{}`. A 25 s watchdog terminates a hung launch once per episode, logging `[startup-hang]`. Add any NEW startup-time `SHGetKnownFolderPath` folder/flag combination to `kStartupKnownFolderLookups`.
- A stuck process cannot relaunch itself (measured): while another thread holds the loader, `CreateProcessW` blocks and a newly created thread never runs. Only a thread created BEFORE the hang can log and `TerminateProcess`.
- **Crash dumps are keyed by EXE NAME.** HKLM `...\Windows Error Reporting\LocalDumps\WindowsTerminal.exe` (full dumps) stopped matching when the GUI binary became `Agentmaster.exe`, so the 2026-09-29 heap-corruption crash left no dump. Add the same key for `Agentmaster.exe` (admin; STARTUP_GUARD.md §6).

### Every shown terminal owns its own GPU device — past ~62 in one process, a new tab can stay BLACK

- AtlasEngine builds one D3D11 device per `TermControl` and keeps it until the tab is CLOSED. Hidden tabs keep theirs; restored tabs never focused have none, and neither should Deactivated ones (Deactivate closes the control and parks a never-initialized one — the restored state; not yet measured). NVIDIA starts 20 `nvwgf2umx.dll` threads per device, so NVIDIA threads ÷ 20 counts them from outside.
- Once the release holds ~62, the NVIDIA driver hang-recovers at every new tab or focus: System log `nvlddmkm` event 153, "Error occurred on GPUID: 100", ~3 s each. Two storms began at exactly 62 (2026-09-25, 10-01), and each ended only with a restart.
- A tab opened during a storm can stay BLACK for good while its claude runs fine (10-01, "sysd catalog"). The renderer never registers a failure: `d3d10warp.dll` never loads, so the WARP fallback never runs, and the render thread idles on `Renderer::_redraw`. Nothing retries.
- Before blaming the session, read its console (`am_inspect.py screen`): a ready input box behind a black tab means rendering. Remedies: the cog's **Software rendering** switch (re-surfaces every terminal live), closing viewed tabs, or a restart. RENDERING.md §5 has the read-only diagnosis.
- A HIDDEN terminal kept painting: upstream, nothing in `Renderer`, `AtlasEngine` or `TermControl` checks visibility, so a background claude mid-turn rendered ~3–11 frames/s that no one saw. And each device costs even when idle: 2 of its NVIDIA threads poll ~68 times/s for the device's whole life (RENDERING.md §4b, measured with `tools/devidle.cpp`).
