RENDERING.md §4c (built + dev-deployed 2026-10-03, not in a release yet) fixes both: off-screen terminals are suspended (`Renderer::SetPaintingSuspended`, driven by `TermControl`'s `Loaded`/`Unloaded` + window minimize) and, after 60 s hidden, drop their device (`IRenderEngine::ReleaseDeviceResources`). ⚠ A suspended render thread parks in `_waitWhileSuspended` on the `_suspended` WaitOnAddress — anything that must wake it (teardown, a resume) has to CHANGE that flag first, or the wake-up can be lost and a join hangs. To look at a live instance, use `tools/threadcensus.cs` — read-only, it never suspends a thread (unlike `tools/livesample.exe`) — and `tools/renderwait.cs`, which shows each render thread's wait target (`_suspended` = parked off screen, `_redraw` = drawable and idle, `_enable` = painting disabled after failures), the `Renderer` flags read from memory, and when the wait began (a parked thread's wait start is its device release; checked 2026-10-03: 27–49 ms after its 60 s deadline).

### DWM iconic thumbnails — store right after every invalidate, and never exceed DWM's box

- A window with `DWMWA_FORCE_ICONIC_REPRESENTATION` + `DWMWA_HAS_ICONIC_BITMAP` shows the app's own bitmap in the taskbar flyout and in Alt+Tab (the taskbar preview frame, NOTIFICATIONS.md §9). Four traps, all measured on a disposable Windows 11 25H2 desktop on 2026-10-06:
  - Alt+Tab shows the window it pre-selects BLACK when DWM holds no stored bitmap at that moment, and never asks again during that Alt+Tab. Never leave the store empty: `DwmInvalidateIconicBitmaps`, then `DwmSetIconicThumbnail` at once.
  - Without an invalidation, DWM keeps the FIRST peek (live-preview) bitmap forever.
  - A bitmap larger than DWM's maximum gets `E_INVALIDARG`, and the maximum arrives only in `WM_DWMSENDICONICTHUMBNAIL`'s lParam (HIWORD = width, LOWORD = height): 200x108 for a 704x381 window at 100%.
  - `PrintWindow` without `PW_RENDERFULLCONTENT` (0x2) captures a XAML Islands / swap-chain window BLACK.
- Rejected because Alt+Tab items went black: a pre-rendered live-preview bitmap, and `DWMWA_DISALLOW_PEEK`.

### A killed agent leaves its fullscreen terminal modes on — the managed host puts the console back (the console guard)

- **The host:** every managed launch is `pwsh -NoLogo -NoExit -EncodedCommand <script>` (`BuildPwshHostedCommandline`), so when claude or codex exits the tab drops to a `PS <cwd>>` prompt. A clean exit undoes the agent's own terminal modes; a kill or a crash does not.
- **The incident (2026-10-21, dev 0.0.1.33):** tab 2b655223's claude (fullscreen renderer, `CLAUDE_CODE_NO_FLICKER=1`) died at 18:56:55 with no `SessionEnd` and no exit-time transcript line. C: ran out of space that second (Volsnap events 13 / 35; a long-running codex.exe aborted the same second). The alternate screen, mouse tracking 1000/1002/1003 + SGR, focus reports, kitty keyboard flags and the raw VT input mode (`0x208`) stayed on. PSReadLine, which switches to its own escape decoder when it finds VT input on, then typed every mouse move as text (`[555;54;31M`: `ESC [ <3 5` read as Alt+3, a repeat count, then `5`) and Ctrl+C arrived as `[99;5u`.
- **The fix — `WrapPwshScriptInConsoleGuard` (ClaudeSpawn.h, NOT BUILT yet):** before the agent, save the CONIN$/CONOUT$ modes through reflection on PowerShell's own `Microsoft.PowerShell.ConsoleControl` (the same four methods in 7.5 and 5.1, no Add-Type compile); in `finally`, write the VT reset (kitty pop → `?1049l` → pop; DECRST 1000/1002/1003/1004/1005/1006/1007/1015/1016/2004/2026/2031; DECSTR; OSC 8 / 9;4), restore the saved modes, and on a non-zero exit print one note naming the tab menu's Restart session. ConPTY interprets the reset AND passes it through to Agentmaster's terminal (it re-requests focus reports for itself, as it must). Everything is a no-op after a clean exit, so it runs unconditionally.
  - ⚠ Call those methods directly, never through a helper scriptblock: a value passed through `$args` arrives wrapped in a PSObject and `MethodBase.Invoke` refuses it (measured).
  - ⚠ Write the VT reset BEFORE restoring the output mode: Windows PowerShell's own mode may lack VT processing, which would print the reset as text.
  - ⚠ The guard costs ~2,250 command-line chars: `kHandoverPromptEscapedBudget` dropped from 11,500 to 10,000, and `tests_spawn_sched.cpp` builds the worst case (a preset, long paths, a budget-sized prompt) against 32,767.
- **Verified** in a private ConPTY (`tools/console-guard-probe/`: a stand-in that dies the same way, and a real claude 2.1.291 killed with TerminateProcess): unguarded → the garbage and input mode `0x208`; guarded → a clean prompt and the saved `0x1F7`, on PowerShell 7.5.8 and 5.1. Run the probes with `< /dev/null`: with stdout redirected to a file, pywinpty gave the shell the parent's stdin, a pipe that carried Claude Code's own forwarded mouse input.
- **Open (NOT FIXED):** a killed claude never reaches `Done`. Only its `SessionEnd` hook sets that state, and the liveness sweep judges the ConPTY connection, which the `-NoExit` host keeps alive, so the session keeps its last state ("Waiting for you") in the Manager, on the tab and in Telegram. The Fleet Observer does see it (`[activity] wt=… claude -> pwsh`).
- **Recover such a tab today:** the tab menu's Restart session (a new connection; the conversation resumes).

### Symbolize a crash without cdb/WinDbg via the in-box DIA SDK + DbgHelp.

- When the app AVs, the WER **Application Error** event already gives `Faulting module` + `Exception code` + `Fault offset` (the RVA), and a full dump lands in `%LOCALAPPDATA%\CrashDumps\`.
- No debugger is installed, but `msdia140.dll` + the **DIA SDK** ship with VS 2022 (`…\DIA SDK\include\dia2.h`, `…\lib\amd64\ diaguids.lib`).
- A ~60-line C++ tool can `NoRegCoCreate(msdia140.dll)` → `loadDataFromPdb(TerminalApp.pdb)` → `findSymbolByRVA(rva, SymTagFunction)` + `findLinesByRVA` to map the fault offset to **function + source line** (link `diaguids.lib ole32 oleaut32 advapi32`).
- For the full call chain, `DbgHelp`'s `MiniDumpReadDumpStream` (ExceptionStream → faulting thread id; ThreadListStream → its stack memory; ModuleListStream → `TerminalApp.dll` base/size) + scanning the stack for in-module return addresses, symbolized through the same DIA session, reconstructs the stack top-down.
- This is how the re-home use-after-free above was pinned exactly.

### A fire_and_forget coroutine can drop the LAST page ref on a POOL thread, destructing the whole UI tree off the UI thread

- **A fire_and_forget coroutine can drop the LAST page ref on a POOL thread, destructing the whole UI tree off the UI thread — where any UI-affine call in a destructor becomes `std::terminate`.**
- The crash (`0xC0000409`, `ucrtbase+0xA527E`, `ExceptionInformation[0]==7` == `FAST_FAIL_FATAL_APP_EXIT`): closing a window while the ~2 s `_ObserverProbe` tick was in flight holding `get_strong()` meant the **coroutine frame's destruction on the threadpool thread** released the final reference, so `~TerminalPage → ~Tab → ~Pane → ~AgentManagerContent` all ran on that thread, and `DispatcherTimer.Stop()` (UI-thread-affine) threw `RPC_E_WRONG_THREAD` **inside a noexcept destructor** → terminate → the WHOLE process (all windows — one WindowEmperor process) died from ONE window closing.
- Note `~TerminalPage` itself was ALREADY guarded for exactly this mechanism; the **destructor cascade it triggers** was not — a guard on the coroutine BODY does not cover the FRAME's destruction.
- So: (1) every destructor reachable from a page teardown wraps UI-affine calls in `try { … } CATCH_LOG();` — done in `~AgentManagerContent` (3 timer `Stop()`s) and `SafeDispatcherTimer::Destroy()` (the latent twin: `~Tab`'s `_bellIndicatorTimer`, which only escaped the live crash because that 4-second-old window had never shown a bell); (2) release/revoke lines stay OUTSIDE the try so refs always drop; (3) a thread-safe engine detach (`RemoveObserver`) stays BARE on purpose — it must never be skipped by a guarded throw; and (4) **every `fire_and_forget` needs a terminate-net** — an escaped exception there IS `winrt::terminate`, the same `0xC0000409` (16 lanes audited; the pattern for one needing `co_await` is to split the body into an awaited `IAsyncAction`, as `_AdoptExternalSession`/`_AdoptExternalSessionImpl` and `_SweepClaudeLiveness` do).
- A catch that re-arms a latch (`_claudeMissingPromptShowing`, `_sessionsIndexing`) must keep doing so, or the feature is dead for the window's life.
- Not-yet-done hardening: ending each lane with a best-effort hop back to the dispatcher so the last ref normally releases on the UI thread.

### ⚠ The exception-forensics logging path must never log its own failures

- **⚠ The exception-forensics logging path must never log its own failures — 9 `catch (...)` sites in `ClaudeSpawn.cpp` are DELIBERATELY BARE.**
- The *never lose a swallowed exception* policy (Rule #18) says every `catch (...)` reports — with exactly one class of exemption, and it is a hard constraint, not a judgement call.
- The chain is `LogSwallowedException → LogSwallowedExceptionCore → ExcLogThrottleAllow / CaptureRecentThrowStacksText → FormatAddressModuleRva → AppendStateLog → AgentmasterStateDir`, so logging from any link re-enters it.
- Two links are worse than unbounded recursion: **`AgentmasterStateDir` is called BY `AppendStateLog`** to build its path (throw → log → throw → …), and **`AppendStateLog` holds a NON-RECURSIVE `std::mutex`**, so logging its own failure **self-deadlocks** before it could even recurse — i.e. "completing" the sweep here wedges the app on the first failed log.
- Each of the nine carries an explicit `⚠ DELIBERATELY BARE` marker naming its degradation, under one authoritative `LOGGING-PATH RECURSION RULE` block listing them all.
- (The classifier's terminal `catch (...)` — which RECORDS `"unknown exception"` — is explicitly *not* one of them, and is annotated so it isn't "fixed" either.)

### A managed session's conversation id can DIVERGE from its launch id

- **A managed session's conversation id can DIVERGE from its launch id — attribute state + bind by Claude's CURRENT conversation, never the pinned launch id.**
- `/resume` (into another conversation) and `/clear` switch a live claude's active `session_id` (a `/compact` does NOT — it is in-place, same id + same file; HOOKS.md *Compaction*), while the id we launched it with stays **pinned** on the process **cmdline** (`--session-id`) AND in the **`CCMGR_SESSION_ID`** env.
- Two places trusted the launch id and stranded the session: a claude launched `--session-id A` was running conversation `B` (per its OWN presence heartbeat `sessions/<pid>.json` + the `B` transcript), with every hook mis-filed under `A` and **no transcript under `A`**.
- (1) The **hook forwarder** resolved the id ENV-first, so every post-divergence hook fed the dead `A` record — which owns no transcript, so the SessionScanner's missed-`Stop` reconciler (it reads a terminal `stop_reason` from the transcript tail) could never release it → stuck `NeedsApproval`, then `Idle` while `B` ran (a compaction's `SessionStart` reset it to Idle — since fixed: `SessionStart(source="compact")` keeps a running turn Running — and the auto-continuation fires no `UserPromptSubmit`).
- (2) The **Fleet Observer** bound the tab via the cmdline `--session-id` (`ResolveObservedId`), so the tab-strip dot showed `A`'s phantom state, not `B`.
- **Fix:** the forwarder is **payload-first** (the hook `session_id` is Claude's current conversation; the env is only the fallback), and the observer prefers Claude's **own presence heartbeat** (pid-keyed, validated against PID reuse by `PresenceRowMatchesProcess` — the exact `procStart`, else the `startedAt` window) over the cmdline id when they disagree — matching the `agentmaster` CLI's bind precedence (HOOKS.md *Session correlation*, OBSERVER.md §8b). External rows + Adopt use the same presence-first id.
- Both are no-ops until an actual divergence (a fresh spawn's first conversation has payload id == `--session-id` == the env).
- **Latent corollaries (not fixed):** the scanner can't reconcile a LIVE session with **no transcript at all** (`ShouldSynthesizeStop` needs a tail — a `live + active-state + zero-transcript + quiescent → release` safety net would self-heal one); and `/resume`-ing an already-open conversation into a managed tab is **two writers** on one transcript (the adopt-external hazard, now reachable manually).

### A `--fork-session` fork echoes its SOURCE id on the FIRST SessionStart, not the new id

- **A `--fork-session` fork echoes its SOURCE id on the FIRST SessionStart, not the new id — don't re-home the fork's tab onto it.**
- `claude --resume <src> --fork-session --session-id <new>` (the Sessions-page / duplicate-tab / adopt-external fork) writes a NEW transcript `<new>` and every steady hook lands on `<new>` — BUT its **first `SessionStart` hook fires under the SOURCE id `<src>`** (claude is "resuming `<src>`" at that instant, before it forks).
- **Fix:** a fork records `SessionInfo::forkParentId = <src>` (**persisted** — see the never-messaged-fork note below) AND its ConPTY's `tabToken` is stamped **eagerly at launch** (not lazily on the first hook — the echo arrives BEFORE any own-id hook).
- `SessionRegistry::OnHookEvent` then IGNORES a `SessionStart` for `<src>` whose `tabToken` matches a live fork carrying `forkParentId == <src>` (`[fork-echo] ignored …`); `_BindClaudeSessionToTab` has the same backstop.
- The guard is **one-shot** — cleared on the fork's first own-id hook — so a LATER deliberate `/resume <src>` in the fork's tab still re-homes normally.
- (Mirrors the observer's id resolution, which already prefers `--session-id` over `--resume`; the push side needed the same.)

### A `--fork-session` fork writes NO transcript until its FIRST turn

- **A `--fork-session` fork writes NO transcript until its FIRST turn — so a never-messaged fork must be RE-FORKED (not restore-freshed) on reopen, or its branch is lost.** Empirically (live, both profiles): forking `claude --resume <src> --fork-session --session-id <new>` does **not** create `<new>.jsonl` until the fork's first message — a fork the user created but never prompted has the SOURCE transcript on disk and **none** for `<new>`.
- The restore path is transcript-gated (`ClaudeConversationExists`), so on every window restart such a fork failed `wantResume`, fell to **`[restore-fresh]`**, and came back as a brand-new EMPTY conversation with the same `"(fork)"` title but a **churned id** — the forked context silently gone.
- **Fix:** `SessionInfo::forkParentId` is now **persisted** (Persistence.cpp `ToJson`/`SessionFromJson`, omitted when empty), and `_LaunchClaudeSession` detects the case — when a restored record has `!wantResume` (the fork's own transcript is absent) AND a `forkParentId` whose source transcript still exists, it **re-forks from the source into the SAME id** (`[restore->refork]`): `BuildClaudeSpawn`'s new `forkIntoSessionId` param forks back into the fork's existing id instead of minting, so the fork keeps its identity (and its `WindowRecord` tab ref) across restarts.
- Gated on `!wantResume`, so a fork that LATER got its own transcript is always resumed, never re-forked off a now-divergent source (the registry's one-shot first-own-hook clear also wipes `forkParentId` the moment the fork produces content, persisted on the next change); if the source ALSO vanished, it falls through to a fresh launch.
- The re-fork's source-id echo is handled by the existing guard above (the fork is live with `forkParentId == <src>` + the eager `tabToken`).
- **Every DISPLAY surface reads the fork SOURCE's transcript until the fork has its own** — through ONE resolver, `ResolveClaudeDisplayTranscript(id, forkParentId)` (`ClaudeSpawn.h`; own transcript first, then the source's, flagged with the source id). The 2026-10-05 report: on a fresh, unprompted fork, Show Transcript said "Nothing to show yet", the Manager's Summary pane "No messages recorded", Copy ▸ Summary / Transcript did nothing, and the tab hover card showed no body (only the per-tab / side-panel summary panel had its own fallback, since 88cb5e415). Now all of them show the source's conversation; Show Transcript and Copy ▸ Transcript prepend `ForkSourceTranscriptNote` (one italic line naming the source), the summaries fall back silently like the panel. Never CACHE a fallback path (the hover card and the panel don't): the fork's first message must switch the view to its own transcript. The mechanics (resume / re-fork / fresh, the scanner tail) keep `ResolveClaudeTranscriptPath` — the display fallback must never decide a launch. ⚠ The WHOLE source transcript is shown: a RUNNING fork holds only the source lines written before its claude started (a dormant fork re-forks from the current source when it wakes, so for it the view is exact) — TRANSCRIPT_OVERLAY.md §3e.
- Codex has the SAME class of issue (a never-prompted Codex fork has no rollout) — **not yet fixed** (its source-rollout uuid isn't persisted; tracked as a follow-up).

### Tab title — the mechanism + its traps (the MECHANISM behind Rule #11).

- A tab carries TWO title channels: the user-rename **override** `Tab::_runtimeTabText`, and the active control's OSC/profile/`StartingTitle` title.
- `Tab::Title()`/`_GetActiveTitle()` returns the override when set, else the control title — but **`Tab::GetTabText()` returns ONLY the override** (the one fact the two title sync directions hinge on; missing it is easy — even a careful audit mis-read it as "the OSC title leaks").
- A managed Claude/Codex tab **pins** the override (`SetTabText`), so claude's volatile OSC title is hidden AND can never reach the registry: the reverse mirror `_SyncClaudeTitleFromTab` reads `GetTabText()` (the override), while the OSC `TitleChanged` handler only calls `UpdateTitle()` (sets `Title()`, never the override).
- An UNBOUND `+`-tab claude has an EMPTY override, so it floats with the OSC title until the observer binds it (`_BindClaudeSessionToTab` then pins the managed/derived name; a name the user already gave the `+` tab wins).
- **Every registry→tab pin MUST go through `_SetClaudeTabTextPinned`** (the `_pinningClaudeTabTitle` latch): `SetTabText` synchronously raises `PropertyChanged("Title")` → `_UpdateTitle` → `_SyncClaudeTitleFromTab`, so a raw pin would mirror our OWN write back into the registry — and an async observer that captured a now-stale title would write it back and the tab⇄registry directions **ping-pong forever** (the observed `/clear` title-swap + `[Unknown]` flood).
- The latch is a plain bool, UI-thread-only and synchronous-safe (set → `SetTabText` → re-entry no-ops → cleared, one stack frame, `wil::scope_exit`); the cross-window re-pin (`_SyncClaudeTabTitleFromRegistry`) reads the title **FRESH** on the UI thread, never the captured snapshot.
- **Persistence is split by tab kind:** a managed tab persists as a sessionId **ref** in the `WindowRecord` (the title rides `sessions.json` — Option 1, `SessionInfo.title`); a shell tab persists its `_runtimeTabText` as a `RenameTab` action inside `actionsJson` (`BuildStartupActions`).
- The two are mutually exclusive (`_ClaudeSessionForTab` non-empty → ref, empty → Other), so a managed tab is never ALSO replayed as a RenameTab-bearing shell; on restore a managed tab re-pins from `SessionInfo.title`, a shell tab replays its `RenameTab`.
- **On-disk DISPLAY titles are a SEPARATE concept** — the Sessions browser / CLI / external-row enrichment derive one from the transcript via `PickDisplayTitle` (precedence `customTitle > aiTitle > summary > firstPrompt`); it is **display-only** and never written over a managed `SessionInfo.title` (resume/fork-from-disk seed a record's title only when the session is UNKNOWN to the registry — `_ResumeSessionFromDisk` / `_ForkSessionFromDisk` gate on `!existing`).
- `DeriveSessionTitle` (the cwd-derived default) is never empty, so a managed title can't go blank; renames are trimmed/rejected on BOTH entry points (`_CommitRename` and `_SyncClaudeTitleFromTab`).

### Claude's Ctrl+S stash is NOT a discard

- **Claude's Ctrl+S stash is NOT a discard — it AUTO-RESTORES into the input box ~0.4s after the NEXT submit** (the status line reads `› stashed` while the slot is loaded, and the pop survives idle indefinitely).
- Any clear that stashes and WALKS AWAY therefore plants a scheduled re-paste for whichever submit comes next — the mail-button MOVE-clear did exactly that, and the moved draft reappeared right after its own queued copy was delivered, reading as a double-send because the stash content and the queued prompt are the same bytes.
- The DRAFT SWAP may keep its stash rung ONLY because its restore always CONSUMES the slot (deliberate pop, or the auto-pop + verify-first).
- Clearing keys: fast double-`Esc` truly clears at idle but **`Esc` INTERRUPTS a running turn even with text in the box** (and fast double-Esc on an EMPTY box opens the Rewind menu) — never an automation rung; **`End`+`Ctrl+U` / `End`+backspaces are true discards AND mid-turn safe** (killed text returns only via a manual `Ctrl+Y`; `Ctrl+U` is caret→line-start so `End` must precede it; blind backspaces stall at position 0 with lines below; a `[Pasted text #N]` placeholder deletes atomically on one press) — the mail-move's `DecideDraftDiscard` ladder is built on exactly these facts (PENDING_INPUT.md §8d/§9).

### Claude Code 2.1.277+ CANCELS an Enter that finds an "invisible" character — and its editor holds NFC text, deleted by grapheme

- **The Enter-time cleanup** (2.1.280 bundle: `wZe`, called from the submit path) removes zero-width characters, bidi controls and direction marks, tag characters, variation selectors, C0/C1 controls, SHY and fillers, and rewrites VT/FF/NEL/LS/PS/lone-CR to LF — and when it removed ANYTHING the submit is **cancelled** (*"Removed N invisible characters · review and press Enter to send"*, the cleaned text left in the box). A launch-commandline prompt is cleaned and sent — the cancel is Enter-only.
- **It is context-sensitive, so never approximate it**: the VS16 of a warning-sign emoji, emoji ZWJ sequences, Indic/Arabic-family joiners, Thai/Khmer ZWSP, a Persian ZWNJ, and **an LRM/RLM on a line holding Hebrew/Arabic letters** (unless redundant between same-direction letters or before a mark) are KEPT. A superset strip damages Hebrew/emoji text; a subset still trips the cancel. The ONLY sanctioned implementation is `ClaudeInvisibleChars` (DELIVERY.md §15), pinned to claude's own outputs by a generated table — re-run `tools/claude-invisible-oracle/` when a Claude Code release touches the rule.
- **Anything that types text into a Claude box must go through `BuildPromptFill`** (whose body is `PromptFillBody` = the sanitize) — never a raw `Inject(text)`. **Any compare of a prompt against claude's view of it** (a hook/transcript echo, a box read-back, a dedupe) must go through `NormalizePromptTextForCompare` (directly, via `FoldTrimPromptText` / `DraftMatchesPromptText` / `PromptTextsEquivalent`, or the swap's `DraftSwapCompareKey`) — an exact compare fails on exactly the prompts the cleanup touched.
- **Claude's editor NFC-normalizes what it holds** (its `MeasuredText` constructor) — the box renders NFC and the submit is NFC, so Hebrew niqqud typed dagesh-before-vowel reads back REORDERED; the compare key applies NFC for that reason. And its cursor steps/deletes by **grapheme cluster** (`Intl.Segmenter`), so a count of "editor characters" (the Foreign-undo budget) must be `CountGraphemeClusters`, never a UTF-16 length.

### Claude Code's FULLSCREEN renderer lives in the ALTERNATE screen buffer — never skip the alt buffer when reading Claude's box

- **`CLAUDE_CODE_NO_FLICKER=1`** (set in this machine's `~/.claude/settings.json`) or **`/tui fullscreen`** selects Claude Code's fullscreen renderer. A server-side "feature of the week" flag can also turn it on, so it can appear without any local setting.
- It writes `ESC[?1049h` before its first frame, draws its WHOLE UI in the **alt buffer**, and turns mouse tracking on.
- The input box keeps its shape: the `❯` + NBSP caret row between two full-width flush-left rules. It sits at the bottom of a viewport-sized screen, with a footer row below it. There is **no terminal scrollback**; claude virtual-scrolls the conversation itself.
- **"In the alt buffer" therefore no longer means "a child full-screen app owns the screen".** `132e19b5d` (2026-09-18) assumed it did. It made `ReadPendingInputDraft` answer NoBox for every alt-buffer screen, and release 0.6.8.12 went dark for every Claude tab (2026-09-26):
  - no draft was detected, so the mail button stayed dead;
  - Copy Current Prompt came back empty;
  - the §10 re-fill logged "box never readable";
  - the autorunner held every advance on "input box not visible" and delivered nothing.
- A child app (the Ctrl+G editor, a pager) is told apart by **content**: the detector's first-glyph caret between anchored rules. Microsoft Edit's `N │ ` gutter keeps even a pasted Claude box from matching. Details and fixtures: PENDING_INPUT.md §12, `TestPendingInput` cases 28–29.
- Diagnose from the logs:
  - `[advance-skip] … (input box not visible)` across the whole fleet, with no `[pending]` lines, means the box reader is blind rather than claude being busy;
  - `pending_probe.exe <claude-pid>` shows what the detector would see;
  - the raw ConPTY stream (the `claude-wrap-ConPTY` driver) shows which renderer a claude runs.
- Under fullscreen, the summary-panel JUMP only reaches prompts on claude's current screen (SUMMARY_JUMP.md §5).

### The hook forwarder must DECODE its stdin as UTF-8 — `[Console]::In` garbles every non-ASCII prompt

- `[Console]::In.ReadToEnd()` decodes with the **console's input code page** (the ConPTY's OEM page — 437, 862, …), but claude writes the hook payload as **UTF-8**: every Hebrew/emoji/em-dash prompt reached the registry as mojibake (`שלום` → `╫⌐╫£╫ò╫¥`) — 481/1014 non-ASCII `Typed` rows, 405 of them double-recorded, no non-ASCII push echo ever matching. The forwarder now reads the raw stdin STREAM through a UTF-8 `StreamReader` (the console fallback only if that throws) and every file it reads with `Get-Content -Encoding UTF8` (HOOKS.md *Encoding*). ⚠ Never "fix" it with `[Console]::InputEncoding = UTF8` — that re-code-pages the console SHARED with claude and its shell.
- **The forwarder file carries a UTF-8 BOM** (`MaterializeSharedHookFiles`): Windows PowerShell 5.1 parses a BOM-less `.ps1` in the ANSI code page, and the script embeds the profile's paths — under a Hebrew (any non-ASCII) Windows user name the bridge.json discovery looked in a non-existent folder and the hook was dropped. Keep the script BODY pure ASCII; the BOM is added at write time.
- Verify without a build: extract the `PSHOOK` script from `ClaudeSpawn.cpp` (two adjacent raw-string pieces — MSVC caps one literal at 16,380 bytes, C2026 — so concatenate all of them), run it under `powershell -File` with a Hebrew payload on stdin, capture the wire line on a throwaway pipe (HOOKS.md *Encoding* has the recipe).

### A shell-form hook pays Git Bash + the user's `BASH_ENV` on EVERY run — and SessionEnd gets 1.5 s in total

- On Windows, Claude Code runs a shell-form hook (`"command": "<string>"`) through **Git Bash**, and a non-interactive bash sources `$BASH_ENV` before anything else. Here that is `~/.claude/bash-env.sh`, which rebuilds a cache every 30 minutes: measured **0.2–0.7 s warm and 6–15 s on a rebuild**, on every forwarder run. Claude waits on SessionStart, UserPromptSubmit, Stop and PreCompact hooks, so this was the "hooks at … launch go till timeout" report (an 11.6 s SessionStart and a 15.3 s Stop on record, each with `bash-env.sh: line 81: …/.env-cached-win-…: No such file or directory` in its stderr).
- **Exec form** (`"command": "<exe>", "args": [ … ]`, Claude Code ≥ 2.1.139) spawns the exe directly: no shell, no `BASH_ENV`, and no quoting. The forwarder uses it whenever the resolved `claude.exe`'s PE version allows (`SelectHookExecHost`). ⚠ Never hand an exec-form file to a claude older than 2.1.139: it ignores `args` and runs the bare `command` through its shell with no arguments, so the forwarder never sees its `-File`.
- **SessionEnd's whole phase gets 1.5 s** unless some SessionEnd hook declares a `timeout`; then it takes the largest, capped at 60 s (`CLAUDE_CODE_SESSIONEND_HOOKS_TIMEOUT_MS` overrides all of it). Nothing declared one, so SessionEnd deliveries were cancelled mid-flight ("hooks at end go till timeout"). Our forwarder's SessionEnd now declares 5 s. That budget is SHARED: it also becomes the ceiling for the user's own SessionEnd hooks.
- Diagnose from Claude's own records, not guesses: each transcript carries `attachment` lines (`hook_success` / `hook_cancelled` / `hook_error`) with `hookName`, `durationMs`, `timedOut`, `timeoutMs`, `stderr` and the `command`. hooks.log's `[hooks] claude <ver> -> EXEC-form | SHELL-form` line says which form the next sessions snapshot.

### The Manager lens is a FULL rebuild — it must know whether it is on screen, and every per-card cost multiplies by the fleet.

- **The 2026-09-04 release slowness** (17 live sessions / 2 windows, "UI reacts slowly, switching tabs takes too long", a 45s `[ui-stall]` that began INSIDE a `[nav] tab-focus` with no notify storm behind it) was measured with `tools/livesample.exe` (`census` → the ONE Emperor UI thread at 30-42% of a core; the new `walk` mode = every-sample stack walk with inclusive our-frame histogram + BUSY EPISODES; `lockwatch` = name the thread holding a terminal lock the UI thread waits on): 33-44% of all busy UI samples were `AgentManagerContent::_Refresh` — a from-scratch `_RebuildBoard` + `_RebuildTree` + `_RebuildPlan` (1-6s per rebuild) fired by EVERY registry notify (~4/s cap, gap measured from the START of the rebuild = back-to-back), by the 30s timing tick (a full rebuild to move an "ago" string), and by EVERY tab switch (`_SyncManagerSelectionToTab` → `SelectSession` → `_Refresh`) — per window, on the one shared UI thread, **even while the Manager tab was hidden**.
- Inside each rebuild: `Snapshot()` deep-copied ALL 821 registry records + 4034 queued prompts THREE times (`_Refresh`, `_RebuildTree`'s scoped copy, `_DormantCounts`); every card built TWO full session `MenuFlyout`s (⋯ + right-click) and every row one more (~50 eager menus of ~13 items + 4 submenus + 2 launch-model lists + ~20 tooltips + a registry `Get` per refresh, almost never opened — `_MakeSessionMenu` ~12-15% of busy samples); `ResolveSessionColorHex` → `GetDirColor` → `LoadDirColors` read+parsed `dir-colors.json` from DISK per card; `AgentmasterStateDir()` did a `create_directories` syscall on EVERY call (every log line and state read included); `IsDevPackage` a PFN syscall per card; `_UpdateReopenButton` enumerated+parsed `windows/*.json` per refresh. **Fixed** (same pass): `SetLensVisible` (driven from `_OnTabSelectionChanged`; hidden ⇒ `_Refresh` marks dirty, ONE catch-up on show), `SessionRegistry::SnapshotLive()` (the lens, the ~2.5s `_ReconcileClaudeTabsImpl` tick, `_DormantCounts`, keep-awake), `_MakeLazySessionMenu`/`_MakeLazyExternalMenu` (an EMPTY flyout filled in its `Opening` handler — the WT tab menu's fill-at-flyout-open idiom; the eager builders became `_PopulateSessionMenu`/`_PopulateExternalTreeMenu`), a (size,mtime)-validated `LoadDirColors` cache, a 2s-rate-limited `AgentmasterStateDir` re-assert, a memoized `PackageFamilyName`, the 30s tick → `_RefreshTimingTexts` (rewrites the bound timing `TextBlock`s in place; a full rebuild only when a ⚡ glyph must flip), a 5s TTL on the reopen count, and the notify gap stamped AFTER the rebuild returns.
- **Don't regress:** never add per-card work that touches disk/registry/syscalls (resolve once per rebuild or cache by file stamp); never attach an eagerly built flyout to a per-refresh-rebuilt element (use the lazy builders); never call `Snapshot()` on a periodic/UI path that only needs live sessions; on a periodic path use the narrowest lean registry read that holds what it reads (`LiveTabBindings`, `GetTabToken`, `GetWithoutQueue`, `ManagedCodexTabTokens`, `SnapshotLiveForScan`, `SnapshotLiveForCards`, `HeadlinesOf` for a few named sessions, archived ones included — a whole-record copy carries up to 200 queued prompts and every transcript text; UI_THREAD.md §10g / §10j / §11); the side panel's card list is edited IN PLACE (SIDE_PANEL.md §2c — a per-card model diff writes only changed values, `PlanKeyedListOps` reorders with the fewest removes / inserts / moves), so never put a `Children().Clear()` + rebuild back on a per-update path; and any new lens trigger must go through `_Refresh` (the visibility gate lives there).
- **The second measured shape** — the UI thread BLOCKED 5-8s in `til::recursive_ticket_lock::lock` (a FIFO ticket lock, no reader/writer split) from plain `TermControl::_PointerMovedHandler → ControlCore::IsVtMouseModeEnabled` / `_updateSelectionMarkers → HasSelection`: some other thread (the ConPTY output thread's `Terminal::Write` or the render thread's `_PaintFrame` — the only non-UI holders) held ONE terminal's lock for seconds. Not yet attributed (the renderer's synchronized-output wait RELEASES the lock — checked). The tab-switch funnel now carries a **stopwatch** (`[tab-switch-slow]` with a 4-lap breakdown when a switch takes ≥250ms, `[tab-switch-settle]` when the UI thread isn't idle again within 750ms) so the next slow switch names its step; run `livesample lockwatch <pid> <uiTid> 24000 40` while the user works to catch the lock holder.

### A windowed tail reader must never gate its read on the file SIZE alone — a finished turn never grows its file again.

- **The 2026-09-25 stuck-Running report** (release 0.6.8.11, session `d1f3f198`): the session showed `Running` for 7+ hours while claude's own heartbeat (`~/.claude/sessions/<pid>.json`) read `idle`. It was a restored dormant tab; the autorunner delivered its queued prompt 1 s after claude started; claude aborted that turn with **no assistant line and no `Stop` hook** and put the prompt back in its box. The presence-idle release exists for exactly that shape, but it never fired.
- **Why:** `SessionScanner` reads at most 1 MiB of transcript per pass, and its read gate was `size != lastSize`, stamped after EVERY read (capped ones included). The 3.8 MB transcript therefore got one chunk per append, and the cursor froze mid-history on a two-day-old `stop_reason: tool_use` the moment the file went quiet. `recon-stop` needs a terminal tail, and `recon-stop-idle` refuses a Running pending `tool_use` (the API-retry flap fix), so nothing could ever release it. Replaying `SessionScanner`'s exact chunking over the on-disk transcript reproduced it: the cursor stops at 2,041,106 / 3,088,636 on `tool_use`, and only a third size change would have caught up.
- **Fixed** (HOOKS.md *State machine*, "The tail is judged only once the cursor has CAUGHT UP"): the read gate is `ShouldReadTranscriptDelta` (size changed **or** the last read did not reach the stat'd end); a fresh cursor on a >1 MiB transcript starts at its **tail window** (`FirstSightReadOffset` + `FirstCompleteLineStart`, falling back to replay-from-0 when the window lies inside one giant line) instead of replaying 1.3 GB of fleet history; and the tail-judging synths hold while `ScanState::readReachedEnd` is false. The earlier 2026-08-14 wedge fix (`DecideDeltaNoNewline`) promised "the next pass retries from the same cursor"; the size gate had silently broken that promise too.
- **Don't regress:** any cursor that consumes a bounded window per pass needs a "caught up?" bit beside its size/mtime change-gate, and anything that JUDGES the tail must check that bit first. When a session reads the wrong state, replay the reader's exact chunking over the real transcript (a 30-line Python fold) before theorizing about the inputs.

### A message typed WHILE claude works is NOT a `user` line — it is a `queued_command` attachment

- **What claude writes:** Enter queues the message (`queue-operation` enqueue; its `UserPromptSubmit` fires then, so the registry records a Typed row). When the running turn absorbs it at its next tool-result boundary (`queue-operation` remove, `reason:"absorbed_mid_turn"`), claude appends `{"type":"attachment","attachment":{"type":"queued_command","commandMode":"prompt","prompt":…,"origin":{"kind":"human"}}}` on the live chain, stamped with the Enter time and written after the tool result. A message the turn does NOT absorb is dequeued and submitted as an ordinary `user` line.
- **The 2026-10-03 report** (session `f62a102b`, Claude Code 2.1.282): the summary listed the first message but not the second one, typed while the first turn ran. `AnalyzeSessionTranscript` read only `user` lines. It now lists a human `queued_command` like a typed prompt, and so does `CollectConversationSegments` (one rule: `SeExtractQueuedHumanPrompt`, `ProcessInspect.Content.cpp`).
- **The same type carries machine traffic.** Only `commandMode:"prompt"` with no `origin` (builds up to 2.1.179) or `origin.kind:"human"` is the user's words; `commandMode:"task-notification"` and `origin.kind` `peer` / `task-notification` are not (SESSIONS.md §3 has the corpus survey). `TranscriptTurns` (the Copy ▸ Transcript port) has its own handling.
- **Still blind to it:** `TranscriptStore::ClassifyTranscriptLine` files every attachment as context, so the Sessions page's 👤 search, its msgs count, `ReadTranscriptInfo` (the External rows' read-only plan) and the MOST RECENT sort's tail read miss these messages. Any new reader that lists the user's messages must handle both shapes.

### The claude-agent-sdk-cpp submodule: its Windows process file spins a core, and `--autocompact` alone never compacts

- **The SDK's own `process_win32.cpp` is not compiled.** Its `ReadPipe::has_data(timeout)` ignores the timeout, so the transport's reader loop uses a whole CPU core for as long as the child lives; it is also ANSI-only and cannot remove an inherited environment variable. `ClaudeSdkProcessWin32.cpp` implements the same SDK-internal interface instead (SDK_HARNESS.md §3). Never add the SDK's file to `ClaudeSdkUnity.cpp`, and after a move of the submodule pin follow SDK_HARNESS.md §9.
- **`--autocompact N` alone does not turn auto-compaction on.** A harness child runs with `--setting-sources ""`, and Claude Code then falls back to the legacy `~/.claude.json`, where a user may have `autoCompactEnabled: false` (this machine has). The `--settings` text must carry `"autoCompactEnabled": true` beside `"autoCompactWindow"` (`BuildHarnessSettingsJson`; measured on 2.1.291).
- **`nlohmann::json member{ other }` builds a one-element ARRAY** (the braces pick the initializer-list constructor). A tool schema stored that way made Claude Code reject the tool list, and the model answered that it had no tools. Use parentheses for JSON members.
- **A tool handler runs on the SDK's reader thread.** While it runs, that thread cannot deliver the interrupt of its own request. A handler must return fast, and a slow one must ask whether its request is still wanted (`SessionSearchToolset::Callbacks::generation`).
- **A permission mode that Claude Code does not allow is LOWERED SILENTLY, not refused** (2.1.291, measured 2026-10-12). `--dangerously-skip-permissions` under a policy that disables bypass starts in `auto`, with no line on stdout or stderr; with auto disabled too, in `default`. Never assume the mode that was asked for: read `current_permission_mode` from the answer to `initialize` (or `permissionMode` from `system/init`). `--restricted` is the exception: it ends the process at start with an error. A harness process is windowless, so it must end in a mode that never asks (`bypassPermissions`, `auto`, `dontAsk`): `SettlePermissionMode`.
- **With bypass permissions the tool list is the ONLY limit of a harness process.** Before a conversation gets a built-in tool (`Bash`, `Write`, …) or a user MCP server, decide that it may use it without any check, in a process nobody sees. The harness passes `--tools ""` and removes the `resources` capability from its in-process MCP servers for this reason (the SDK announces it, and Claude Code then gives the model three MCP-resource tools).
- **Never run `harness_live.exe` (or any engine test exe) against a real profile.** A shell hosted by a running Agentmaster carries `AGENTMASTER_PROFILE` = the live profile. The live test forces a scratch profile itself; keep that guard.
- **Inside a `TerminalPage` member, a bare `FontFamily{…}` is the page's own `FontFamily` property**, not the type (C3321 "an initializer list is unexpected"). Write `winrt::Windows::UI::Xaml::Media::FontFamily{…}` there, like `CornerRadius`.

### The session database: DuckDB's memory limit binds per TRANSACTION, not per statement (2026-10-15)

- **The service runs at a 1 GB `memory_limit`** (the cog's default; DuckDB's own default is 80% of RAM — never leave it unset). Under it, three things that passed on a scratch database failed at full scale with "Out of Memory Error: failed to pin block":
  - a merge that read the WHOLE hot tier through one materialized `SELECT … FROM doc_hot ORDER BY doc_id` — one large batch tipped it over, and every failure left a bigger hot tier for the next try (the first full cold build ended with 401 K blocks unmerged). **Merge in slices** (`TextIndex::Merge`, ~64 MB of text per transaction);
  - `INSERT INTO line_new SELECT * FROM line ORDER BY line_id` over 1.6 GB of raw JSON: the sort alone passed, the unsorted copy passed, the sort plus the ZSTD writer did not. **Sort in slices** of a quarter of the limit (`PlanLineSlices`);
  - the text-index re-cluster's statements, each of which passed alone (127.6 M postings sorted in 27 s), inside ONE transaction. **Commit the new tables one by one; make only the swap one transaction.**
- **A failed maintenance step must back off**: the re-cluster stayed due after its failure and re-ran every ~20 s, a full read of the database each time (`kReclusterBackoff*`).
- **`pragma_storage_info(t)` takes a literal only** (no LATERAL over `duckdb_tables()`), and a string segment's `additional_block_ids` hold most of its blocks: `pragma_database_size()`'s `used_blocks` is the reliable size. A cold build leaves the file at about twice its used blocks (DuckDB reuses free blocks but never shrinks the file).
- **Never point the one-shot modes (`sql`, `recluster`, `resync`, `verify-summary`) at a profile whose service runs**: they open the file themselves, and DuckDB allows one writing process per file.

## Correctness rules (do not regress)

1. **"Waiting-for-you" is three states.** A session is *ready* for an auto-send when it is
   **turn-complete** (`Stop` → `WaitingForInput`) **or** sitting **Idle** with no turn in
   progress (a freshly launched / just-resumed plan must START, not wait for a `Stop` it will
   never emit); `Running` / `NeedsApproval` / `Error` / `Done` are never ready. A change-driven
   advance is held to **one prompt per turn** by the pickup guard (don't regress that — it
   prevents a queue-drain). And "ready" must be **settled**: an automatic send waits until the
   session has HELD a sendable status for `kStateSettleMs` (40 s) since the later of its last
   status change and the prompt's queue time, restarting on every further change (the STATE
   SETTLE hold, DELIVERY.md §16) — never type into a momentary "ready". `Notification(permission)` →
   `NeedsApproval`, which the prompt queue never answers: every queued prompt waits until the user
   answers in the terminal (nothing auto-approves — `ApprovalPolicy` is a persisted placeholder no
   code evaluates).
   A `Stop` whose last message is a question keeps the next prompt **Pending** (the question-guard —
   never parked in `Held`, a legacy status only rehabilitated) until a non-question turn-complete or
   an explicit re-arm.
2. **Tree `Enter` = Activate** (jump to the session's live tab); never forward it to
   `ITerminalConnection::WriteInput` (that would submit a stray carriage return).
3. **Bind queue → sessionId**, never "the selected session" at send time.
4. **Idempotent sends:** mark `Sent` atomically + persist; survive restart without replay.
5. **Backstops:** stop-on-error, maxAutoSends, global pause/kill, pause-on-human-input (⚠ that last one
   has no feeder yet — `NoteHumanInput` has no caller, so it never fires; don't count on it).
6. **Startup ARCHIVES, never auto-launches; restore = resume, not replay (transcript-gated).**
   On startup, persisted sessions load into the registry as **closed** (`live=false`) and are
   **NOT** re-launched — the prior fleet is resumable
   from the **Sessions browser** (a deliberate reversal of the old auto-reopen). A user-initiated
   **Resume** re-launches one: `claude --resume <id>` (same id ⇒ hooks still correlate) **only when
   Claude has a transcript for that id**, else a fresh session (new id, same dir + queue, stale
   record dropped). Queues reload with statuses intact. **Close is the only close verb — it always
   archives** (keeps the `live=false` record so the session stays resumable; it NEVER deletes, and
   there is no Delete — FAVORITES.md). The Claude transcript on disk is never touched — with ONE
   exception, an append: before a resume of a conversation whose turn died on an API error, the
   rewind line (HOOKS.md *API-error turns*; never a deletion, never while a claude runs it); mark a session
   you want to keep with the **★ favorite** (SessionStore). Never decide **resume-vs-fresh** from the
   persisted `SessionState` (that is transcript-gated, above) — but the persisted state DOES seed a
   reopened session's DISPLAY state: its at-rest "needs you" triage (WaitingForInput / NeedsApproval)
   is PRESERVED across a crash while in-flight/ended states normalize to Idle (`RestoredSessionState`,
   Rule #16). See Gotchas. **A turn that was in flight is not forgotten by that normalization:** every
   seam that applies `RestoredSessionState` to a Claude record first records the fact
   (`RestoredMidTurnFlag` → `SessionInfo::midTurnAtRestore`), and the scanner reads a turn that never
   finished, and that no process can be running, as **Error** — never as a ready Idle (HOOKS.md
   *Cut-off turns*; the 2026-10-09 incident sent a queued prompt past a turn that a crash had cut off).
7. **State is hook-derived,** never screen-scraped (the Ink TUI repaints constantly).
8. **Same directory = same path, filesystem-aware.** Group/scope/match sessions by working
   dir through `PathEq` (case-insensitive on Windows, case-sensitive on POSIX), so
   case-variant spellings of one directory never fork the Explorer Tree into two roots.
9. **Adopt only on `SessionStart`; bind by `tabToken`, never guess.** A hook for a session we
   didn't Launch creates an `external` record ONLY on `SessionStart` (any other event for an
   unknown id is ignored — there's no connection to bind). Promotion to control correlates the
   `WT_SESSION` `tabToken` to a live ConPTY and binds the injector to THAT id (Rule #3); if no
   connection matches (a claude hosted outside this app), it stays observe-only — never bind to
   "the active tab".
10. **One engine per process; a closing window must detach; records reference, never copy.**
    Exactly ONE `SessionRegistry` / `HooksBridge` / `Scheduler` for the whole process
    (`SharedEngine()`), shared by every window — never re-create them per `TerminalPage`, and
    never key the bridge on anything that collides across windows (the `<pid>` pipe is fine
    *because* there is one bridge). Each window registers its lens observer + adoption handler +
    activate sink by **token** and detaches them on teardown (`RemoveObserver` in
    `~AgentManagerContent`; `RemoveAdoptionHandler` + `UnregisterWindowActivateHandler` in
    `~TerminalPage`); the fleet loads **process-once**
    under the `Engine::restoreMutex` barrier (a second window blocks until it's loaded, then skips) so it can't
    double-insert NOR race its tab re-home against a half-loaded registry. Per-window persisted UI
    state (geometry + lens + ordered tab refs) is the `WindowRecord` (`windows/<id>.json`) and it
    must **reference** sessions by id, never copy them — `sessions.json` + `SessionInfo.live`
    remain the one session source of truth (Option 1).
11. **A session's title is ONE value — Explorer name == tab title == persisted `SessionInfo.title`.**
    `s.title` is the single source of truth; it is **pinned** onto the WT tab at launch / restore /
    adopt (`Tab::SetTabText`, so the tab stops floating with claude's volatile OSC title). Renaming
    from **either** side writes that one value and persists (the autosave-on-change observer): the
    Explorer-tree **Rename…** routes through the `rename` callback → `_RenameClaudeSession` (registry
    + tab); a WT tab rename (double-click / right-click **Rename Tab** / `renameTab` action — all
    funnel through `Tab::SetTabText` → `PropertyChanged("Title")` → `_UpdateTitle`) flows back via
    `_SyncClaudeTitleFromTab`. The sync is **cross-window**: a rename run in a window that does NOT
    host the tab (the board/tree show the whole fleet) writes the registry, and the hosting
    window's registry observer re-pins its tab (`_SyncClaudeTabTitleFromRegistry`, riding the
    tab-dot push). Equality guards make an already-in-step sync a no-op (no loops); an
    emptied override (`ResetTabText`) re-pins. A WT-tab rename is **normalized like the Explorer
    editor** (`_CommitRename`): `_SyncClaudeTitleFromTab` trims surrounding whitespace/newlines and
    **rejects** a blank/whitespace-only rename (re-pins the managed name — a title never goes empty),
    and when the user typed surrounding whitespace it re-pins the tab to the trimmed value so the strip
    matches the stored name. It reads the `_runtimeTabText` override (`Tab::GetTabText()`), **never**
    the volatile active/OSC title (`_GetActiveTitle()`), so claude's OSC title can't leak into the
    registry. On **adopt**, a name the user already gave the `+` tab wins (mirrored into the registry);
    else the tab is pinned to the managed name. On an in-session **`/resume`** re-home (the tab's claude
    switches conversation id on a stable ConPTY — `_BindClaudeSessionToTab`'s `reHomedFromOtherId`
    latch), the new conversation keeps **ITS own** title (its registry title if any, else
    `DeriveSessionTitle`) and the tab is re-pinned to it — it **never** inherits the superseded
    (now-archived) conversation's still-pinned tab text, which would mask the new id under the old one's
    name. A **fork's** name comes from `DeriveForkTitle`: a first fork appends ` (fork)`, forking a fork
    **increments** (` (fork 2)`, ` (fork 3)`, … — multi-digit, nested/earlier parens preserved) instead
    of stacking ` (fork) (fork)`. Don't reintroduce a separate tab title or scrape claude's OSC title
    for the name.
12. **A tab's color is ONE value per COLOR KEY — PERMANENT.** The key is per-`tabColorMode` (the
    cog's **Tab coloring** dropdown): the **working dir** (default — the classic rule below, verbatim),
    the **session itself** (`Individual` — the color persists on `SessionInfo::tabColorHex`, never in
    the dir map, so Individual can't exhaust the folder palette; no fan-out), or the **inferred
    working dir** (`InferredWorkingDirectory` — the SAME dir machinery keyed by
    `SessionInfo::inferredWorkingDir` when known, else the cwd; `SessionColorKeyDir` is the one key
    resolver, `ResolveSessionColorHex` the one read-side resolution every display surface shares).
    **Across the dir-keyed modes a git WORKTREE keys its MAIN repo's color** (`SessionColorKeyDir` →
    `ResolveWorktreeMainRoot`, memoized): a repo and all its worktrees resolve to ONE color key, so
    they wear one color — the SINGLE place the color key diverges from `EffectiveWorkingDir` (which
    stays worktree-granular for grouping + mechanics: a new session still spawns in the worktree, the
    Explorer tree still lists it there); a main-checkout / submodule / non-git dir is unchanged.
    The fourth mode, **Remove colors** (`NoColor`), SUSPENDS painting rather than re-keying it,
    STRIP-WIDE: a managed tab is reset (re-derivable from the maps), every NON-managed tab's
    runtime color — ex-claude shell tabs, user-colored/restored pwsh tabs — is
    PARKED on the tab (`Tab::SetTabColorSuspended`; persistence still records the parked value via
    `GetPersistableTabColor` / `BuildStartupActions`' fold), "Change tab color" +
    `openTabColorPicker` are disabled on EVERY tab (`Tab::SetColorPickerEnabled`), any color
    landing mid-mode is parked by the `_OnClaudeTabColorChanged` chokepoint, and the persisted maps
    are neither read nor WRITTEN (`ResolveSessionColorHex` ⇒ empty) — permanence upheld by
    abstinence: switching back to any colored mode restores exactly the prior colors.
    Every paint routes through `_ApplySessionTabColor` (the mode dispatch); everything below is the
    DEFAULT (dir-keyed) mode's contract, which the inferred mode inherits over its key. Every Claude
    tab in a dir
    shares one color (filesystem-aware key `NormDirKey` — slash/case/trailing-normalized), and a dir
    **keeps the same color across tabs, windows, and restarts** (the user gets used to a folder's
    color). `dir-colors.json` (schema **v2**) is the single source of truth — the PERMANENT
    folder→color map, holding BOTH user picks and auto-assigned colors (no in-memory cycle state).
    On launch/restore/adopt `_ApplyDirColorToTab` paints the dir's persisted color if it has one,
    else `AssignDirAutoColor` deals a fresh one and **persists it**. The deal (pure core
    `ChooseDirColor`): (0) an already-assigned dir keeps its color; (1) else the first color in the
    dir's seeded probe order (Fisher-Yates / splitmix64 over `(seed, key)`) that **no other folder
    already holds** — the "color collection" is just the palette colors present in the map, so two
    dirs never share a color while colors remain; (2) when **every palette color is already
    assigned** ("the color list is over") the collection **resets** — reuse is allowed, but the deal
    still **avoids any color an OPEN tab is actively showing** (across all windows, via `openDirKeys`
    = the process-wide registry's live set); (3) only when every palette color is active (more open
    dirs than colors) is reuse unavoidable → the dir's preferred slot. The **seed** (Engine init →
    `SeedDirColors`, random) only randomizes a dir's **first-ever** assignment, which then persists —
    it never moves an assigned color. A user color change
    (`Tab::SetRuntimeTabColor`/`Reset` → `TabColorChanged` → `_OnClaudeTabColorChanged`) **persists
    it for the dir AND recolors every live tab in that dir** (Rule #8); a reset drops the entry. A
    single de-dupe (`GetDirColor(dir) == newHex`) makes our OWN writes no-ops — our launch-time auto
    paint AND a user-pick fan-out already match the persisted color, so only a genuine NEW user pick
    re-persists + re-fans (no loop). A one-time `MigrateDirColorsToV2IfNeeded` (Engine init)
    **de-collides** a v1 file (`DeCollideDirColors`): every folder keeps its color where possible,
    duplicate palette colors are reassigned to free ones, and off-palette user picks are kept
    verbatim — fixing the existing collisions without discarding the mapping. (The default *name*,
    `DeriveSessionTitle`, only seeds an *untitled* session — a real/renamed title wins, Rule #11.)
13. **Fleet Observer: rostered == ours; correlate by `WT_SESSION`, never guess; provenance: push
    wins for state.** A claude correlated to a tab in OUR published roster (its shell == the ConPTY
    root of one of our tabs) is running in one of our windows, so it is **OURS** (`RunningApp::
    Agentmaster`) and bindable **regardless of `AM_SESSION`** — a hand-typed `+`-tab claude does NOT
    reliably inherit `AM_SESSION` (WT regenerates a tab's env; see Gotchas), so the **roster
    correlation is the authoritative bind signal**, and `AM_SESSION` is only the secondary signal
    for the census of NON-rostered claudes (a background daemon we spawned, or telling our instance
    from another's / a real WindowsTerminal). Correlation keys on the **exact `WT_SESSION`** (==
    `ITerminalConnection::SessionId()`) — never PPID ancestry, never the stale tab cwd, never "the
    active tab". `ObserveClaude` **enriches facts but NEVER sets `SessionState`** — push hooks + the
    transcript tail own state (Rule #1/#7); a hooked and an un-hooked claude converge on the same
    record. The observer **only reads** (PEB / Toolhelp / filesystem) — it must NEVER write to a
    shell's stdin (the invisibility invariant). **Adopting** an external (the Explorer-Tree
    EXTERNAL scope's *Adopt*) upholds this — it never injects into / kills the foreign process; it
    resumes the external's *conversation* into a NEW managed tab (`claude --resume <id>`, id from
    `ResolveSessionId`, transcript-gated → fresh if none) and leaves the original running. The
    transient enrichment fields are **never persisted** (`Persistence.cpp`); the observer
    re-derives them each run.
14. **Conversation identity is by transcript CREATION time ≈ process start, not newest-mtime.** A
    claude's OWN transcript is created when it first writes — at/after its process start.
    `ResolveSessionId` (for a bare claude with no explicit `--session-id`/`--resume <guid>`) picks
    the cwd's transcript whose **creation time is closest to (and not significantly before) the
    claude's start**, and REJECTS transcripts created before it started. This is what keeps **two
    claudes sharing one cwd** bound to their OWN conversations (newest-mtime collapses them onto
    whichever is momentarily most active), and resolves a **never-prompted** claude to `""` (no card
    until its first prompt — §11d) rather than collapsing it onto a stale leftover transcript. An
    explicit `--session-id` / `--resume <guid>` is authoritative and wins (collision-free, known
    before the transcript exists).
15. **One profile per install, resolved ONCE, before ANY state read; everything persists inside
    it.** The WindowEmperor resolves the profile **after** winning the single-instance handoff and
    **before** the first settings/state read — first launch **auto-selects the per-identity default**
    (release → Default, dev → Development) WITHOUT UI, so a handed-off process or a `-Embedding`
    (defterm) activation takes the same silent path; the only profile picker left is the cog's
    explicit **Change…** (never shown from a handed-off / `-Embedding` process). Never read or
    write persisted state (engine files, Terminal settings, window records, the reopen scan)
    through any path that isn't `AgentmasterStateDir()` / the `AGENTMASTER_PROFILE`-redirected
    `GetBaseSettingsPath()`. The resolution is cached for the process lifetime — a profile change
    (the cog's Change…) applies on restart only; never re-home state mid-run. The ONLY data outside
    a profile: the `.agentmaster.profiles` choice file + the env override (the pointer must live
    outside what it points at) and the fixed `~/.agentmaster/locks/` build mutex (it guards the ONE
    build tree, not a profile). Library-side resolution (`ProfileBootstrap::ResolveProfileDir`)
    must never show UI — headless hosts (tests, tools) silently land on the per-identity default,
    which for unpackaged runs is the historical `~/.agentmaster` (don't regress that — it's what
    keeps the test harness and old tooling stable). Generated artifacts that embed paths or live
    endpoints (the shim, hooks-settings, the forwarder's `bridge.json` discovery) must be derived
    from the ACTIVE profile at engine init — a fixed `~/.agentmaster` literal in generated content
    is a cross-instance routing bug (the forwarder had exactly that).
16. **Durability: never lose a window, tab, or session unless the user CLOSED IT DELIBERATELY — and
    even then, demote to recoverable, never erase** (PERSISTENCE.md §13.5 / FAVORITES.md). The
    workspace survives **every** exit — graceful close, app quit, AND a hard crash. "Closed slowly"
    (deliberately, one window at a time) changes only whether a window is **auto-reopened** next
    launch vs. **kept recoverable on demand** — closing NEVER deletes anything. Three durable layers
    carry it, all under the ACTIVE profile (Rule #15): **`windows/<id>.json`** (one `WindowRecord`
    per window — geometry + Manager lens + ORDERED tab refs + focused-tab identity; Option 1 *refs*,
    never session copies), **`open-windows.json`** (the open-at-exit manifest = the live window-id
    set), and **`sessions.json`** (the fleet — only ever GROWS, never pruned). The **"closed slowly"
    distinction IS the manifest's SKIP-EMPTY rule** (`Engine::UnregisterLiveWindow`): each window
    unregister rewrites the manifest EXCEPT a write that would EMPTY it is skipped — so closing
    windows one-by-one prunes each (a deliberately-closed window won't auto-reopen) while the LAST
    close leaves the final snapshot, and a hard shutdown that kills the threads before they
    unregister leaves the FULL set (everything auto-reopens). A deliberately-closed window's
    `WindowRecord` STAYS on disk → it is still offered by the Manager's **"Reopen Windows (N)"**
    button (`RecoverableWindows` = records − live − content-less). On startup the Emperor reopens the
    manifest∩records set (`WindowEmperor.cpp`), gated by the **decide-prompt** when >1; **No** still
    loses nothing (one window claims the front record, the rest stay recoverable). **Session half**
    (FAVORITES.md): **Close ALWAYS ARCHIVES** (`live=false`, keep the record + Prompt Queue +
    autorunner; the transcript `.jsonl` is NEVER touched) — there is no Delete; a closed session
    leaves the Board and is resumable from the **Sessions browser** (the sole history view), the
    recover button bringing back the WHOLE window. **Enforcement that must not regress:** the
    **deterministic close/quit flush** captures the record BEFORE teardown clears `_claudeTabs`
    (`CloseWindow` / `RequestQuit`, latched by `_windowRecordTeardownFlushed`) with an idempotent
    `~TerminalPage` catch-all for the non-initiating windows on a quit-all, and that flush is the
    window's LAST write: once latched, `_FlushWindowRecord` refuses (`[window-save] <id> late save
    refused`) and `_ScheduleWindowRecordSave` arms nothing, because an autosave still pending at the close
    fired after the archive and saved the Claude tabs as shells (flows TAB-K1, fixed 2026-10-23, NOT BUILT
    yet); the **load barrier**
    (`Engine::restoreMutex`) blocks a 2nd reopened window until the fleet is loaded so it can't flush
    an EMPTY record over its workspace; **anti-clobber** refuses to overwrite a good record with a
    pre-`Initialized` / no-tabs-AND-no-geometry capture; and the **reclaimable pool** re-claims a
    this-session-closed record BY ID (real id + lens), so a recover/reopen never mints a lens-less
    duplicate. **The ONLY two erasures**, both content-free by construction: a window emptied to just
    its Agent Manager page (no tab left) self-closes + deletes its (content-less) record (`_CloseWindowIfManager-
    Only`, race-safe via `ReserveManagerOnlyClose` — the last window STAYS open, record discarded),
    and a session whose transcript VANISHED from disk simply stops listing ("not found → don't show";
    `cleanupPeriodDays` is seeded to ~never so Claude never sweeps). Never add a destructive close
    path, a manifest write that can legitimately empty the file, a capture that runs AFTER
    `_claudeTabs` is cleared, or a `sessions.json` prune that drops a record with a live transcript.
17. **A bookmark TAG is removed only EXPLICITLY, and its color/name resolution is single-source.** Untagging
    a tag's LAST carrier must NEVER delete the tag — it stays in the universe at ·0 (re-appliable) via the
    durable known-tag registry (`tags.json`), and only the ✕ on a 0-carrier row deletes it
    (`UnregisterKnownTag`). A tag any session still carries can NOT be deleted (the derived union keeps it
    alive), so the ✕ is shown only on 0-carrier rows. A deleted tag KEEPS its `tag-colors.json` entry (a
    re-created name regains its color). Creation is capped by `AppSettings::maxTags` (applying/removing an
    existing tag is never capped). Every surface resolves a tag's color through the ONE
    `ResolveTagDisplayColor` (user-picked `tag-colors.json` > `TagColorFor` name-hash), and the tab badges +
    tooltip read it from the resolved `AgentTagsSpec` (`name\t#AARRGGBB`) the producer wrote — never
    re-resolve per consumer, or the same tag can wear two colors.
18. **Never lose a swallowed exception.** Wherever we `catch (...)` — at ALL times, in ALL cases — the
    exception and its origin must survive into `hooks.log`, so any later report is diagnosable in full
    without a repro. A catch may still recover silently *for the user*; it may not be silent *for us*.
    Report through the ONE chokepoint: **`Agentmaster::LogSwallowedException(L"<context>")`** in the
    plain-C++ engine TUs (no WinRT — they must keep compiling in the standalone harness and the CLI) and
    **`AgentLogCaughtException(context)`** (`AgentCatchLog.h`) in the TerminalApp layer, which adds
    `hresult_error`/`wil` detail; both attach the VEH-captured **throw-site** stack as
    `Module.dll+0xRVA` (the catch site itself runs after the unwind, so the raise-time ring is the only
    place that stack still exists). **Preserve every recovery side-effect exactly** — a re-armed latch, a
    fallback return, a `continue`-skip — logging is purely additive; and never gate behavior on it (both
    reporters are `noexcept` and throttled, so they cannot turn a contained failure into a new one). Two
    exemptions, both narrow: a catch that is itself on the **logging path** MUST stay bare (it recurses /
    self-deadlocks — see Gotchas; mark it `⚠ DELIBERATELY BARE` with its degradation), and a catch whose
    throw is genuinely expected control flow in a hot loop may stay bare *with a comment saying so*.
    "It's just a best-effort helper" is NOT an exemption — those are exactly the ones that vanished
    (`OutputDebugStringW` doesn't count: it needs a debugger attached at the time, so in the field it is
    silence). Don't add a bare `catch (...)` to this codebase.

## Conventions

- Mark our additions with `Agentmaster`. The repo `LICENSE` is **AGPL-3.0-or-later** (the combined-work license; paid escape hatch in `LICENSE-COMMERCIAL.md`); the upstream Windows Terminal **MIT** license is retained in `LICENSE-MIT` and the third-party attributions in `NOTICE.md` (the build's `NOTICE.html` source) + `THIRD-PARTY-NOTICES.txt` — **never strip them** (MIT requires retaining the notice). External contributions require signing `CLA.md` before merge (the dual-license depends on it).
- Keep the diff against upstream minimal where practical (additive files, small touches at integration points) so rebasing onto `microsoft/terminal` stays cheap.
- Build artifacts (`bin/`, `packages/`, `Generated Files/`) are gitignored — never commit them.

### The Agent Manager UI is ALWAYS dark, independent of the Windows / Windows Terminal theme.

- Every Agentmaster surface root forces `RequestedTheme(ElementTheme::Dark)` — the Agent Manager page `_root` (`AgentManagerContent`), the full-window Sessions page host, and the settings / claude-missing / path-picker overlays — and paints an **explicit dark fill** (e.g. `_root` uses `#2e2e2e`, NOT an app-theme-resolved brush: `Application.Resources().Lookup("UnfocusedBorderBrush")` resolves against the *app* theme and returns light `#e8e8e8` in light mode, which used to bleed through the Manager pane's widget gaps).
- Confirm dialogs that **self-host** (`AgentManagerContent`, via `ShowAsync`) follow `_root.ActualTheme()` (Dark); dialogs shown via WT's **shared presenter** (`TerminalWindow::ShowDialog`, which force-themes *every* dialog to the WT setting up its whole ancestor chain) opt into dark by tagging themselves **`agentmaster-dark`** (the Archive/Sessions page + batch-close confirms do).
- **Popups** that render in the popup root inherit Dark from their now-dark anchor: `MenuFlyout` context menus (`_MakeSessionMenu` / `_MakeExternalTreeMenu` / `_MakePromptMenu` / the Copy submenu) and `ComboBox` dropdowns follow their **target element's** theme — the SAME mechanism WT uses for all its own flyouts (nothing in the tree sets `MenuFlyoutPresenterStyle`, and WT's per-app theming proves it), so menus/combos under `_root` need no per-flyout theming — and the path-picker `Popup` is explicitly Dark.
- The one exception is **`ToolTip`s**: `ToolTipService` theme inheritance is unreliable under XAML Islands (the reason `AgentTipHelpers` exists), so `AgentSetTip` pins every tip `RequestedTheme(Dark)`.
- Don't reintroduce an app-theme-dependent brush/lookup on these surfaces, tag any new presenter-shown Agentmaster dialog `agentmaster-dark`, and keep new tooltips going through `AgentSetTip`.
